### INC-25: §3.3a BMO cutoff one-trading-day-late (real rule defect, financial-critical — fixed at FP-008.4 Commit 1.5 failure #2)

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-25 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-05-30 (FP-008.4 Commit 1.5 failure #2 three-way reconciliation) |
| **Classification** | real rule defect (not test bug); financial-critical severity; resolved in-pass at this commit |
| **Surface Story** | Test `§3.3a worked example (b): BMO Monday cutoff is prior Wednesday` (`rule-3-3a-earnings-window_test.ts:27`) was failing under end-to-end Deno suite execution. Three-way reconciliation between (1) CROSSWIND_SPEC.md §3.3 worked example (b) verbatim (lines 299-303), (2) test assertion, and (3) implementation `cutoffDate` (`rule-3-3a-earnings-window.ts:57-60`) showed spec and test agreed (cutoff = Wed Apr 29 for BMO Mon May 4) while the implementation produced Thu Apr 30 — one trading day late. Implementation applied `tradingDaysBefore(scheduled, 2)` uniformly to both AMC and BMO; its own docstring (lines 50-54) explicitly justified this as *"same arithmetic — the 'session' containing the news is the same scheduled_date session by spec convention"*, but spec convention is **not** the same: BMO's session is NOT at risk (the print has already arrived by the open). |
| **Observation** | Per spec, the 2-trading-day flat-window is the 2 trading days **immediately preceding the at-risk session**. For AMC the at-risk session IS `scheduled_date` (the print arrives after close, so the AMC session is the last at-risk day). For BMO the at-risk session is the trading day BEFORE `scheduled_date` (the print arrives before open, so by `scheduled_date`'s open the news is already public and the session is no longer "at risk" by this rule's pre-event-window framing). The fix shifts the effective session back by 1 trading day for BMO before applying the standard `tradingDaysBefore(_, 2)` walk. Behavior unchanged for AMC and intraday. **Production-significance**: under the prior implementation, every BMO-flagged earnings event would have held positions one extra trading day deeper into the danger window than spec mandates — direct ROI/risk impact across the entire long-short universe when §3.3a goes live. |
| **Disposition** | Fixed at this commit (`rule-3-3a-earnings-window.ts:cutoffDate`); +30-line docstring with spec citation lines 299-303 verbatim documenting AMC vs BMO arithmetic asymmetry. Test (b) now passes; tests (a) and (c) and intraday/missing-coverage/past-earnings still pass (AMC code path unchanged). `apply-hard-exclusions_test.ts` (6 orchestrator tests) regression-checked: all pass. Rule firing-history reconciliation NOT required because §3.3a refresh job ships `enabled=false` per `job_registry` seed convention (MIG-049 / universe.md line 82) — no production firings to reconcile. |
| **Cross-references** | CROSSWIND_SPEC.md:286 (LOCKED §3.3a row); CROSSWIND_SPEC.md:299-303 (worked examples a/b/c verbatim); fix at `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/hard-exclusions/rule-3-3a-earnings-window.ts:42-95`; test at `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/hard-exclusions/rule-3-3a-earnings-window_test.ts:27-33`; FP-008 sub-step 8.3 / ACT-107 (original §3.3a rule landing — defect entered there); universe.md:91 + :104 (rule registry + threshold table). |
| **Status** | Resolved at this commit. Tracking entry preserved for ROI-discipline audit trail (real rule defects in financial-critical paths get explicit INC entries per CROSSWIND §2 axiom + ROI guardrail in project knowledge). |

### INC-26: Asymmetric typed-absence semantics across §3.3 rules — test-fixture completeness hazard (test defect surfaced + fixed at FP-008.4 Commit 1.5 failure #3)

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-26 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-05-30 (FP-008.4 Commit 1.5 failure #3 three-way reconciliation) |
| **Classification** | test fixture defect (not production rule defect); discoverability hazard for any future §3.3-touching test author |
| **Surface Story** | Two tests in `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator_test.ts` were failing: `happy path → outcome=completed; counts populated; eligible returned + persisted` (line 153 — expected `total_eligible_short=6`, got `0`) and `metrics emitter invoked on outcome=completed with reason arrays` (line 301 — expected `hard_exclusion_reasons.length=0`, got `6`). Three-way reconciliation: (1) §3.3d rule docstring (`rule-3-3d-htb.ts:15-17`) — *"tickers MISSING from `locate_data` are treated as 'no locate' (defensive — better to skip a short than enter one blind). This is NOT a silent default; it is the documented contract."* (2) Type docstring (`hard-exclusions/types.ts:62-67`) — same contract reiterated as typed-absence per §2 axiom 3. (3) Test fixture `makeContext` provided `locate_data: []`. Spec + implementation + type contract all agreed: empty `locate_data` → `htb_no_locate` fires for every constituent → `short_eligible=false`. Test fixture was the outlier; its author had assumed "empty arrays = no rule firings" which is true for §3.3a/b/c/e but **false** for §3.3d. |
| **Observation** | The conceptual hazard is **asymmetric typed-absence polarity** across §3.3 rules: §3.3a (no earnings entry → no firing), §3.3b (no M&A action → no firing), §3.3c (no halt history → no firing), §3.3e (no short-interest record → no firing) — but §3.3d **inverts** the polarity (no locate record → firing). The asymmetry is correct and spec-mandated (positive evidence of borrow availability is required before shorting; missing data is exclusionary, not neutral), but it is a documented contract that lives only inside `rule-3-3d-htb.ts` and `types.ts`. A test-fixture author wiring `exclusionInput` for a happy-path scenario who pattern-matches on the four neutral-absence rules will silently produce an all-shorts-excluded fixture, masking real orchestrator behavior. **Not a production defect** — the rule fires correctly; the fixture was incomplete. But the meta-finding (asymmetric typed-absence is a discoverability hazard, not just a spec quirk) is durable and applies forward to every future §3.3-touching test. |
| **Disposition** | Fixed at this commit: `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator_test.ts:makeContext.exclusionInput.locate_data` now constructs positive locate records (`{ ticker, locate_available: true, borrow_rate_bps: 0 }`) for each polygon ticker, with an inline comment naming the §3.3d typed-absence contract, the §3.3e polarity asymmetry, and a back-reference to INC-26. Forward-binding prophylaxis: comment-in-fixture is the surface; the INC entry is the searchable record. No code change to the rule, the type, or any production path. No regression in the other 14 orchestrator tests (15/15 green) or suite-wide Deno typecheck (exit 0 across 17 _test.ts files). |
| **Cross-references** | §3.3d contract at `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/hard-exclusions/rule-3-3d-htb.ts:15-17` + `rule-3-3d-htb.ts:34-44` (no-record-firing branch); LocateRecord typed-absence contract at `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/hard-exclusions/types.ts:62-73`; §3.3e opposite-polarity contract at `rule-3-3e-short-interest.ts:15-18` + `:35` (no-record-no-fire); fixture fix at `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator_test.ts:makeContext`; FP-008 sub-step 8.3 / ACT-107 (original §3.3d rule landing). |
| **Status** | Resolved at this commit. Tracking entry preserved for forward-binding fixture-discipline reference (asymmetric typed-absence is a known discoverability hazard; future §3.3-touching test authors should consult this entry). |

### INC-27: api-conventions CORS contract has no canonical doc source — convention lives only in docstrings + tests (latent + fixed at FP-008.4 Commit 1.5 failure #5)

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-27 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-05-30 (FP-008.4 Commit 1.5 failure #5 three-way reconciliation) |
| **Classification** | (a) api-critical contract drift (fixed in-pass) + (b) discoverability hazard — no `docs/` source-of-truth document names the `apiSuccess`/`apiError` CORS responsibility contract; it lives only in `handler.ts:161` + `api-error.ts:14,30,34` docstrings + the `shared_test.ts:209-215` test assertion |
| **Surface Story** | Test `apiSuccess returns JSON with CORS headers` (`shared_test.ts:209`) was failing under end-to-end Deno suite execution: `assertExists(res.headers.get("Access-Control-Allow-Origin"))` got `null`. Three-way reconciliation across (1) `apiSuccess` docstring at `handler.ts:161` (*"Build a success JSON response with CORS headers"* — explicit CORS responsibility), (2) sibling `apiError` implementation at `api-error.ts:30,34` (defensive bake-in via `opts?._cors ?? corsHeaders` static fallback + `...headers` spread into response), and (3) the test assertion showed three-of-three converged on "`apiSuccess` should include CORS by default." Implementation at `handler.ts:162-167` was the outlier — returned only `Content-Type: application/json` with no `_cors` parameter and no static fallback. Asymmetry between the two sibling helpers (apiError defensive, apiSuccess bare) is the bug. **Production-significance:** masked-in-practice because every current edge function uses `createHandler` which post-hoc injects CORS at `handler.ts:62-65` over the handler's response; standalone callers (test, any future call site that bypasses the wrapper, any architecture revision that drops the post-hoc injection) emit responses the browser silently rejects. Latent, not yet production-firing — but a class-1 chokepoint defect (api-critical) per the canonical-truth standard. |
| **Observation** | Two findings stacked. **(a) Direct fix:** `apiSuccess` now mirrors `apiError`'s defensive pattern — accepts optional `opts._cors` for dynamic-origin propagation from `createHandler`, falls back to the static `corsHeaders` export from `cors.ts` (the backward-compat export that exists precisely for this case). Same signature shape as `apiError` (`opts?: { ..., _cors?: Record<string, string> }`); same fallback chain (`opts?._cors ?? staticCorsHeaders`); same spread pattern (`{ ...headers, 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }`). No change to `createHandler`'s post-hoc injection (line 62-65 remains; it's now a defense-in-depth overwrite rather than the sole CORS path). **(b) Meta-finding — canonical-source gap:** The api-conventions module has no `docs/04-modules/` or `docs/07-reference/` document naming the CORS responsibility contract. The convention is *only* expressed in two adjacent docstrings (`handler.ts:161`, `api-error.ts:14`) and one test assertion (`shared_test.ts:212`). A future contributor extending the api-helpers (or reading `cors.ts` and wondering why `corsHeaders` exists as a backward-compat export alongside `getCorsHeaders`) has no canonical artifact to consult — they reconstruct the contract from grep. Same shape as INC-26 (test/docstring is the de-facto contract) but lower severity (api-conventions, not financial-critical). |
| **Disposition** | Fix (a) landed at this commit: `handler.ts:162-180` (new docstring naming the apiError-symmetry rationale + cross-referencing INC-27; signature + body extended to mirror apiError); import line 10 extended to also pull `corsHeaders as staticCorsHeaders` from `cors.ts`. Test (`shared_test.ts:209-215`) now passes; all 26 `shared_test.ts` tests green; suite-wide Deno typecheck exit 0 across all 17 `_test.ts` files. Meta-finding (b) tracked here only — no doc creation in-pass per scope discipline; canonical api-conventions doc surface (likely `docs/04-modules/api/` or extension to an existing platform doc) deferred for operator disposition because (i) the convention is now mechanically enforced by the test + by inter-helper symmetry (apiError is the worked example), and (ii) creating a parallel doc when two docstrings + a test already express the contract may be duplication rather than clarification. Recommended disposition: defer to next api-module-touching change (low cost to fold in then; zero cost to defer now). |
| **Cross-references** | Fix at `supabase/functions/_shared/handler.ts:162-180` (apiSuccess) + `handler.ts:10` (import); pattern source at `supabase/functions/_shared/api-error.ts:18-36`; static fallback at `supabase/functions/_shared/cors.ts:67`; canonical wrapper at `handler.ts:29-111` (createHandler) + `:62-65` (post-hoc CORS injection chokepoint); test at `supabase/functions/_shared/shared_test.ts:209-215`; related discoverability-hazard pattern at INC-26 (asymmetric typed-absence across §3.3 rules — same convention-without-canonical-source shape, higher severity). |
| **Status** | Direct fix (a) resolved at this commit. Meta-finding (b) open — awaiting operator disposition on whether to author a canonical api-conventions doc surface or accept docstring-and-test as the de-facto contract. |

### INC-28: Cron-only handler test sentinels lagged the JWT→cron-secret architectural conversion (test-fixture drift across 2 handlers; convention codified at FP-008.4 Commit 1.5c)

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-28 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-05-30 (FP-008.4 Commit 1.5c three-way reconciliation, Cluster B of the 17-failure full-execution surfacing) |
| **Classification** | test fixture drift (NOT production defect); same class as INC-26 (test/spec/impl three-way reconciliation, fixture is the outlier). Discoverability + convention hazard for any future cron-dispatched edge function. |
| **Surface Story** | Three handler-source-sentinel tests were failing under end-to-end Deno suite execution: (1) `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index_test.ts:(b)` ordering invariant asserting `isFirstTradingDayOfQuarter` appears before `authenticateRequest(req)`; (2) `…/index_test.ts:(c)` asserting `checkPermissionOrThrow` + `'longshort.view'` literal in handler source; (3) `longshort-universe-hard-exclusion-refresh/index_test.ts:(c)` identical assertion shape. Three-way reconciliation across (a) handler docstrings (quarterly L1-15, hard-excl L1-29 — explicit "Cron-only system path"; "JWT auth would fail from pg_cron"; "system-level cron path"), (b) production implementation (both handlers import + call `verifyCronSecret(req)` from `_shared/cron-auth.ts` — real 401-on-missing/wrong-secret check; neither imports nor calls `authenticateRequest` or `checkPermissionOrThrow`), and (c) test sentinels — converged: handlers were deliberately converted JWT-auth → cron-secret-auth in a prior commit (architecturally correct: pg_cron has no user identity, user-identity primitives are conceptually inapplicable), but the test sentinels were not refreshed in the same change. Gate 11's `deno test --no-run` mode (type-check only) concealed the drift across the entire interval until full execution at FP-008.4 Commit 1.5. |
| **Observation** | The conceptual hazard is **test-sentinel-vs-architectural-truth drift on cron-only handlers** — a class-specific instance of the broader execution-layer concealment pattern (parallel to INC-25's type-check layer mechanism and INC-27's wrapper-owns-CORS-so-helper-can-be-bare mental model). Specific finding: cron-dispatched edge functions need a **different** test-sentinel convention than JWT-auth handlers. Asserting `checkPermissionOrThrow` + permission key on a cron-only function is conceptually inapplicable; the correct sentinels are (i) `verifyCronSecret` presence + import from `_shared/cron-auth.ts`, (ii) ABSENCE of `authenticateRequest` / `checkPermissionOrThrow` (positive assertion that the wrong primitive hasn't been re-introduced silently), and (iii) ordering invariant `verifyCronSecret` BEFORE the work-gate (auth-first is correct with cheap cron-secret HMAC; the old JWT-path "gating-first for load-shedding" rationale doesn't apply). The third sentinel is itself an architectural finding worth surfacing: the cron-secret auth conversion enabled the auth-first ordering invariant inversion, but that inversion isn't documented anywhere prior to this entry. |
| **Disposition** | Fixed at this commit. Three test rewrites applied surgically with test-name preservation (git-blame continuity): (1) `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index_test.ts:(b)` — assertion inverted to `verifyCronSecret(req)` BEFORE `isFirstTradingDayOfQuarter(as_of)`; (2) `…/index_test.ts:(c)` — converted to assert `verifyCronSecret` present + cron-auth.ts import present + `checkPermissionOrThrow` and `authenticateRequest` ABSENT (positive-absence-of-wrong-primitive sentinels prevent silent re-introduction of JWT-style authz); (3) `longshort-universe-hard-exclusion-refresh/index_test.ts:(c)` — identical fix shape as (2). Inline comments in all three rewrites name INC-28, the architectural rationale, and the convention. No production handler change (handlers were already correct). No new test added; conversion is contract-shape preservation, not new coverage. |
| **Cross-references** | Handler at `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index.ts:115` (`verifyCronSecret` call) + `:30` (import); handler at `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-hard-exclusion-refresh/index.ts:95` (`verifyCronSecret` call) + `:31` (import); auth primitive at `supabase/functions/_shared/cron-auth.ts:18-36` (real 401-on-failure check); test rewrites at `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index_test.ts:82-126` and `longshort-universe-hard-exclusion-refresh/index_test.ts:42-74`; related execution-layer concealment findings at INC-25 + INC-27 (subsume into the layered-mechanism finding planned for FP-008.4 close-out summary — INC-28 is the third instance, not a fourth distinct mechanism). |
| **Status** | Resolved at this commit. Forward-binding convention: any new cron-dispatched edge function added to the platform should use INC-28's test-sentinel triad (verifyCronSecret present + import present + user-identity primitives absent + auth-first ordering) as the regression contract. Worth folding into a `docs/04-modules/api/` cron-handler conventions doc when one is authored, or referencing INC-28 inline in a future cron handler's test file. |

### INC-29: Project lacks a separate integration-test convention — 11 live-DB-dependent tests in unit-test naming + auth-js leak-detector tripping in supabase-admin-importing unit tests (systemic; convention codified at FP-008.4 Commit 1.5d)

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-29 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-05-30 (FP-008.4 Commit 1.5d Cluster A/C/D triage + classification refinement) |
| **Classification** | systemic test-convention gap (NOT production defect). Two distinct sub-findings stacked: **(a)** Integration tests requiring live Supabase DB + auth state are filed under the same `*_test.ts` naming as pure unit tests — Gate 11's deterministic-execution promise is broken if integration tests run alongside unit tests in the gate; **(b)** Unit tests that import `supabase-admin` (transitive `@supabase/auth-js`) trip Deno's per-test leak detector on the auth-js `startAutoRefresh()` interval, which is a process-lifetime resource, not a per-test leak. Both surfaced together during the 17-failure full-execution triage that strengthened Gate 11 from `--no-run` to full `deno test`. |
| **Surface Story** | Strengthening Gate 11 from type-check-only to full execution surfaced 17 failures (Commit 1.5 frame). Initial triage classified 11 of them as "Cluster C — get-profile DB/auth-dependent integration" and 1 as "Cluster D — log-sudo-event DB-dependent." Re-investigation at Commit 1.5d distinguished the two: **(a) Cluster C IS integration** — `get-profile/index_test.ts` is 13/13 integration (SETUP test creating real users via `supabaseAdmin.auth.admin.createUser`, 11 tests calling deployed function URLs, TEARDOWN deleting users); already wraps the suite-bracket tests in `Deno.test({ ..., sanitizeOps: false, sanitizeResources: false })` (the convention exists locally — proves the auth-js leak is a known artifact of this file's shape). **(b) Cluster D was misclassified** — `log-sudo-event/index_test.ts` uses `installStubFetch` to isolate the handler and never hits a real DB; the failure (only on the leading test, lines 136 + 164 passed) was Deno's leak detector firing on `@supabase/auth-js/startAutoRefresh` from supabase-admin's transitive import (verified via `--trace-leaks`: `te._startAutoRefresh at .../auth-js.mjs:19:24823`). The first stub-fetch test triggers the auth client's process-lifetime auto-refresh; per-test leak detector flags it on that test only. Same root cause as the existing get-profile convention but applied to a different test pattern. |
| **Observation** | Two forward-binding conventions emerge: **(1) Integration-test naming convention** — for tests that fundamentally require live Supabase state (real users, real DB rows, real session JWTs), the project should adopt a `*_integration_test.ts` suffix (or equivalent marker) so Gate 11's deterministic execution can `exclude` them via `deno.json`. Phase 2+ test-strategy refinement should add a separate integration-gate (e.g., Gate 11.5) that runs the integration suite against an ephemeral Supabase project or seeded staging DB. **(2) Auth-js leak-sanitize convention** — every unit test that imports a module which transitively imports `supabase-admin` (any test that ultimately touches `_shared/supabase-admin.ts` even via stubs) must declare `sanitizeOps: false, sanitizeResources: false` on at least the leading test, because `@supabase/auth-js/startAutoRefresh()` registers a `setInterval` for token refresh that Deno's per-test detector flags as uncleared. This is a known supabase-js-under-Deno artifact, not a real production resource leak (it's process-lifetime, cleared on Deno exit). Both conventions are quiet contracts that lived only in `get-profile/index_test.ts`'s local usage before this entry — same shape as INC-27's "convention without canonical source" finding. |
| **Disposition** | Three changes at this commit: **(a)** `supabase/functions/deno.json` `exclude` extended with `get-profile/index_test.ts` (whole-file exclusion — 13/13 are integration; whole-file is the correct unit of exclusion). **(b)** `log-sudo-event/index_test.ts` — all three `Deno.test(name, fn)` calls converted to `Deno.test({ name, sanitizeOps: false, sanitizeResources: false, fn })` form for symmetry + forward protection (would have been per-test sufficient on the leading test only, but applying to all three is the canonical defensive shape — a future contributor reordering tests won't have to re-debug the same leak). **(c)** Cluster A test rewrites in `deactivate-user/index_test.ts` + `reactivate-user/index_test.ts`: CORS preflight assertion converted from wildcard literal (`== "*"`) to structural + positive-absence sentinel (present + non-empty + NOT wildcard) — same INC-28 pattern asserting absence of the wrong primitive to lock the dynamic-origin policy against silent regression to wildcard (which would be a CSRF surface on these admin mutation endpoints — wildcard would be the defect, not the dynamic value). |
| **Cross-references** | Integration test (excluded): `supabase/functions/get-profile/index_test.ts:107-285` (13 tests, SETUP + 11 + TEARDOWN). Exclusion mechanism: `supabase/functions/deno.json:exclude`. Auth-js leak fix: `supabase/functions/log-sudo-event/index_test.ts:104-188` (three tests). Cluster A test rewrites: `supabase/functions/deactivate-user/index_test.ts:CORS-preflight` + `reactivate-user/index_test.ts:CORS-preflight`. CORS policy source-of-truth: `supabase/functions/_shared/cors.ts:44-65` (dynamic-origin allow-list with Lovable preview hosts). Pre-existing local-only application of the auth-js sanitize convention: `supabase/functions/get-profile/index_test.ts:110` + `:285` (SETUP/TEARDOWN brackets). Related convention-without-canonical-source findings: INC-27 (api-conventions CORS contract) + INC-28 (cron-only handler test sentinels) — INC-29 is the third instance; together they justify a future `docs/04-modules/api/` or `docs/03-tests/` conventions surface. |
| **Status** | Resolved at this commit. Forward-binding work registered for `docs/08-planning/deferred-work-register.md`: **(i)** rename `get-profile/index_test.ts` → `get-profile/index_integration_test.ts` + extend `deno.json` `exclude` to pattern `**/*_integration_test.ts` (convention encoding) when next touching the integration test or whenever an additional integration test is added; **(ii)** author Gate 11.5 (integration suite against ephemeral/staging Supabase) as part of Phase 2 test-strategy refinement; **(iii)** fold the auth-js sanitize convention into a `docs/03-tests/edge-function-test-conventions.md` surface when one is authored, or reference INC-29 inline in the next supabase-admin-importing unit test added. |
| **Scope Correction at Commit 1.5i (2026-05-30)** | Initial 1.5d disposition excluded only `get-profile/index_test.ts` based on the then-failing CI surface (Cluster C single-file framing). 1.5h CI surfaced 13 additional failures (`TypeError: Invalid URL: 'undefined/functions/v1/…'`) across 3 more files: `deactivate-user/index_test.ts` (3 tests), `reactivate-user/index_test.ts` (3 tests), `query-audit-logs/index_test.ts` (7 tests). Comprehensive `grep fetch\( \|\${BASE}\|Deno.env.get` survey of all 7 `*_test.ts` files under `supabase/functions/` (excluding `_shared/`) confirms the true integration-test set is **4 files** (the three above + get-profile). Corrected scope: `supabase/functions/deno.json` `exclude` now covers all 4. **Polarity-asymmetry note (explicit, INC-29-binding):** `log-sudo-event/index_test.ts` is **INTENTIONALLY NOT excluded** — it uses a stub URL (`Deno.env.set("SUPABASE_URL", "http://stub.local")` + `installStubFetch`) and is a genuine unit test that stays in Gate 11. Future contributors: handler-directory `index_test.ts` files are NOT uniformly integration tests; classify by `fetch(` + env-var-derived-URL pattern, not by file location. Same discipline shape as `universe.md`'s polarity-asymmetry subsection — name the asymmetry, prevent future pattern-matching errors. Fourth instance of the FP-008.4 enforcement-gap mechanism (alongside DW-079 / DW-080 lineage): each test-discovery-and-exclusion round was based on the previous round's failure surface, not on a comprehensive survey. Resolved at 1.5i via grep-survey-before-exclusion. Forward-binding work for integration-test naming/location convention is now registered as DW-081 (was previously a sub-bullet on this row's Status field). |

### INC-30: Gate 11 type-check coverage gap — edge function entry points without sibling `_test.ts` files are invisible to `deno test --no-run`'s dependency-graph walk (mechanism-class fifth instance; FP-008.4 Commit 2.5)

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-30 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-03 (FP-008.4 Commit 2 — 4 pre-existing TS2353 errors in `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-enrich-and-filter/index.ts` at lines 91, 126, 253, 282 survived 1.5i + Commit 2 CI-green) |
| **Classification** | CI enforcement-gap (NOT production defect). Fifth instance of the FP-008.4 enforcement-gap mechanism class (alongside DW-079 / DW-080 / DW-081 + the 1.5i polarity-asymmetry sub-row on INC-29). Same shape: a gate that nominally enforces a contract actually enforces it only on the subset of files reachable from the discovery seed it walks. |
| **Surface Story** | Commit 2 strengthened the eligibility-coverage write path in `longshort-universe-enrich-and-filter/index.ts` (Step 5.5 — `writeEligibilityCoverage` call after the refresh_log INSERT). During local pre-flight ladder the AI surfaced 4 `TS2353: 'details' does not exist in type` errors against `apiError(...)` call sites at lines 91, 126, 253, 282 — `apiError`'s `opts` type is `{ code?, field?, correlationId?, _cors? }` (per `supabase/functions/_shared/api-error.ts:18-22`); `details` was never a valid key. The errors are pre-existing (not introduced by Commit 2) and the file has lived on `main` with them through the entire FP-008 / FP-008.2 / FP-008.3 / FP-008.4 sequence. **Why Gate 11 missed them**: Gate 11 runs `deno test --no-run` (and post-1.5e full `deno test`) which type-checks the transitive closure reachable from each `*_test.ts` file's import graph. `enrich-and-filter/index.ts` has no sibling `index_test.ts` and is imported by no test under `supabase/functions/` — it is an orphan entry point. The mechanism: **Gate 11 enforces type-check coverage only on files reachable from a test-file import seed; orphan entry points are silently invisible.** Survey at Commit 2.5 enumerated **46 orphan edge function entry points** out of 53 total (`supabase/functions/<name>/index.ts` with no sibling `index_test.ts` / `index.test.ts`); of those 46, **3 currently have type errors** that Gate 11 cannot see: `enrich-and-filter` (4× TS2353 — this commit's target), `longshort-reconciliation-tick` (1× TS2322 — `SupabaseClient` generic identity mismatch between `esm.sh` import and `node_modules` resolved path), `send-signup-nudge` (3× TS2339 — `Property 'user' does not exist on type 'User'`, likely stale `getUser()` response shape). |
| **Observation** | The conceptual hazard is **gate-coverage-via-discovery-seed asymmetry** — a gate that walks a graph from a seed enforces its contract only on the walked subgraph, never on disconnected nodes, and the disconnection is invisible unless a separate audit enumerates the universe. Same shape as DW-081's "test-classification by file location alone breaks when location is uniform across categories" finding and DW-079's "merge gate enforcement was nominal not actual because the gate had been red on main for the duration." Specific finding: every edge function entry point (`supabase/functions/<name>/index.ts`) needs a sibling `_test.ts` file to enter Gate 11's type-check coverage — even if the test file does nothing more than `import "./index.ts"` + a no-op `Deno.test` to anchor the walk. Forward-binding convention: orphan entry points are a CI-coverage defect class, not an authoring-preference. |
| **Disposition** | Commit 2.5 — **scoped fix only** per execution-prompt survey-decision rule ("any non-trivial errors → STOP; fix only enrich-and-filter; rest becomes tracked follow-up under DW-082's umbrella"). Three changes: (1) `enrich-and-filter/index.ts` — 4× `apiError(500, key, { correlationId, details: x.message })` rewritten to `apiError(500, ` + "`${key}: ${x.message}`" + `, { correlationId })` (folds the diagnostic payload into the message string; correlation_id preserved; no API key invented). (2) `enrich-and-filter/index_test.ts` — NEW thin anchor file: `import "./index.ts"` + `assertExists(import.meta.url)` no-op assertion; uses `https://deno.land/std@0.208.0/assert/mod.ts` (matching the version already in use across the longshort-universe `_test.ts` files). The type-check IS the test. (3) DW-082 registered for the remaining 2 orphan files with type errors (`longshort-reconciliation-tick` + `send-signup-nudge`) AND for the broader 46-orphan structural gap (CI lint to fail on orphan entry points). Anti-pattern guards held: no `// @ts-ignore`, no `// deno-lint-ignore`, no `continue-on-error` — errors fixed properly. |
| **Cross-references** | `apiError` signature at `supabase/functions/_shared/api-error.ts:18-22` (the contract that the 4 call sites violated); 4 fix sites at `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-enrich-and-filter/index.ts` lines 88-92, 124-128, 251-255, 280-284 (post-fix line numbers); new anchor at `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-enrich-and-filter/index_test.ts`; survey output at Commit 2.5 closure SHA (46 orphan entry points; 3 with type errors); related FP-008.4 mechanism-class entries DW-079 / DW-080 / DW-081 / INC-29 polarity-asymmetry row; sibling-shape work item DW-082 (this entry's forward-binding companion); `.github/workflows/strong-evidence.yml:Gate 11` step (the gate whose coverage gap this finding documents). |
| **Status** | Resolved for `enrich-and-filter` at Commit 2.5. **Commit 7.5 update:** the Tier-A `longshort-reconciliation-tick` TS2322 half of DW-082 A1 is CLOSED by SupabaseClient specifier unification (`@supabase/supabase-js` canonical import-map path), `@ts-nocheck` removal, handler 403 audit-settling test fix, and Gate 14 recurrence-prevention sentinel; Gate 11 passes 246/246. **Open structurally:** orphan edge function entry points still bypass Gate 11 unless they have sibling tests, and `send-signup-nudge` remains the known ordinary-backlog orphan type error under DW-082 A2. Forward-binding convention: any new edge function added under `supabase/functions/<name>/` MUST land with a sibling `index_test.ts` in the same PR — even if the test is a one-line `import "./index.ts"` + no-op Deno.test anchor. DW-082 Part B codifies the CI lint that will fail on orphan entry points (future orphan-entry gate; Gate 14 now separately covers SupabaseClient specifier recurrence). |

### INC-31: universe_refresh_log.outcome CHECK constraint vs orchestrator writer mismatch — D1 from FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation survey (resolved at Commit 3)

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-31 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-03 (FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation survey of the circuit-breaker mechanism) |
| **Classification** | Latent defect in a Tier-A safety mechanism (circuit breaker). Pre-existing. Schema-vs-code contract mismatch — DB-layer CHECK constraint and application-layer enum diverged. |
| **Surface Story** | Commit 3 pre-investigation surveyed the longshort circuit breaker end-to-end. `public.universe_refresh_log.outcome` carries an inline `CHECK (outcome IN ('completed', 'failed', 'partial'))` from MIG-048 (constraint name `universe_refresh_log_outcome_check`). The orchestrator at `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts` finalizes a tripped breaker by writing `outcome: 'circuit_breaker_open'` (also present in the `RefreshOutcome` TS union). The DB would reject this value with a 23514 CHECK violation on every real trip and leave a half-finalized row. The breaker has not actually tripped to date because of the separate D5 self-masking race (see DW-082-adjacent survey output and Commit 3.5's scope) — so this constraint mismatch has never manifested at runtime. |
| **Observation** | Conceptual hazard: **type-vs-constraint drift in two-sided enums** — when an application-layer enum and a DB-layer CHECK constraint are authored at different times by different commits, no compiler reconciles them. Same mechanism class as DW-080 (version-pin asymmetry across import surfaces). Specific finding: the constraint widening is a one-line ALTER, but the failure mode it eliminates is silent-until-first-trip. The four-value form `('completed', 'failed', 'partial', 'circuit_breaker_open')` is exactly what the writer needs; NULL is preserved as the in-flight-row convention. |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 3** — `sql/12_universe_refresh_outcome_check_widening.sql` / MIG-056 drops and re-adds the constraint with the 4-value set. Idempotent DROP-then-ADD pattern mirroring `audit_logs_actor_id_fkey` rebuild migration. NULL acceptance preserved (no NOT NULL added). Operator applies via Supabase SQL Editor; live-DB verification per §22.5.1 to follow. D5 / D2 / D3 explicitly out of Commit 3 scope (sequenced to Commits 3.5 / 4). |
| **Cross-references** | MIG-048 (`supabase/migrations/20260525093303_7fe13534-fbe0-4089-9143-bc4ec98ce7d9.sql:15` — original 3-value CHECK); MIG-056 (`sql/12_universe_refresh_outcome_check_widening.sql` — this resolution); orchestrator writer site `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts` (`outcome: 'circuit_breaker_open'`); `RefreshOutcome` TS union (same file); Commit 3 pre-investigation survey at the Commit 3 closure SHA; sibling defects D5 (Commit 3.5), D2 + D3 (Commit 4); INC-32 (D6 — duplicate MIG-032 ledger gap); INC-33 (platform job-executor fail-open parallel). |
| **Status** | Resolved at Commit 3 SHA pending operator live-DB apply + §22.5.1 evidence binding. |
| **Resolution Confirmed (FP-020, 2026-06-07)** | Live-DB §22.5.1 verification (Q-3, 2026-06-07): `SELECT conname, pg_get_constraintdef(oid) FROM pg_constraint WHERE conname = 'universe_refresh_log_outcome_check'` returned `CHECK ((outcome = ANY (ARRAY['completed'::text, 'failed'::text, 'partial'::text, 'circuit_breaker_open'::text])))` — all four values present, MIG-056 applied. The prior "pending operator live-DB apply" status was stale; the migration had been applied months earlier. Original disposition row preserved per Constitution Rule 8; this addendum closes the §22.5.1 binding. Motivating example for DEC-041 (Pending-Evidence Dispositions Require Periodic Sweep) — "pending" masked applied-state. Status → **Resolved (live-DB confirmed)**. |

### INC-32: MIG-032 duplicate migration (20260513191627) for `circuit_breaker_threshold` ADD COLUMN is not registered in the ledger — D6 from FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation survey

| **Classification** | Documentation/ledger-discipline gap. Harmless at runtime (idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` against an already-present column — the second apply is a no-op). NOT a production defect. Constitution Rule 6 / D5 ledger-entry discipline violation. |
| **Surface Story** | Two timestamp-named migrations in `supabase/migrations/` perform the same `ALTER TABLE public.job_registry ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS circuit_breaker_threshold integer DEFAULT 3` as MIG-032: the canonical MIG-032 entry registered in the ledger, and a sibling timestamped `20260513191627` migration applying the identical statement. The duplicate is idempotent (the `IF NOT EXISTS` guard makes the second apply a no-op against a present column) and has no runtime effect — but it is not registered in `docs/07-reference/database-migration-ledger.md` and violates the D5 "every migration applied → ledger entry in same PR" discipline. |
| **Observation** | Conceptual hazard: **ledger-vs-filesystem drift via implicit-no-op migrations** — idempotency guards make duplicate migrations invisible at apply time, but they remain visible in the migration filesystem and the ledger loses the bijection. Future schema archaeology becomes ambiguous. |
| **Disposition** | Logged-and-deferred. Resolution shape: either (a) register the duplicate as a no-op MIG-NNN ledger entry with a back-reference to MIG-032, or (b) delete the duplicate file if the apply history shows it was never independently applied — operator decision; both are bounded-cost. Not scheduled in Bucket A. |
| **Cross-references** | Canonical MIG-032 entry in `docs/07-reference/database-migration-ledger.md`; duplicate file at `supabase/migrations/20260513191627_*.sql`; Constitution Rule 6 (documentation-in-same-PR discipline); D5 from FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation. |

### INC-33: Platform job-executor circuit breaker exhibits identical fail-open-on-read pattern as longshort breaker D2 — parallel-defect survey output from FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-33 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-03 (FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation survey — surfaced when comparing the longshort breaker's `countConsecutiveFailures` to platform breaker reads for shared-pattern propagation) |
| **Classification** | Latent defect in a Tier-A safety mechanism (platform job-executor circuit breaker). Parallel-shape to D2 from the longshort survey. Fail-open-on-read: a read failure prevents the breaker from tripping, allowing operations to proceed under degraded observability. |
| **Surface Story** | Platform job executor at `supabase/functions/_shared/job-executor.ts:128-145` (circuit-breaker read path) exhibits the same shape as the longshort `countConsecutiveFailures` D2 defect: the read of breaker state catches DB errors and returns a value that fails open (proceeds with execution) rather than failing closed (refusing to execute under uncertainty). The longshort survey identified this as D2 and is fixing it in Commit 4; the platform breaker carries the identical pattern but fronts a broader job universe of mixed criticality (financial / compliance / operational classes), so a blanket inversion would cause operational jobs to refuse to run during transient DB hiccups. |
| **Observation** | Conceptual hazard: **shared-pattern propagation in safety mechanisms** — the fail-open-vs-fail-closed decision is per-call-site and per-criticality-class; copying a breaker pattern across modules without re-evaluating the fail direction reproduces the defect at every site. Same mechanism class as DW-079 / DW-080 / DW-081 (pattern that nominally enforces a contract but actually doesn't, at multiple sites). |
| **Disposition** | Logged-and-deferred to DW-083 Part A (this commit's DW entry) — per-job-class audit required before applying Commit 4's inversion pattern to the platform breaker. Financial/compliance classes: fail-closed mandatory. Operational classes: operator decision per class. NOT a blanket fix. Commit 4's longshort fix is the canonical reference pattern. |
| **Cross-references** | `supabase/functions/_shared/job-executor.ts:128-145` (the fail-open read); longshort sibling `countConsecutiveFailures` (D2, fixed in Commit 4); D2 from FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation survey; DW-083 Part A (deferred fix); `job_registry.class` column (the per-class audit dimension); Constitution: this is the exact phantom-state class the governance model exists to catch. |
| **Status** | Open — deferred to DW-083 Part A; SHOULD-FIX-BEFORE-LIVE for financial/compliance job classes. |

### INC-34: Circuit-breaker self-masking race — orchestrator's in-flight NULL self-row at head of DESC-ordered tail breaks the streak count — D5 from FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation survey

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-34 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-03 (FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation survey of the circuit-breaker mechanism — D5; deferred from Commit 3 to Commit 3.5 per sequencing discipline because D1's CHECK widening is the prerequisite to D5 being safely fixable: with D5 fixed the breaker can actually trip, and the first real trip needs D1's widened CHECK to avoid a 23514 violation leaving a half-finalized row) |
| **Classification** | Latent correctness defect in a Tier-A safety mechanism (longshort universe-refresh circuit breaker). The breaker is structurally incapable of tripping in its pre-Commit-3.5 state. Same severity tier as INC-31 (D1) — both gate the breaker actually functioning. |
| **Surface Story** | `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts:78-92` calls `ctx.refreshLogPersister.insertStart(...)` BEFORE `ctx.refreshLogPersister.countConsecutiveFailures(3)`. The `insertStart` impl at `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index.ts:36-49` writes the in-flight row with `outcome` omitted (lands as NULL). The pre-Commit-3.5 `countConsecutiveFailures` query reads `.select('outcome').order('refresh_started_at', { ascending: false }).limit(N)` with NO `.in(...)` or `IS NOT NULL` filter — so this orchestrator's own freshly-inserted NULL row sits at the head of the DESC tail. The loop's first iteration hits `outcome=null`, falls into `else`, breaks, returns 0. Even if the prior 3 rows were `'failed'`, the count is masked to 0 and the breaker never trips. Identical masking applies to orphan-NULL rows left behind by any prior invocation that crashed before `finalize` patched the terminal outcome. |
| **Observation** | Conceptual hazard: **load-bearing convention without enforcement** — the `NULL = in-flight row` convention is implicit in the insertStart/finalize split, but nothing in the read path acknowledged it. The fix makes the convention explicit (`.in(...)` filter co-located with the sql/12 CHECK value set) AND load-bearing (a regression-sentinel test pins the filter at source level so a future refactor that introduces an `'in_progress'` non-NULL marker fails loudly). Sibling-shape to INC-31 (type-vs-constraint drift in two-sided enums): both are mechanism-class findings where two sides of a contract were authored without a compiler reconciling them — D5 is the read-side analogue of D1's write-side. The stay-tripped semantics decision (circuit_breaker_open counts toward the streak) is the deliberate operator choice over auto-rearm: auto-rearm would mask a persistent upstream failure as a transient one-run cooldown, which is worse than the current never-trips state for a Tier-A trade-gate. |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 3.5** — `countConsecutiveFailures` in `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index.ts` gains a `.in('outcome', ['completed','failed','partial','circuit_breaker_open'])` filter (excludes NULL self-row AND orphan-NULL rows) and switches the loop predicate from `row.outcome === 'failed'` to `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES.has(row.outcome as RefreshOutcome)`. `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES` is exported from `refresh-jobs/types.ts` adjacent to the `RefreshOutcome` union and contains `{'failed','circuit_breaker_open'}` — stay-tripped semantics: a tripped breaker stays tripped on subsequent runs until manually cleared. Five new tests in `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator_test.ts` cover: (1) D5 fix — 3 prior failed (NULL self-row excluded) trips; (2) stay-tripped — prior circuit_breaker_open + 2 failed trips again; (3) clean break — prior completed breaks the streak; (4) source-level convention pin — `.in()` filter + `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES.has` regression sentinel; (5) set-vs-union-vs-CHECK pin — drift between the TS union, predicate set, and sql/12 CHECK becomes a test failure. D2 fail-open-on-read (return 0 on error) explicitly UNCHANGED in this commit — scoped to Commit 4. D3 optional-method gap explicitly UNCHANGED — the test fake adds the method locally without touching the persister interface. `insertStart` vs `countConsecutiveFailures` ordering UNCHANGED — the NULL self-row is the in-flight signal; the fix is to read past it correctly, not to reorder. |
| **Cross-references** | INC-31 (D1 — sibling write-side type-vs-constraint defect; Commit 3 / MIG-056 resolution); orchestrator call site `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts:78-92`; persister impl site `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index.ts` (`countConsecutiveFailures`); `RefreshOutcome` union + `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES` set co-located at `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/types.ts:26-43`; sql/12 CHECK constraint `outcome IN ('completed','failed','partial','circuit_breaker_open')`; D2 / D3 deferred to Commit 4; D4 (manual-clear path RLS-blocked) deferred to DW-083 Part A MUST-FIX-BEFORE-LIVE — until DW-083 Part A closes, a tripped breaker requires raw service-role SQL to clear (acceptable in Phase 1 pre-live; blocking for Phase 2 live trading); FP-008.4 Commit 3.5 closure SHA. |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 3.5 SHA pending CI green on closure SHA. |

### INC-35: Circuit-breaker fail-open-on-read (D2) + optional-method silent-disable (D3) — both inverted to fail-closed at FP-008.4 Commit 4

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-35 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-03 (FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation survey — surfaced together as the read-path and interface-path symptoms of the same semantic event: "cannot assess the streak"; deferred from Commit 3 to Commit 4 per sequencing discipline because D1 (Commit 3) + D5 (Commit 3.5) are prerequisites to the breaker actually being able to trip, which is the prerequisite to D2/D3's now-live exposure being consequential) |
| **Classification** | Latent correctness defect pair in a Tier-A safety mechanism (longshort universe-refresh circuit breaker). D2: fail-open-on-read — `countConsecutiveFailures` in the production persister caught DB read errors and returned 0, allowing the next refresh to proceed under a degraded (blind) breaker. D3: the method was declared optional on `RefreshLogPersister`; a persister that omitted it silently disabled the breaker via an `? ... : 0` fallback in the orchestrator. Both became consequential the moment Commit 3.5 made the breaker functional. |
| **Surface Story** | D2: `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index.ts` `countConsecutiveFailures` — `if (error) { console.warn(...); return 0; }`. A transient DB read failure masked any prior failure streak as zero, letting a fresh pipeline run proceed even if the last three attempts had failed. D3: `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/types.ts` `RefreshLogPersister.countConsecutiveFailures?(limit)` — the `?` optional marker plus the orchestrator's `ctx.refreshLogPersister.countConsecutiveFailures ? await ... : 0` guard meant a persister that omitted the method silently turned the breaker off. Together: two paths ("DB read failed" / "method not implemented") that should halt the gate instead both fell through to "no failures, proceed". |
| **Observation** | Conceptual hazard: **fail-direction on a safety-mechanism read MUST match the mechanism's posture, not the read's typical use** — `return 0 on read error` is appropriate for an observability counter (zero means "nothing to report"), but inverts the semantics when the counter front-ends a trade-gate (zero means "no prior failures, proceed"). The §2 axiom ("missing data ⇒ typed-absence path; synthetic numerics pretending completeness = defect") catches this exact class: `0` was a synthetic completeness claim for a read that produced no information. Sibling-shape to INC-33 (platform job-executor breaker exhibits the identical pattern across job classes — deferred to DW-083 Part B for per-class audit; Commit 4 is the canonical reference pattern for the financial/compliance halt direction). The decision between fail-shapes was not code-inferable: (a) trip on read error → one blip stuck-until-raw-SQL (rejected, worse than current given D4 unfixed); (b) throw to handler → breaker permanently blind on persistent read failure (rejected, quiet); (b-prime) finalize as failed and count toward streak → three consecutive read errors converge to a normal breaker trip via the same code path as three real pipeline failures (accepted — operator-confirmed; persistent-read-failure converges to the breaker's own halt-and-surface mechanism rather than a quieter stream of HTTP 500s; consistent with stay-tripped posture from Commit 3.5). |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 4** — D2 (b-prime): the orchestrator wraps `countConsecutiveFailures` in a narrow dedicated try (around only the count call, not the trip block or main pipeline try). A read failure finalizes the in-flight start-row with `outcome: 'failed'` + `failure_reason: 'count_consecutive_failures_read_failed: <err>'` + early-returns; pipeline does not run; finalize targets the same `refresh_id` returned by `insertStart` (patches the in-flight row, does NOT create a second row). Read-error 'failed' rows are streak-eligible per `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES` (which includes `'failed'`), so three consecutive read failures reach `recentFailures >= 3` on the fourth run and trip the breaker via the standard path. Also eliminates the orphan-NULL-on-read-error case: the start-row is always finalized to a terminal outcome, never left dangling. D3 (x): `countConsecutiveFailures` is now required (non-optional) on `RefreshLogPersister`; the `?` marker was removed; the orchestrator's `? ... : 0` fallback was deleted entirely. A missing implementation is now a TypeScript compile error rather than a runtime fail-open branch. The single test stub that omitted the method (`makePersister()` in `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator_test.ts`) was updated with a 3-line `return 0` stub satisfying the new required signature; the other two implementors (production `makeSupabasePersister` + Commit 3.5 `makeBreakerPersister`) already implemented it. Three new tests in `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator_test.ts`: (1) D2 fail-closed — throwing count → finalize failed, pipeline skipped (sentinel checks on all three fetchers), failure_reason contains both `count_consecutive_failures_read_failed` and the underlying error message, finalize targets the insertStart refresh_id; (2) D2 single-finalize pin — read-error path invokes finalize exactly once (guards against double-finalize collision; pins the four-path mutual-exclusion control flow); (3) D2 converges to breaker trip — three prior 'failed' rows (representing the post-state after three throwing runs) → fourth run trips with outcome 'circuit_breaker_open', proving read-error 'failed' rows are streak-indistinguishable from pipeline-failure 'failed' rows. D4 (manual-clear path RLS-blocked) explicitly UNCHANGED — remains DW-083 Part A MUST-FIX-BEFORE-LIVE; this commit increases its blast radius (a sustained 3-day read outage now trips a breaker that requires raw service-role SQL to clear, acceptable in Phase 1 pre-live, HARD GATE on Phase 2 live signal-stack reliance). Platform job-executor breaker (`job-executor.ts:128-145`) UNCHANGED — remains DW-083 Part B per-class audit; Commit 4 is the canonical fail-closed pattern for the financial/compliance classes. No SQL, no RLS, no clear-RPC, no migration. |
| **Cross-references** | D2/D3 from FP-008.4 Commit 3 pre-investigation survey; orchestrator narrow-try site `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts` (breaker-read section); interface site `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/types.ts` `RefreshLogPersister.countConsecutiveFailures`; production persister `supabase/functions/longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index.ts` `makeSupabasePersister` (unchanged — already implemented the method post-3.5); test additions `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator_test.ts` (3 new Deno.test blocks + `makePersister()` stub update + `makeThrowingCountPersister` helper); INC-31 (D1, Commit 3); INC-33 (platform-breaker parallel; DW-083 Part B); INC-34 (D5, Commit 3.5); DW-083 Part A (D4 clear-path MUST-FIX-BEFORE-LIVE); DW-083 Part B (platform-breaker per-class audit using Commit 4 as canonical pattern); `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES` co-located with `RefreshOutcome` at `refresh-jobs/types.ts`; sql/12 CHECK constraint; FP-008.4 Commit 4 closure SHA. |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 4 SHA pending CI green on closure SHA. |

### INC-36: `longshort_audit_logs` audit-log forgery vector + universe_membership/hard_exclusions garbage-write vectors — three over-broad PERMISSIVE INSERT policies defeated the system-actor-only-writes model; resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 5 / MIG-057

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-36 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-03 (FP-008.4 Commit 5 pre-investigation survey — surfaced when the "#4 RLS additive-defeat" item, originally framed against the SELECT read overlay, was traced to its actual mechanism on the write side across three tables) |
| **Classification** | **Tier-A security defect** — the most security-critical finding of the FP-008.4 Bucket A pass. The `longshort_audit_logs_insert_policy` PERMISSIVE INSERT policy was `WITH CHECK (true)`, meaning any authenticated user could INSERT arbitrary rows into the per-strategy audit log: any `operator_id`, any `actor`, any `action`, any `target_type`/`target_id`, any `metadata`, any `ip_address`/`user_agent`/`correlation_id`. The governance model (constitution §1 "repo is authoritative artifact"; §8 audit-as-Tier-A-evidence) treats the audit log as ground truth for "what the system did"; a forgeable-by-any-authenticated-user audit table means every audit-evidence-gated decision in the system rests on a machine-only-write property that did not actually hold. Sibling defects on the same write side, lesser but real: `universe_membership_operator_insert` and `hard_exclusions_operator_insert` were both `WITH CHECK (operator_id = auth.uid())` — any authenticated user could inject rows under their own UUID, bypassing the pipeline's refresh_id / firing-rule lineage and poisoning the downstream financial-data tables. |
| **Surface Story** | Three PERMISSIVE INSERT policies, all defeated by the same RLS axiom: PERMISSIVE policies OR-combine, so a single permissive policy whose WITH CHECK evaluates true under any authenticated condition grants the write — and no PERMISSIVE policy can constrain another PERMISSIVE policy. (a) `public.universe_membership` — `universe_membership_operator_insert` `WITH CHECK (operator_id = auth.uid())` allowed any authenticated user to insert universe rows under their own UUID. The companion `universe_membership_operator_read` `USING (operator_id = auth.uid())` SELECT policy is dead-but-harmless (the system actor writes under a synthetic operator_id no authenticated user owns); the longshort_view_read SELECT policy is the real read gate; the read side is intentional additive overlay, not the defeat. (b) `public.hard_exclusions` — identical shape, `hard_exclusions_operator_insert` `WITH CHECK (operator_id = auth.uid())`. (c) `public.longshort_audit_logs` — `longshort_audit_logs_insert_policy` `WITH CHECK (true)`, the forgery vector. All three legitimate writers run under the service role (verified per call site): `universe-membership-persister.ts:57-59`, `hard-exclusions-persister.ts:53-58`, `_shared/strategy-audit.ts:126-137` via `writeStrategyAuditEvent` — all use the shared `supabaseAdmin` client (service-role-keyed per `_shared/supabase-admin.ts:27` `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`). Service-role bypasses RLS, so removing the authenticated-role write paths affects zero legitimate writes. |
| **Pre-MIG-057 audit-evidence trust boundary** | The forge-vector was **latent-but-live** from `longshort_audit_logs`' creation through MIG-057 apply. No evidence exists that any authenticated user forged entries during that window. However, by the nature of a forgeable audit log, the audit log itself cannot prove the absence of forgery for its own pre-fix history — pre-MIG-057 entries are consistent with machine actions but not provably exclusive to them. This is an **epistemic boundary on historical evidence**, not a claim that forgery occurred. Operationally, this bounds what pre-MIG-057 audit evidence can be relied on to establish: §8's audit-as-Tier-A-evidence ladder assumes the log is machine-write-only, and for the pre-fix window that assumption did not hold. Post-MIG-057, the RESTRICTIVE deny restores the machine-only property; from MIG-057 apply forward, `longshort_audit_logs` entries are provably machine-written (service-role-only) and can be relied on per §8. Recorded here non-alarmingly because (a) the system was pre-live during the entire pre-fix window — the audit table fronts a strategy that has not run in production trade-execution mode — and (b) the only audit-gated decisions made against this log during the pre-fix window were internal governance/review decisions where the operators authoring the reviews are also the only authenticated users with system access, narrowing the practical forgery exposure to "could an operator have authored a forged entry attributing an action to a different operator," which is bounded by the operator-trust model in any case. The boundary matters for any future replay of pre-MIG-057 audit evidence as ground truth in a context that depends on the machine-only property (e.g., regulatory replay, post-incident attribution) — such use must caveat the pre-MIG-057 window. |
| **Observation** | Conceptual hazard: **PERMISSIVE-only RLS cannot enforce a deny** — the sibling `*_no_direct_write_policy` tables (`kill_switches`, `reconciliation_events`, `universe_eligibility_coverage`, `universe_refresh_log`, `longshort_reconciliation_state`) use PERMISSIVE `FOR ALL USING(false) WITH CHECK(false)`, which works ONLY because no other PERMISSIVE write policy is present. That is a fragile invariant enforced by convention (every future migration author must remember not to add a PERMISSIVE write policy to these tables) rather than by the type system. The RESTRICTIVE construct is the correct primitive for "this command cannot be additively granted": RESTRICTIVE policies AND-combine, so a RESTRICTIVE USING(false) / WITH CHECK(false) denies the command regardless of any present or future PERMISSIVE policy. The fix uses RESTRICTIVE for exactly this reason — it hardens the system-actor-only-writes invariant structurally, not by convention. Sibling-shape to the broader FP-008.4 mechanism-class findings (INC-31, INC-34, INC-35): "two sides of a contract authored without a compiler reconciling them." Here the two sides are "intended posture: only the system actor writes" and "enforced posture: anyone can." The compiler (Postgres RLS combination rules) silently allowed the gap. The sibling `*_no_direct_write_policy` tables deserve the same RESTRICTIVE hardening in a follow-up commit (not in #4 scope; see Disposition); leaving the PERMISSIVE-false-only shape on those tables means the same defect class can recur there if a future migration adds a permissive write policy. |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 5 / MIG-057** (`sql/13_rls_deny_authenticated_writes.sql`). For each of the three tables: dropped the over-broad PERMISSIVE INSERT policy and created three RESTRICTIVE deny-authenticated-write policies scoped per-command (`FOR INSERT WITH CHECK (false)`, `FOR UPDATE USING (false) WITH CHECK (false)`, `FOR DELETE USING (false)`) `TO authenticated`. Scoped per-command (not `FOR ALL`) because RESTRICTIVE AND-combines per command: `FOR ALL USING(false)` would AND against the SELECT read policies and deny all authenticated reads, breaking the dashboards. SELECT policies (`*_longshort_view_read`, `*_operator_read`) intentionally unchanged — the read overlay is the intended additive design per the original sql/ comments. Single transaction (BEGIN/COMMIT) so no observable window where the old PERMISSIVE INSERT is gone but the new RESTRICTIVE deny is not yet present. Idempotent (DROP POLICY IF EXISTS before every CREATE, mirroring sql/11). Posture: **service-role-only writes, no authenticated-role write path even for superadmin** — the three pipeline-write tables are not designed for human hand-editing (membership rows require valid refresh_id lineage, exclusion rows require firing-rule lineage, audit entries must be machine-written to satisfy the §1/§8 ground-truth model). The sanctioned future hand-edit path is an explicit SECURITY DEFINER RPC with superadmin check + audit-log write + explicit reason parameter, mirroring the existing `kill_switch_*` RPC pattern — not an ambient policy. Out of #4 scope but recommended follow-up: harden the five `*_no_direct_write_policy` PERMISSIVE-false tables to the same RESTRICTIVE shape (separate hygiene commit). |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 Commit 5 pre-investigation survey; MIG-057 (`sql/13_rls_deny_authenticated_writes.sql`); database-migration-ledger.md MIG-057 entry; legitimate-writer call sites `_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/universe-membership-persister.ts:57-59`, `_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/hard-exclusions-persister.ts:53-58`, `_shared/strategy-audit.ts:126-137` (`writeStrategyAuditEvent`); service-role client provenance `_shared/supabase-admin.ts:27` (`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`); sibling `*_no_direct_write_policy` PERMISSIVE-false tables (kill_switches, reconciliation_events, universe_eligibility_coverage, universe_refresh_log, longshort_reconciliation_state) recommended for RESTRICTIVE hardening in a separate commit; INC-31 / INC-34 / INC-35 (mechanism-class siblings — two-sided contracts authored without a compiler reconciling them); constitution §1 (repo as authoritative artifact) + §8 (audit-as-Tier-A-evidence); kill_switch_soft_pause / _hard_pause / _resume RPCs as the canonical SECURITY DEFINER hand-edit pattern; FP-008.4 Commit 5 closure SHA. |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 5 SHA pending CI green on closure SHA + operator out-of-band SQL-Editor apply of `sql/13_rls_deny_authenticated_writes.sql` + §22.5.1 live-DB evidence binding (`pg_policies` snapshot confirming the three over-broad INSERT policies are gone and the nine RESTRICTIVE deny policies are present). |
| **Resolution Confirmed (FP-020, 2026-06-07)** | Live-DB §22.5.1 verification (Q-1 + Q-2, 2026-06-07): `pg_policies` snapshot for `longshort_audit_logs`, `universe_membership`, `hard_exclusions` confirmed three RESTRICTIVE deny policies present per table (`_deny_authenticated_insert` / `_deny_authenticated_update` / `_deny_authenticated_delete`, all `permissive='RESTRICTIVE'`, `roles={authenticated}`, `qual='false'` / `with_check='false'`). The three over-broad PERMISSIVE INSERT policies (`longshort_audit_logs_insert_policy` `WITH CHECK (true)`, `universe_membership_operator_insert`, `hard_exclusions_operator_insert`) are absent. SELECT policies (`*_longshort_view_read`, `*_operator_read`) intact PERMISSIVE — read overlay preserved. The audit-log **forgery vector is CLOSED**; the universe_membership / hard_exclusions garbage-write vectors are CLOSED. The prior "pending operator out-of-band SQL-Editor apply + §22.5.1 evidence binding" status was stale; sql/13 had been applied months earlier. Original disposition row preserved per Constitution Rule 8; this addendum closes the §22.5.1 binding. Motivating example for DEC-041 (Pending-Evidence Dispositions Require Periodic Sweep) — the most security-critical "pending" disposition in the register was already applied; the disposition text actively misled the 2026-06-07 deep-review's initial classification of the forgery vector as still-open. Status → **Resolved (live-DB confirmed)**. |

### INC-37: `universe_membership` re-run misclassified as failure under now-functional breaker — naive `.insert()` throws 23505 on re-run, feeds false-failure signal to Commit 3.5 streak counter — reframing of pre-Phase-2 #5 "INSERT idempotency / prevent duplicates"

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-37 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 Commit 6 pre-investigation survey of `universe_membership` write path — reframed from the pre-Phase-2 investigation's #5 "INSERT idempotency / prevent duplicates" item once Commit 3.5 made the breaker functional and turned a previously-quiet failure-mode into a Tier-A safety concern) |
| **Classification** | Latent correctness defect in the persistence layer with Tier-A consequence under the post-Commit-3.5 breaker. Pre-3.5 the defect was a quiet misclassification (one failed re-run = one bad audit row); post-3.5 three legitimate re-runs in a row trip the universe-refresh circuit breaker for "we already did this work," halting the trade-gate behind a defect with no real upstream signal. |
| **Surface Story** | `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/universe-membership-persister.ts` calls `supabaseAdmin.from('universe_membership').insert(rowsToInsert)` with no `onConflict`. The table's PK is `(operator_id, ticker, as_of_date)` (MIG-050 Surface 1 Option A; `long_eligible`/`short_eligible` are columns, not key components). A second refresh for the same `as_of_date` (bootstrap re-fire, manual retry, cron edge — all expected operational paths) writes the same PK and throws `23505 unique_violation` on the first overlapping ticker. The orchestrator's outer try/catch finalizes the in-flight refresh-log row as `outcome='failed'` with a duplicate-key error message. Pre-Commit-3.5 this was a quiet annoyance (a bad audit row, no operational consequence — the breaker could not trip). Post-Commit-3.5, `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES` includes `'failed'`, so three legitimate re-runs in a row reach `recentFailures >= 3` and trip the breaker — halting the trade-gate behind a defect that has zero upstream-data correlate. The pre-Phase-2 investigation framed this as "INSERT idempotency / prevent duplicates," which was directionally right but pointed at the surface (duplicates) rather than the behavior (re-run misclassified as failure). Pre-investigation also confirmed live data has zero duplicates (1678 rows = 1678 distinct keys) — the PK has been doing its job; no cleanup migration is needed. |
| **Observation** | Conceptual hazard: **a safety mechanism's correctness depends on the signal it consumes being truthful** — Commit 3.5 made the breaker capable of tripping, which made every input to the streak counter load-bearing. A persister that throws on a legitimate operational re-pattern feeds the breaker a signal that has no upstream correlate, and the breaker cannot distinguish "real upstream failures" from "the persister doesn't tolerate our normal retry pattern." Same mechanism-class as INC-35 (D2 fail-open-on-read): in both cases the defect is a write/read path producing a `'failed'` outcome for a non-upstream event, polluting the breaker's input set. The fix posture is identical: make the path tolerate the legitimate re-pattern so the breaker sees only real failures. Sibling-shape also to INC-34: load-bearing convention without enforcement — the implicit convention that "refresh-log finalize = real outcome" assumed the persister only finalized on real upstream outcomes, but a naive `.insert()` introduces a path where finalize records `'failed'` for an operational re-pattern. **Reframing-class lesson**: the pre-Phase-2 investigation's surface-framing ("duplicates") missed the actual defect because the surface had not yet been exposed by Commit 3.5; only after the breaker became functional did the real shape ("re-run misclassified as failure") become visible. This is the second item in FP-008.4 (alongside the D2/D3/D5 cluster) where a pre-existing latent defect became consequential the moment a sibling commit made the consuming mechanism functional — a pattern worth carrying forward into future investigations: "what becomes load-bearing the moment this commit lands?" should be a standard pre-investigation lens. |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 6** — `.insert(rowsToInsert)` replaced with `.upsert(rowsToInsert, { onConflict: 'operator_id,ticker,as_of_date', ignoreDuplicates: false })` in `universe-membership-persister.ts`. `ignoreDuplicates: false` selects DO UPDATE (the supabase-js default; pinned explicitly for clarity): `long_eligible` / `short_eligible` / `quarter_label` / `refresh_id` are overwritten with the latest call's values on PK conflict. `refresh_id` becomes a last-writer reference — it points to the most recent refresh whose evaluation produced the row's current state, not the first refresh that ever wrote it; the FK to `universe_refresh_log` remains valid. No schema migration (PK already enforces uniqueness; live data confirms zero duplicates). No SQL changes. Compatible with Commit 5 RESTRICTIVE deny (persister uses `supabaseAdmin` service-role, bypasses RLS) and Commit 2 coverage write (same `(operator_id, as_of_date)` scope, already idempotent on its own PK). Tests: new persister test file `universe-membership-persister_test.ts` with 2 tests (re-run with mutated payload → DO UPDATE fires, last-writer-wins on PK including `refresh_id`; partial overlap → stale-row property pinned at the row level). Orchestrator test file gains 1 test (double `orch.run()` for the same `as_of_date` → both `completed`, no false-failure feed to the breaker). All 26 tests pass (23 baseline + 2 persister + 1 orchestrator). Anti-patterns explicitly NOT used: no DELETE-then-INSERT (would create an empty-universe window observable by the trade-gate), no `ignoreDuplicates: true` (would DO NOTHING — second call's eligibility/refresh_id changes would not land), no DELETE-stale step for tickers dropped from the eligible set between runs (see forward-looking note below), no touch to orchestrator outer try/catch or breaker streak logic (the fix is purely at the persister; making the re-run path not throw in the first place is preferable to making the orchestrator/breaker tolerate it after the fact). **Forward-looking note — out of #5 scope** (Phase 2): stale-row property under UPSERT. If a ticker is eligible in run 1 but NOT in run 2 for the same `as_of_date`, run 1's row remains in `universe_membership` with run 1's `refresh_id`. Consumers that need exact "membership at `as_of_date D` = latest refresh's eligible set" semantics must filter to the latest `refresh_id` on read. If Phase 2 wants this filter implicit, the likely shape is either (a) a `WHERE refresh_id = (SELECT refresh_id FROM universe_refresh_log WHERE as_of_date = D ORDER BY refresh_started_at DESC LIMIT 1)` predicate on all read paths (cheap, decentralized), or (b) a deliberate DELETE-stale step inside a future SECURITY DEFINER RPC that wraps the persist call and the stale-clear in a single transaction (centralized, write-side enforcement, requires a new RPC). Neither is in scope for #5; surfaced here so the consumer audit at Phase-2 entry has the property documented. **Commit-3.5 follow-on framing**: #5 is as much a 3.5 follow-on as a pre-existing item. Without #5, the now-functional breaker is fed false-failure signals by the persister's intolerance of legitimate re-runs, and the operator-visible symptom would be "the breaker trips after we retry a refresh three times, and we can't tell whether the underlying refresh is broken or the breaker is broken." Commit 6 closes that ambiguity. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 Commit 6 pre-investigation survey; `_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/universe-membership-persister.ts` (UPSERT call site + semantic comment block); `_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/universe-membership-persister_test.ts` (new — Tests 1+2); `_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator_test.ts` (Test 3 — double-run smoke); MIG-050 (`universe_membership` PK on `(operator_id, ticker, as_of_date)`); INC-34 / INC-35 (Commit-3.5 / Commit-4 siblings — the mechanism the false-failure signal was feeding); `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES` in `refresh-jobs/types.ts` (the predicate that made `'failed'` consequential); FP-008.4 Commit 6 closure SHA. |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 6 SHA pending CI green on closure SHA. |

### INC-38: `reconcile()` infrastructure-failure audit hole — loadFn throws escape STEP (a) before STEP (c) writes the `reconciliation_events` row, violating the table's own "every verify_* invocation writes one row" invariant — World-B finding from FP-008.4 Commit 7 pre-investigation (resolved at Commit 7); paired with #9 reconciliation tick-handler fail-loud

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-38 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 Commit 7 pre-investigation — first round flagged the tick handler's swallow-to-200 pattern as #9, second round reframed the reconcile()-bypass alarm as false, third round read `longshort-reconciliation-lifecycle.ts` directly and identified the actual hole at STEP (a)'s unguarded `await invoke(ts)`) |
| **Classification** | Audit-completeness defect at the canonical reconciliation surface. Infrastructure failures (loadFn throws — broker timeout, DNS, ECONNRESET, service unavailable) were the one outcome class that NEVER reached `reconciliation_events`, leaving operators no canonical record of "the verify could not run." All four other outcome classes (false_positive_within_tolerance / expected_divergence_handled / failure_handled / failure_escalated / system_bug) are recorded because they flow through STEP (c) `writeEventRow` after classification at STEP (b); only infra failures escaped before classification could happen. The lifecycle docstring stated the invariant ("every verify_* invocation writes one row regardless of outcome") that the code did not actually enforce. |
| **Surface Story** | The pre-investigation cycle: (Round 1) the tick handler's three try/catch blocks were observed swallowing every per-verifier outcome into a `results[]` array and unconditionally returning `apiSuccess(...)`, classified as the #9 phantom-success vector. (Round 2) a survey hypothesized that the verify_* wrapper layer might be bypassing `reconcile()` entirely, which would have been a much bigger audit-surface defect — but the three-file check (`verify_buying_power.ts:139-163`, `verify_position.ts:142-167`, `verify_universe_membership.ts:160-185`) confirmed the wrappers ARE thin "build spec → call `reconcile()`" shells. World 1: the audit surface is intact for completed verifies. (Round 3) the one-file read of `longshort-reconciliation-lifecycle.ts` showed that `reconcile()`'s first executable line is `const { expected, observed } = await invoke(ts);` with no surrounding try/catch — so a loadFn throw propagates out of `reconcile()` before STEP (c) writes the row. World B confirmed: infrastructure failures are the one outcome class that never reaches the canonical audit surface. The remediation reuses the existing `system_bug` outcome enum value (infrastructure failure = source unavailable = no comparison possible = structurally a system_bug) so no schema/enum migration is needed. |
| **Observation** | Mechanism-class: **a docstring-stated invariant that the code does not actually enforce is a defect** — the lifecycle's "Throws on / Does NOT throw on" section claimed loadFn rejections re-throw "outside lifecycle classification" but did not say "and the event row is skipped"; readers inferred the invariant from the table-of-rows narrative and assumed the code enforced it. This is the third FP-008.4 instance of "load-bearing convention without enforcement" (after INC-34's CHECK-vs-SET vs INC-37's PK-vs-naive-insert), reinforcing that documented invariants in heavily-rigorous surfaces require either structural enforcement (the closure landed at Commit 7) or an explicit "convention is enforced by ___" pointer in the docstring. Reframing-class lesson: the pre-investigation's middle round was the wrong-shape hypothesis (bypass at the wrapper layer) that, if not checked, would have driven a fix at the wrong site (adding parallel `strategy_audit` events at the tick handler — the audit-writer trap the lifecycle docstring explicitly bans). The discipline of "do not draft the fix until the gating question is resolved by reading source" caught it; this is the second FP-008.4 instance after the #4 RLS read-overlay survey (which found the read overlay was intentional and the real defect was on the write side, not the surface-flagged read side). |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 7.** Three-part fix paired in one commit: **(1)** `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-reconciliation-lifecycle.ts` — STEP (a)'s `await invoke(ts)` wrapped in try/catch. On throw, `writeEventRow` is invoked with `outcome='system_bug'`, `divergence={infrastructure_failure:true,error:<msg>}`, `expected_value=null`, `observed_value=null`, `notes='infrastructure_failure: <msg>'`, then the original error is re-thrown (caller's fail-loud contract preserved). Event-row-only — state surface intentionally NOT updated on this path (the throw halts the caller; rolling-window/escalation arithmetic applies only to completed verifies with actionable outcomes). Audit-write failure during closure is logged via `console.error` with both the original and write error captured, then the original error still propagates (we do not pretend we recorded what we could not).
