All 17 verifier wrappers + the cross-check inherit the closure for free — they call `reconcile()` internally. **(2)** `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts` — #9 fail-loud disposition routing via new exported pure helper `classifyTickDisposition(results)`: any `failure_escalated` / `system_bug` / `infrastructure_failure` outcome → HTTP 500 (`apiError(500, 'reconciliation_tick_escalated')`); otherwise → HTTP 200. Zero `strategy_audit` events emitted — every outcome including infrastructure failures is now in `reconciliation_events` via the closure (the HTTP status IS the disposition signal; emitting parallel strategy-audit events would hit the audit-writer trap the lifecycle docstring bans). NOT-FOR-LIVE-INVOCATION docstring guard added at handler top. **(3)** #8 forward-binding sentinel (Gate 13 — `scripts/check-verify-after-mutation.ts` + `_test.ts`, added to `.github/workflows/strong-evidence.yml`): flags any in-scope file where a `.insert(/.update(/.upsert(/.delete(` precedes a verify_* wrapper invocation (17-name allowlist) or `reconcile(` call; override-annotation escape `// gate-13-allow: post-mutation verify per §7.6 — <reason>` accepted on the verify line or in the immediately-preceding `//` comment block. File-level lexical heuristic (intentionally over-eager — per-file, not control-flow). Current HEAD baseline: 0 violations after annotating the cross-check site at `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index.ts:243` (the .insert() in the same file is `insertStart` writing a bookkeeping row to `universe_refresh_log`, not a strategy-state mutation in the §7.5 sense). #8 lifecycle-ordering itself had no behavioral defect in live code — all live mutator-with-verify sites are already pre-mutation; the sentinel is forward-binding only against Phase-5 order-execution code. **Tests:** 4 new in NEW `_shared/longshort-reconciliation-lifecycle_test.ts` (audit-hole closure behavioral test via globalThis.fetch stub + fake SUPABASE_URL/SERVICE_ROLE_KEY env; state-surface-not-updated invariant test; audit-write-failure-during-closure original-error-still-propagates test; source sentinel pinning the try/catch shape). 6 new in NEW `longshort-reconciliation-tick/index_test.ts` (five disposition cases over `classifyTickDisposition` + source-level no-swallow sentinel; closes the orphan-anchor half of DW-082 A1, now split into A1.a CLOSED + A1.b OPEN). 9 new in `scripts/check-verify-after-mutation_test.ts` (mutation→verify-wrapper flagged; mutation→reconcile() flagged; override-annotation escapes on line and preceding comment block; verify-before-mutation clean; string/comment literal ignored; scope/sanction predicates; clean-baseline assertion). **Out of scope explicitly:** no `strategy_audit`/`audit_logs` events (audit-writer trap); no 6th outcome enum value (system_bug reused); no SQL/schema change; no verifier-wrapper change; no cross-check site change beyond the gate-13-allow annotation; no DW-082 A1.b TS2322 fix (remains MUST-FIX-BEFORE-LIVE); no DW-083 Part B platform-breaker touch. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 Commit 7 pre-investigation (three-round narrative); DEC-034.1 clause (4) (six-step lifecycle); `reconciliation_events` invariant ("every verify_* invocation writes one row regardless of outcome"); `_shared/longshort-reconciliation-lifecycle.ts` (closure site); `longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts` (#9 disposition routing site); `scripts/check-verify-after-mutation.ts` (Gate 13 sentinel); `.github/workflows/strong-evidence.yml` (Gate 13 step); INC-34 / INC-37 (sibling load-bearing-convention-without-enforcement mechanism class); DW-082 A1.a (orphan-anchor half CLOSED at Commit 7) + A1.b (TS2322 half CLOSED at Commit 7.5); DW-083 Part B (platform-breaker per-class audit, untouched); FP-008.4 Commit 7 closure SHA. |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 7 SHA pending CI green on closure SHA. |

### INC-39: Cross-artifact seam — `job_registry.enabled=true` pointing at a mock-backed / NOT-FOR-LIVE handler — `longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep` enabled by MIG-045 vs `longshort-reconciliation-tick` marked NOT FOR LIVE INVOCATION at Commit 7; disarmed at FP-008.4 Commit 8 / MIG-058

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-39 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 Commit 8 pre-investigation — #11 ("tick cron-schedulability + two-invocation rule") was framed as forward-looking work; reading the source showed it was actually a latent live-activation hazard: `job_registry.enabled=true` on a `system_critical`, `*/5 * * * *` scheduled job whose handler is mock-backed and was marked `NOT FOR LIVE INVOCATION` at Commit 7. Live-DB read confirmed `enabled=true` + zero `job_executions` rows — armed but not firing only because scheduler dispatch isn't currently wired.) |
| **Classification** | Cross-artifact-seam mechanism class — the defect lived in the *relationship* between two individually-correct-looking artifacts (registry seed + MIG-045 enablement on one side; handler source + Commit 7 NOT-FOR-LIVE docstring on the other side), not within either artifact in isolation. The repo looked internally consistent — MIG-045 was well-commented ("verifier registry complete, dispatch function exists, can run end-to-end"), the handler's guard was present, Commit 7's hardening was complete — but the *seam* between them encoded a phantom-reconciliation hazard per CROSSWIND §2 axiom 2 (internal derivatives treated as external primes) + §20 (phantom decision throughput). Latent rather than active because pg_cron dispatch wiring is absent in the current environment; the protection was an absence, not a deliberate gate. Same mechanism class as INC-36 (audit-log forgery — three-way seam between RLS `WITH CHECK (true)` + RESTRICTIVE-deny convention + service-role bypass) and INC-37 (PK-vs-naive-insert — seam between `.insert()` semantics + ON CONFLICT idempotency convention + circuit-breaker streak counter). |
| **Surface Story** | Pre-investigation for FP-008.4 Commit 8 / #11. Independent first-pass read of `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts` source (post Commit 7) noted the migration filename `20260522110000_step_6_3d_activate_reconciliation_periodic_sweep.sql` (MIG-045) and flagged the tension between "activate the reconciliation periodic sweep" and Commit 7's NOT-FOR-LIVE guard. Direct read of MIG-045 confirmed `UPDATE job_registry SET enabled = true WHERE id = 'longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep';`. Cross-reference of `_shared/reconciliation_jobs_seed` (`step_6_2_reconciliation_jobs_seed.sql`) confirmed the seed has `schedule='*/5 * * * *'`, `trigger_type='scheduled'`, `class='system_critical'`, `execution_guarantee='exactly_once'`, `concurrency_policy='forbid'`. Live-DB read (operator-run): `SELECT id, enabled, status, schedule, trigger_type, updated_at FROM job_registry WHERE id = 'longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep';` returned `enabled=true`, `status=registered`, `schedule='*/5 * * * *'`, `updated_at=2026-05-24 03:57:55.969625+00`. Live-DB read: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM job_executions WHERE job_id = 'longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep';` returned zero — over ~11 days since MIG-045's `updated_at`, the scheduler has never picked up this enabled job (pg_cron dispatch not currently wired in this environment). State assessed as latent loaded gun: `enabled=true` against a mock/NOT-FOR-LIVE handler, disarmed only by absence of dispatch wiring, would fire every 5 minutes the moment dispatch is connected — exactly the §2/§20 phantom-throughput hazard. |
| **Observation** | Mechanism-class: **a financial-safety gate that depends on an absence (no dispatch wiring) is not a gate** — it converts to active hazard the instant the absence is filled, which is *exactly* what bringing pg_cron + job-executor live will do. Asymmetric review surface: MIG-045's reviewer would have needed to read the handler source (`longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts`) to catch the contradiction; Commit 7's handler reviewer would have needed to read the registry seed + MIG-045 to catch it; neither review surface naturally crosses the seam. This is the generalizable lesson — load-bearing cross-artifact invariants ("`enabled=true` ⇒ handler is live-ready") that no single-file review can verify need either structural enforcement or a mechanical sentinel that scans across the seam. Same lesson surface as INC-36 (RLS additive defeat — single-table policy review couldn't catch the OR-combination defeat across all PERMISSIVE policies) and INC-37 (idempotency convention — single-call-site review couldn't catch that the streak counter consumes the duplicate-PK failure signal). The generalizable mitigation is a Gate-15-class sentinel scanning for any `enabled=true` + `trigger_type='scheduled'` job in `job_registry` seed/enable migrations whose target handler source contains `NOT FOR LIVE INVOCATION` or a mock-fetcher marker (`MOCK_*_FETCHER`, etc.) — to be authored in #11's second commit alongside the two-invocation liveness rule (the two pair naturally: the liveness rule catches "fires but reconciles nothing real" at runtime; the sentinel catches "armed against a non-live handler" at CI). |
| **Disposition** | **Disarm landed at FP-008.4 Commit 8 / MIG-058.** Idempotent single-row UPDATE: `enabled=false` for `longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep` in `public.job_registry`, with `WHERE enabled = true` guard + `DO $$ ... $$` end-state assertion. Live verified post-apply: `enabled=false`, all other registry fields preserved. MIG-045 NOT deleted (forward-only history per D2); MIG-058 supersedes its enablement only. Handler docstring updated to reference MIG-058 explicitly. **Sentinel deferred to Commit 8 second commit** — the cross-artifact `enabled=true`-vs-NOT-FOR-LIVE Gate-15 scanner and the two-invocation liveness rule pair naturally; out of scope for the disarm-only commit per scope discipline. **Re-enable conditions** (all three required, via a future migration explicitly citing MIG-058): (1) real broker fetchers (FP-006 sub-step 6.7 — Alpaca paper) replacing `MOCK_BP_FETCHER` / `MOCK_POSITION_FETCHER`; (2) two-invocation liveness rule live (#11 second commit); (3) explicit re-enable migration confirming (1)+(2). |
| **Cross-references** | MIG-045 (`supabase/migrations/20260522110000_step_6_3d_activate_reconciliation_periodic_sweep.sql`) — the premature enablement superseded; MIG-058 (`supabase/migrations/20260604090759_*.sql`) — the disarm; `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-tick/index.ts` (handler, mock fetchers, NOT-FOR-LIVE docstring, Commit 7 disposition routing); `supabase/functions/_shared/reconciliation_jobs_seed` (`step_6_2_reconciliation_jobs_seed.sql`) — `*/5 * * * *` schedule + `system_critical` class origin; FP-008.4 Commit 7 (#9 fail-loud + reconcile() audit-hole closure + NOT-FOR-LIVE docstring guard); FP-006 sub-step 6.7 (Alpaca paper — real broker fetcher integration, re-enable prerequisite); FP-008.4 Commit 8 pending second commit (#11 two-invocation liveness rule + cross-artifact Gate-15 sentinel); INC-36 + INC-37 (sibling cross-artifact-seam mechanism class instances). |
| **Status** | Disarm resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 8 / MIG-058. Cross-artifact-seam mechanism pattern recorded for the upcoming Gate-15 sentinel (Commit 8 second commit). |

### INC-40: `reconciliation_events` provenance gap — a "real broker, zero positions" event row was data-indistinguishable from a "mock fetcher, returned null" event row; the two-invocation liveness rule (FP-008.4 #11) was unbuildable as a pure SQL query and required a schema column

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-40 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 Commit 9 pre-investigation survey — surfaced when scoping the two-invocation liveness rule and discovering that `reconciliation_events` carried no field distinguishing mock-fetcher output from real-broker output) |
| **Classification** | Tier-A latent — affects re-enablement safety of `longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep` (system_critical job). Disarmed at MIG-058; re-enable gated on this rule per MIG-058's documented conditions. |
| **Surface Story** | The pre-investigation evaluated three candidate definitions of "real verify row" for the liveness predicate: (i) non-zero / non-null result — REJECTED, because `MOCK_POSITION_FETCHER.fetchPosition → null` is indistinguishable from a real broker's "no position open" return, and `MOCK_BP_FETCHER → available_bp: 100000` is a valid live value; flat broker accounts produce mock-like output. (ii) constant-signature detection — REJECTED, brittle (couples detector to mock magic numbers; breaks when mocks change). (iii) provenance-flagged — chosen. The handler knows its own fetcher source at construction time (mock vs live is a property of which fetcher the dispatcher wired, not derived data); the rule's `fetcher_source = 'live'` predicate makes the distinction first-class in the audit surface. The defect class this prevents: a future re-enable that silently degrades to mock-backed dispatch (e.g., a wiring regression at 6.7 that reverts one verifier to its mock fetcher) would otherwise look like "the sweep is running, events are flowing" while being phantom — the same hazard MIG-058 disarmed at the registry layer, but now caught at the data layer if the registry is later re-armed. |
| **Observation** | Mechanism-class sibling to INC-31 / INC-34 / INC-35 / INC-36 — "two sides of a contract authored without a compiler reconciling them." Here the two sides were "what the dispatcher knows" (fetcher provenance — known at module-load time, encoded only in import statements) and "what the audit surface can answer" (which historically had no provenance field). The schema change makes provenance a typed, persisted property; the lifecycle's required `fetcher_source: FetcherSource` parameter makes missing-it a compile-time error (no column default by design — see MIG-059 DEVIATION block). Honest-backfill discipline applied: pre-MIG-059 rows are backfilled to `'unknown'`, NOT retroactively claimed as `'mock'` or `'live'`, mirroring INC-36's epistemic-boundary stance on pre-MIG-057 audit evidence (don't claim a property the historical evidence cannot prove). |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 9 / MIG-059.** Added `reconciliation_events.fetcher_source text NOT NULL CHECK IN ('mock','live','replay','unknown')` (three-step add — nullable → backfill `'unknown'` → NOT NULL + CHECK, deliberately without a default). Lifecycle `reconcile()` gains required `fetcher_source: FetcherSource` parameter threaded to both the normal write and the Commit-7 infrastructure-failure catch write. All 17 verifier wrappers updated to thread the parameter. Tick handler tags all three dispatches `'mock'`; universe quarterly-refresh cross-check tags `'live'`. New `liveness_check` value added to `VerifyCallName` union (DW-069 precedent: non-verify_* reconcile identifier). |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 Commit 9 closure SHA (pending); MIG-058 (the disarm this provenance gap was preventing safe re-enable of); MIG-059 (the resolution); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-reconciliation-types.ts` (FetcherSource type + union widening); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-reconciliation-lifecycle.ts` (required parameter); `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check/index.ts` (the consumer); INC-31 / INC-34 / INC-35 / INC-36 (mechanism-class siblings); INC-36 honest-backfill discipline. |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 9 SHA (pending CI green + §22.5.1 live-DB evidence binding: `information_schema.columns` snapshot confirming `NOT NULL` + no default + four-value CHECK + all existing rows backfilled to `'unknown'`). |

### INC-41: Liveness-check STOP ladder rung (b) — direct alert emission omitted because the repo's alerting model is `system_metrics` push + `alert_configs` threshold evaluated by the job-alert-evaluation job, NOT a synchronous emit helper; deferred as DW-086

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-41 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 Commit 9 pre-investigation survey — surfaced when scoping the three-rung STOP ladder for the two-invocation liveness rule) |
| **Classification** | Tier-B operational gap — the 2-rung ladder shipped at Commit 9 (`system_bug` event + `enabled=false` halt) covers the auditable-trail + safe-state requirements; the missing rung is paging/alerting velocity. The HTTP 500 the handler returns is the operational signal in the interim (cron-level retry + monitoring infra fires on the 5xx). |
| **Surface Story** | The originally-scoped 3-rung ladder was (a) `reconciliation_events` system_bug row via `reconcile()`, (b) alert emission via shared helper, (c) `job_registry.enabled=false` halt. When scoping (b), the survey found the repo's alerting architecture is two-phase: callers push metrics to `system_metrics`; `alert_configs` rows define thresholds + comparisons + cooldowns; a separate job-alert-evaluation job (Phase 3 monitoring infra) reads metrics + configs and writes `alert_history` rows when thresholds breach. There is no shared `emitAlert()` helper that synchronously writes `alert_history` from a caller (and adding one would bypass the cooldown/configuration model — wrong shape). The correct future shape is a separate commit that (i) pushes a `liveness_check.stop` metric to `system_metrics` from this job and (ii) configures an `alert_configs` row with appropriate severity + threshold. Doing that here would conflate "build the rule" with "extend the alerting pipeline" — separate concerns, separate commits. |
| **Observation** | The 2-rung ladder is strictly safer than the original 3-rung in one dimension: rung (c) immediately removes the unsafe condition (the sweep can no longer fire), so the paging gap is "operators discover the halt via cron-level 5xx monitoring instead of via a paged alert" — bounded delay, not an unsafe state. Documenting the gap here (and as DW-086) preserves the analysis so the future commit lands the correct shape, not an ad-hoc emit helper. |
| **Disposition** | Deferred to DW-086 (push `liveness_check.stop` metric to `system_metrics` + create `alert_configs` row). The 2-rung ladder remains the canonical halt mechanism; rung (b) is paging velocity, not safety. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 Commit 9 closure SHA (pending); `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check/index.ts` (the 2-rung shipped); `system_metrics` + `alert_configs` + `alert_history` (the canonical pipeline); DW-086 (the deferred rung-(b) commit). |
| **Status** | Open — Tier-B gap; tracked at DW-086; not a hard prerequisite to the periodic-sweep re-enable (the re-enable conditions per MIG-058 are real fetchers + liveness rule + explicit re-enable migration; alerting velocity is operationally desirable but not safety-blocking given the registry-disarm rung). |

### INC-42: Commit 9 liveness-check introduced three Gate-2 sentinel hits — two real defects (wall-clock leak + `?? 0` sentinel-coercion on the liveness count), one false-positive (verify-after-mutation on `reconcile()`-encapsulated disarm); resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 9.5

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-42 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 Commit 9 post-commit Gate-2 sentinel run — caught before merge) |
| **Classification** | Tier-A — two real defects on a financial-path safety mechanism (the periodic-sweep liveness rule itself). Both root-fixed; one false-positive annotated. |
| **Surface Story** | Commit 9's new `longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check/index.ts` tripped three sentinels: (1) `check-wall-clock` flagged `updated_at: new Date().toISOString()` inside `failure_action`'s registry-disarm — a wall-clock read in a financial-path lifecycle that already had an injected clock threaded through `reconcile()` to `ctx.ts`. Banned per DEC-034 clause (4). (2) `check-sentinel-patterns` flagged `live_periodic_sweep_event_count: count ?? 0` on the result of the Supabase `count` query — same fail-direction class as INC-33 / Commit-4 D2: a `null` count (read error / unexpected result) would be silently coerced to `0`, which the liveness predicate would then read as "two consecutive empty windows" → false STOP, halting the sweep on infrastructure noise rather than on real livelessness. (3) `check-verify-after-mutation` (the Gate-13 sentinel built in Commit 7) flagged the `.update({enabled:false})` in `failure_action` paired with the later `await reconcile(...)` call — a false positive: the mutation is *inside* `reconcile()`'s own `failure_action` (STEP e of the lifecycle), executed by `reconcile()` as its halt response, not by a caller mutating financial state before verifying. CROSSWIND §7.5/§7.6's pre-mutation-gate invariant protects positions/orders/lots — not a registry-disarm rung that IS the failure response. |
| **Observation** | Meta-point worth recording: the Gate-13 sentinel we built in Commit 7 caught a pattern in Commit 9's *own* new code on its first run after introduction. The recurrence-prevention is working on subsequent commits as designed — sentinels are not a one-time gate but a continuous floor. The `?? 0` hit is the most instructive: the same fail-direction antipattern (silent zero-coercion on a safety-mechanism count) re-emerged in different code five commits after its first surfacing (INC-33/35), which argues that the sentinel-pattern ban is doing its job — without it, this would have shipped as a latent false-STOP. |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 9.5.** (a) Wall-clock fix: `failure_action`'s `_ctx` widened to `ctx`; `updated_at` now uses `ctx.ts.toISOString()` (the injected clock threaded through `reconcile()`). (b) Sentinel-coercion fix: explicit fail-closed branch — if the count query returns `null`/`undefined`, throw with a descriptive message distinguishing infrastructure failure from a genuine zero; only `count === 0` from a successful query is treated as livelessness. (c) Verify-after-mutation annotation: `// gate-13-allow:` block placed immediately before the `await reconcile(...)` call, citing reconcile()-encapsulated failure_action shape (sibling to Commit 7's cross-check override). No SQL, no live-DB, no other file touched. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 Commit 9.5 closure SHA (pending); `supabase/functions/longshort-reconciliation-liveness-check/index.ts` (all three changes); INC-33 / INC-35 / Commit-4 D2 (fail-direction-class siblings for the `?? 0` fix); Commit 7 cross-check `gate-13-allow:` (annotation precedent); DEC-034 clauses (2) + (4) (banned-pattern authority). |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 9.5 SHA (pending CI green: Gate 2 green, Gate 11 unchanged 246/0). |

### INC-43: `job_registry.enabled` state drift between migration tree and live DB for the universe jobs (`quarterly_refresh` + 4× `hard_exclusion_refresh_*`) — seed migrations ship them `enabled=false` (ACT-109 / sub-step 8.13 pending), live DB has them `enabled=true`; surfaced during FP-008.4 Commit 10 Gate-15 sentinel parser validation; NOT introduced by Commit 10

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-43 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 Commit 10 live-DB verification — Gate-15 sentinel's migration-replay parser resolved the 5 universe jobs to `enabled=false` (matching seed migrations 20260525093303 + 20260525103115, both of which explicitly seed `enabled=false` and document `Ships enabled=false; activated at sub-step 8.13` / `ACT-109`); live `SELECT id, enabled FROM job_registry` returned `enabled=true` for `quarterly_refresh` + all 4 hard-exclusion rows). |
| **Classification** | Tier-B governance/audit-trail gap — the migration tree is supposed to be the single source of truth for `job_registry` state per §22.5.1 (every state-change to a financial-system-critical job is a migration with an auditable file). The drift indicates an operator-initiated `enabled=true` flip outside the migration tree (likely via the Supabase dashboard or an ad-hoc SQL run); there is no migration file recording who, when, or why. This is the mechanism-class sibling to INC-39 (cross-artifact-seam) — only here the seam is between "migration tree says X" and "live DB says Y", with no artifact reconciling them. |
| **Surface Story** | Commit 10's Gate-15 sentinel chronologically replays every migration's `INSERT`/`UPDATE` against `public.job_registry` to derive the final state of each job. The parser correctly handled the MIG-044→MIG-045→MIG-058 sweep-enable sequence (3 overlays), but produced `enabled=false` for `longshort.universe.quarterly_refresh` and the 4 `hard_exclusion_refresh_3_3*` rows, while the live DB returns `enabled=true` for all 5. Grepping every migration in `supabase/migrations/*.sql` for these ids found ONLY the seed `INSERT`s (both shipping `enabled=false` per the seed-migration comments — `quarterly_refresh`: "Ships enabled=false; activated at sub-step 8.13"; hard-exclusion-refresh seeds explicitly cite `ACT-109` for activation). No `UPDATE public.job_registry SET enabled = true WHERE id LIKE '%universe%'` exists in the migration tree. The parser is correct; the migration tree is incomplete. |
| **Observation** | Two implications, both bounded: (1) Gate-15 itself is unaffected at the Commit 10 baseline — the universe handlers (`longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index.ts` + `longshort-universe-hard-exclusion-refresh/index.ts`) are clean of `NOT FOR LIVE INVOCATION` / `MOCK_*_FETCHER` markers (verified at the pre-investigation survey), so even if Gate-15 read them as enabled=true (matching live), Gate-15 would still pass (P1 doesn't trip; P2 doesn't trip because `handler_path` is populated). The sentinel's correctness as a recurrence-prevention does not depend on which side of the drift it sees. (2) The audit-trail gap is a real governance finding: a future operator looking at the migration tree to answer "when did the universe jobs activate?" gets no answer; the live state is unrecorded. Mechanism-class: same shape as INC-39 (the relationship between two artifacts is unmanaged). The proper fix is a small reconciliation migration that explicitly `UPDATE`s the 5 rows to `enabled=true` with a comment block documenting (i) the date of the original operator flip (per `updated_at`), (ii) the authority (ACT-109 / sub-step 8.13 if that was the trigger; or a backfill-acknowledgment if it predated activation authority), and (iii) the discovery context (this INC). That migration also re-aligns the Gate-15 sentinel's migration-derived state with the live DB — eliminating the drift as a class. |
| **Disposition** | **Open — deferred to a focused reconciliation commit (not in Commit 10's scope).** Rationale: Commit 10 is Tier-B CI-hygiene + scope-locked to the Gate-15 sentinel + `handler_path` column; absorbing a state-reconciliation migration would conflate two governance concerns (sentinel-introduction vs migration-tree-reconciliation) and re-introduce the blast-radius coupling Commits 8 / 9 / 10 were explicitly split to avoid. Commit 10's Gate-15 baseline remains CLEAN regardless of which side of the drift the parser sees (the universe handlers are marker-free). The reconciliation migration is registered as DW-087. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 Commit 10 closure SHA (pending); DW-087 closure SHA (pending); MIG-062 (the reconciliation migration that recorded the 2026-06-04 10:16:24Z activation per ACT-109 / sub-step 8.13 authority); `supabase/migrations/20260525093303_*.sql` (`quarterly_refresh` seed — explicit `enabled=false` + "activated at sub-step 8.13" comment); `supabase/migrations/20260525103115_*.sql` (hard-exclusion-refresh seeds — explicit `enabled=false` + `ACT-109` activation reference); INC-39 (mechanism-class sibling — unmanaged relationship between two individually-correct artifacts); MIG-058 (the §22.5.1 binding-evidence discipline this finding identifies a gap in); `scripts/check-handler-liveness-markers.ts` (the parser whose correctness surfaced the drift); DW-087 (the deferred reconciliation migration, closed at MIG-062). |
| **Disposition addendum** | **Resolved at MIG-062.** The reconciliation migration recorded the 2026-06-04 10:16:24Z activation event per ACT-109 / sub-step 8.13 authority — using the live `updated_at` as the actual flip timestamp in the comment block, with the `AND enabled = true` WHERE-clause guard ensuring the migration RECORDS the existing state rather than making it happen, and a `DO $$ … RAISE EXCEPTION $$` post-update sanity check catching any drift between INC-43 capture and apply. The Gate-15 sentinel's chronological replay now sees the same `enabled=true` state for these 5 rows as the live DB, eliminating the drift as a class. The seam (migration-tree-vs-live-DB) is now structurally closed for the universe jobs. |
| **Status** | **CLOSED at MIG-062** (DW-087 closure pass, 2026-06-05). Was: Tier-B; tracked at DW-087; not safety-blocking (Gate-15 clean either way; the universe handlers carry no marker patterns); not introduced by Commit 10 (the drift predates Commit 10's work). |

### INC-44: `job_registry` → handler-file linkage was convention-only across three non-uniform conventions plus a script-dispatched job before MIG-060 — recorded as the rationale for the `handler_path` column and Gate-15's secondary P2 (NULL handler) rule

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-44 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 Commit 10 pre-investigation survey — surfaced when scoping how the Gate-15 sentinel would JOIN registry rows to handler files; discovered the linkage was implicit across three distinct naming conventions with no enforcement). |
| **Classification** | Tier-B mechanism-class finding (resolved at MIG-060) — the three convention shapes were: (i) platform `<id> → job-<id-with-underscores-dashed>` (e.g., `health_check → job-health-check`); (ii) longshort.x → `longshort-<x>` with non-clean suffixes (`longshort.reconciliation_periodic_sweep → longshort-reconciliation-tick`); (iii) universe 4-rows-share-1-handler via `?rule=` query-param dispatch (`hard_exclusion_refresh_3_3a/b/c/e → longshort-universe-hard-exclusion-refresh`). Plus the script-dispatched `replay_chain` (no edge handler at all). No grep, no compile-time check, and no schema constraint reconciled "what the registry says" with "what file exists on disk" — the linkage was a reader's-mental-model contract. |
| **Surface Story** | A new operator adding a job would have to (a) read three existing seeds to infer which convention applies, (b) guess at the convention break for `tick` vs `liveness-check` (both `longshort.reconciliation_*` but one drops `reconciliation` and adds `tick`, the other keeps `reconciliation` and adds `liveness-check`), and (c) discover the `?rule=` shared-handler pattern only by reading the universe seed's description prose. The Gate-15 sentinel needs an authoritative mapping to JOIN on; deriving it convention-by-convention would re-encode the unenforced convention into the sentinel and break the moment a new shape appeared. |
| **Observation** | The right shape was a typed schema column (the value IS a path; columnize it). MIG-060's `handler_path text CHECK (... ~ '^supabase/functions/[a-z0-9-]+/index\.ts$')` makes the linkage queryable, CHECK-enforceable, and self-documenting. The secondary Gate-15 rule (P2: `enabled=true + trigger_type='scheduled' + handler_path IS NULL` → violation) catches the adjacent seam (an enabled scheduled job that dispatches to nothing — registry-completeness defect); it's clean at baseline because `replay_chain` + control rows are all `trigger_type='manual'`. The `replay_chain` row is intentionally NULL because it has no edge-function handler — it's dispatched by `scripts/replay-pass.ts` / `scripts/replay-run.ts`; representing that as NULL is honest about the registry's scope (NOT "we forgot a handler"). |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 10 / MIG-060.** Column added, all 12 real jobs backfilled (4 universe rows share 1 path), control rows + replay_chain left NULL. Gate-15 P2 codifies "an enabled scheduled job with no handler_path is a defect" so future regressions can't reintroduce the gap silently. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 Commit 10 closure SHA (pending); MIG-060 (the resolution); `scripts/check-handler-liveness-markers.ts` (the consumer + P2 rule); INC-39 (the parent defect class this column + sentinel pair prevent recurrence of); DW-084 (this commit closes it). |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 Commit 10 SHA (pending CI green + §22.5.1 live-DB evidence: 18-row `SELECT` confirms backfill matches spec). |

### INC-45: FP-008.4 #13 framing correction — Wikipedia parser bounds-assertion was scoped as a "close the parser-sanity gap" item, but the survey established it is defense-in-depth on top of an already-loud cross-check; the actual gaps closed were a silent per-row-skip failure mode and the orphan test file

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-45 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 #13 pre-investigation survey — the survey looked for a "live financial gap" the bounds assertion would close and found the cross-check already escalates the catastrophic case). |
| **Classification** | Tier-B framing correction + two concrete gaps closed at the same commit (silent per-row-skip forensic blind spot + orphan test file). NOT a live financial defect. |
| **Surface Story** | (1) `parseWikipediaConstituents` (`supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/constituent-ingestion/wikipedia-constituent-fetcher.ts`) was structurally defensive (four `ConstituentFetchError` throws for no-wikitable / no-rows / no-`<th>` / missing-Symbol-column) but had no count-bounds assertion. A structurally-valid parse returning an implausible count (e.g. 40 tickers for sp500 because a Wikipedia layout edit caused most rows to fail the per-row ticker regex) returned the short list with no error. (2) The three per-row `continue` paths (ragged-row, empty-ticker, regex-rejected) were uncounted and silent — there was no on-error breadcrumb to which path drained the list. (3) The fetcher was the orphan of the `constituent-ingestion/` directory: `polygon-constituent-fetcher_test.ts` and `ishares-constituent-fetcher_test.ts` existed; `wikipedia-constituent-fetcher_test.ts` did not. |
| **Framing Correction** | The pre-Phase-2 prompt framed #13 as closing a live gap, but the cross-check (`cross-check-spec.ts` `classify_outcome`, `SURFACE_2_THRESHOLDS.SAFETY_CEILING_SYM_DIFF = 100`) already escalates the 40-vs-500 case to `system_bug` and aborts the refresh — AND Wikipedia is observation-only (`secondary_tickers`); the universe feeds from the operator-seeded primary (`SeededMembershipFetcher`), so a short Wikipedia list cannot silently shrink the universe. The bounds assertion is therefore defense-in-depth: an earlier, more diagnosable fail-fast at the parse boundary ("returned 40, expected 490–520, layout likely changed; skipped 460: 0 ragged, 0 empty, 460 regex-rejected") rather than a downstream vague signal ("jaccard 0.08, system_bug aborted"). Worth doing because the threat (Wikipedia changing its table markup) is genuinely likely over time, and the diagnostic locality + protection for any non-cross-check call site materially improves operability. |
| **Observation** | Defense-in-depth on external-data-parsing paths is the right default when (a) the source's contract is unenforced (HTML scrape, not a typed API), (b) the failure mode is silent (per-row `continue`, no counter), and (c) the downstream backstop's error is diagnostically distant from the actual cause. The skip-counters are a zero-infrastructure forensic improvement (three local ints folded into the throw message) — no new telemetry surface needed, no metrics emit, no orchestrator change. |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved at FP-008.4 #13.** Added `SANITY_BOUNDS: Record<IndexId, {min, max}>` (`sp500: [490, 520]`, `sp400: [385, 415]`; `Record<IndexId, …>` forces exhaustive coverage so a future index addition is a compile-time error without bounds), skip-counters at the three `continue` points, in-parser bounds throw with count + bounds + skip breakdown + "layout likely changed" diagnostic hint, and a 17-case test file (positive in-bounds + bounds boundary + bounds violations + structural negatives + skip-counting behavior + fetcher-wrapper propagation). No change to the cross-check, no change to the universe path, no new telemetry infrastructure. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 #13 closure SHA (pending); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/constituent-ingestion/wikipedia-constituent-fetcher.ts` (the assertion); `…/wikipedia-constituent-fetcher_test.ts` (orphan-closure test file, 17 cases); `…/cross-check-spec.ts` `SURFACE_2_THRESHOLDS.SAFETY_CEILING_SYM_DIFF` (the existing backstop that justified the "defense-in-depth not live gap" framing). |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 #13 SHA (pending CI green; 17/17 Deno tests passing locally). |

### INC-46: Universe staleness was unsurfaced across all three universe UI surfaces (LongShortDashboard / UniverseMembershipPage / UniverseRefreshHistoryPage); the quarter-derivation predicate is now triplicated; the three universe UI pages remain test-orphans

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-46 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 #16 pre-investigation survey). |
| **Classification** | Three sub-findings bundled under #16's resolution: (a) genuine UI gap — the universe surfaces displayed `as_of_date` + `quarter_label` as raw data with no recency interpretation; (b) Tier-B consolidation debt — the "what quarter is this date in" predicate now exists in three places (`supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts::quarterLabelFor`, `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/shared/trading-days.ts::quarterOf`, and the new frontend `src/features/longshort/utils/universe-staleness.ts::currentQuarter`); (c) test-orphan note — `LongShortDashboard.tsx`, `UniverseMembershipPage.tsx`, and `UniverseRefreshHistoryPage.tsx` have no `*.test.tsx` files. |
| **Surface Story** | (a) An operator looking at `/trading/longshort` or `/trading/longshort/universe` could read `as-of 2026-04-01` and not know whether that was current (Q2 2026, freshly seeded), aging (early-quarter, awaiting the new refresh), overdue (well into the new quarter and the re-seed missed), or stale (multiple quarters behind — a clearly-missed re-seed loop). The circuit-breaker badge on the history page surfaces individual refresh outcomes but not "where are we now" cumulative state. (b) The frontend cannot import the Deno `_shared` module (different runtime/module system), so duplicating `quarterOf` was the in-scope option; the alternative (a backend RPC / view returning `is_stale`) was rejected as plumbing-heavy for a pure presentation concern. The orchestrator's inline `quarterLabelFor` (which predates `_shared/trading-days.ts::quarterOf`) is the older duplication — independent of #16. (c) Page-render test coverage was already absent (#13 closed the same orphan pattern for the Wikipedia fetcher; the universe UI pages are the next instance). |
| **Observation** | (a) Resolved at #16 — pure-frontend `universe-staleness.ts` util computes the 5-state staleness signal (`bootstrapping` / `current` / `aging` / `overdue` / `stale`) with a refresh-outcome cross-reference cause (`refresh_failing` for `circuit_breaker_open`/`failed`/`partial` outcomes vs `no_recent_attempt` for `completed` of a prior quarter or no row at all). Badge surfaces on the dashboard (primary) + membership page header (secondary), NOT the history page (which answers "what happened" not "where are we"). (b) Triplication is logged here for a future consolidation pass — possible shapes: (i) a shared `@longshort/universe-quarter` TS package that both runtimes import, (ii) a build-step extraction from the frontend file into the backend, or (iii) a backend-computed staleness RPC that removes the need for the frontend copy. Out of #16's pure-frontend scope. (c) Page-render test coverage closure is out of #16 — the testable unit (`universe-staleness.ts`) is covered (Vitest, all 5 states + cause cross-ref + quarter-label parse + boundary math); a future commit can close the page-render orphan gap alongside or independent of FP-008.4. |
| **Disposition** | (a) **Resolved at FP-008.4 #16.** (b) **Open** — consolidation debt, no urgency (the duplication is 4 lines per copy, with consistency comments at each site). (c) **Open** — page-render test orphans, same class as the pre-#13 Wikipedia fetcher gap; logged for future closure. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 #16 closure SHA (pending); `src/features/longshort/utils/universe-staleness.ts` (the new util); `…/universe-staleness.test.ts` (unit tests); `src/features/longshort/components/LongShortDashboard.tsx` + `src/pages/trading/longshort/UniverseMembershipPage.tsx` (the two surfaces decorated); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts::quarterLabelFor` + `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/shared/trading-days.ts::quarterOf` (the two backend predicate copies). |
| **Status** | Sub-finding (a) resolved at FP-008.4 #16 SHA (pending CI green); sub-findings (b) + (c) open as Tier-B follow-ups. |

### INC-47: Four ad-hoc outcome renderers with three different mappings for the same reconciliation enum; the cross-check card rendered `system_bug` + `failure_escalated` as amber-warning (most-severe display-severity misalignment); dead fallback branch + raw snake_case labels + a design-token violation on the cross-check card

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-47 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 #17 pre-investigation survey). |
| **Classification** | Five sub-findings bundled under #17's resolution: (a) the **most-severe** UI misalignment — `LongShortDashboard.tsx` cross-check card (~L328) used a binary clean/amber `Badge`, rendering `system_bug` (CROSSWIND §11.0.11 ABORT-class; the Commit-7 infra-failure outcome and Commit-9 liveness-rule STOP outcome) and `failure_escalated` (also ABORT-class) with the same amber colour as `failure_handled` (recoverable, standard procedure); an operator could not visually distinguish a catastrophic outcome from a handled noise event. (b) Mapping inconsistency — the same `reconciliation_outcome` enum rendered three different ways on three surfaces (cross-check card binary, dashboard events list 3-way, `ReconciliationEventsPage` 3-way), with no shared helper. (c) Dead fallback branch — `ReconciliationEventsPage.tsx::outcomeBadge` carried a `return <Badge variant="outline">{outcome}</Badge>` fallback that was unreachable (all 5 enum values are covered by the prior branches) and would have rendered a raw enum string if it ever fired. (d) Raw labels — three of four surfaces rendered the raw snake_case enum string (`false_positive_within_tolerance` verbatim) as operator-facing badge text, while `UniverseRefreshHistoryPage` used human labels (`"Circuit Open"`, `"Completed"`) — an inconsistency that left the same enum looking like two different things across pages. (e) Design-token violation — the cross-check card used custom `bg-warning/10 text-warning border-warning/20` Tailwind classes inline, bypassing the design-system semantic-token rule that all colour usage routes through `StatusBadge` or the design-token-driven `Badge` variants. |
| **Surface Story** | (a) Pre-#17, an operator scanning `/trading/longshort` saw the cross-check card amber-badged for every non-clean outcome — the cross-check could trip on a Wikipedia-vs-Polygon catastrophic sym-diff (Surface 2 Option γ ceiling → `system_bug` → refresh aborted) and the dashboard would render that catastrophe identically to a routine `failure_handled` divergence resolved by standard manual override. The escalation severity lived in `failure_action` + the reconciliation runbook; the UI did not reflect it. (b)+(d) The mapping/label drift was a textbook copy-paste history: each surface implemented its own outcome→badge function, and they drifted as the enum grew (`system_bug` added at Commit 7; `circuit_breaker_open` added at Commit 3) without being audited across all renderers. (c) The dead fallback was a defensive-programming relic from before the enum was exhaustively typed in the row schema; harmless but a tell that the renderer was not exhaustively reasoned about. (e) The custom colour classes pre-date the design-system tokenisation pass; they happened to land on the surface that also had the worst severity-collapse, doubling the regression severity. |
| **Observation** | (a)+(b)+(d)+(e) Resolved at #17 — new `src/features/longshort/utils/outcome-display.ts` exposes exhaustive `Record<ReconciliationOutcome, {label; severity}>` + `Record<RefreshOutcomeKey, {label; severity}>` maps with backend-derived severity tiers (CROSSWIND §11.0.10/11 + DEC-038 clause (2) + FP-008.4 Commit 3.5 `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES`), and severity→`StatusBadge`-status / severity→`Badge`-variant adapters. All four surfaces (cross-check card, dashboard events list, `ReconciliationEventsPage`, `UniverseRefreshHistoryPage`) now route through the helper: human labels everywhere, severity faithful to the backend everywhere, design-token compliance on the cross-check card via `StatusBadge`. Variant adapter intentionally maps `clean → outline` (not `default`) so clean outcomes read calm rather than high-contrast attention-grabbing — fixes a secondary survey finding that the events list's `clean → default` was visually noisy. (c) Resolved at #17 — the exhaustive `Record<…>` helper guarantees every enum value is handled; the dead fallback was deleted, and a future enum addition would surface as a TypeScript compile error rather than a silent fallthrough (same pattern as #13's `SANITY_BOUNDS` and #16's `STALENESS_BADGE_*` maps). Vitest unit tests (`outcome-display.test.ts`, 16 cases) include dedicated regression assertions: `system_bug → severe` and `circuit_breaker_open → severe` are pinned with explicit `not.toBe('clean')` / `not.toBe('handled')` / `not.toBe('neutral')` guards so the cross-check card's pre-#17 severity-collapse bug can never recur silently. Page-render test orphans (LongShortDashboard / ReconciliationEventsPage / UniverseRefreshHistoryPage) remain — same class as #16's open sub-finding (c); out of #17's pure-presentation scope. |
| **Disposition** | (a)+(b)+(c)+(d)+(e) **Resolved at FP-008.4 #17.** Page-render test-orphan gap remains **Open** (rolled into INC-46 sub-finding (c); not duplicated here). |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 #17 closure SHA (pending); `src/features/longshort/utils/outcome-display.ts` (the new shared util); `…/outcome-display.test.ts` (16 Vitest cases incl. system_bug/failure_escalated/circuit_breaker_open severity regression guards); `src/features/longshort/components/LongShortDashboard.tsx` (cross-check card + events list adopters); `src/pages/trading/longshort/ReconciliationEventsPage.tsx` + `…/UniverseRefreshHistoryPage.tsx` (the two page-level adopters); `src/features/longshort/services/baseline/baseline-query-helpers.ts::ReconciliationOutcome` + `src/features/longshort/utils/universe-staleness.ts::RefreshOutcome` (canonical frontend enum types the helper keys off — no third copy introduced); CROSSWIND §11.0.10 / §11.0.11 + DEC-038 clause (2) + FP-008.4 Commit 3.5 `STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES` (the backend severity sources). |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 #17 SHA (pending CI green; 16/16 Vitest tests passing locally). |

### INC-48: FP-008.4 #23 framing correction — "per-ticker enrichment failure attribution" was scoped as 404-only but survey found two silent-skip channels (fetcher 404 + caller try/catch swallow); the caller's catch-all collapse to `kind: 'skipped'` was the larger attribution leak, with INC-24's per-ticker context landing in `console.warn` and nothing structured

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-48 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-04 (FP-008.4 #23 pre-investigation survey). |
| **Classification** | Five sub-findings bundled under #23's resolution: (a) **scope correction** — the original Bucket A framing was "per-ticker enrichment failure attribution" focused narrowly on `PolygonEnrichmentFetcher.fetchTickerDetails()` returning `null` on HTTP 404 (`polygon-enrichment-fetcher.ts:115`) and the fetcher's silent `continue`; the survey of the live caller (`longshort-universe-enrich-and-filter/index.ts:184-190`) found a second, larger silent-skip channel — the per-ticker worker's `try { … } catch (e) { console.warn(…); return { kind: 'skipped' }; }` collapsed EVERY thrown `ConstituentFetchError` (HTTP 401/5xx after retries, JSON parse, timeout, network) into the same `kind: 'skipped'` bucket as a benign 404, with the per-ticker error context surviving only as a `console.warn` line and nothing structured persisted. A 401 (system-wide auth failure), a 429 (rate-limited), a delisting-404, and a JSON-parse (Polygon API change) are operationally distinct but were indistinguishable in the audit. (b) **same defect class as #13** — silent per-item skip → attribution blind spot, this time on the enrichment path; the fix mirrors #13's "count + identities + reason" pattern with the addition of a persistence field since enrichment-skips need to survive into the refresh log (unlike Wikipedia's parse-time bounds which fail loud in-process). (c) **iShares-source skip silently reducing count** — `polygon-enrichment-fetcher.ts:112` `if (c.source === 'ishares') continue` silently reduced the enriched-set size with no attribution; benign-by-design but completes the attribution shape. (d) **downstream conflation** — `universe_refresh_log` had no field for enrichment-skip counts; `total_constituents_raw - total_post_filters` conflated enrichment-stage drops with filter-stage rejections (a 200-ticker enrichment collapse would appear as "filtering was harsh," wrong attribution). (e) **orchestrator-path-also-affected** — `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts:218` (the planned phase-2 batch-mode call site) also called `enrich()` and silently lost any 404 + iShares skips; the contract change fixes the fetcher boundary for both call sites, but persistence symmetry at the orchestrator's own refresh-log write is out of scope (registered as DW-088). |
| **Surface Story** | Pre-#23: an operator inspecting a refresh that came up suspiciously small (e.g., 850 eligible from a 903-ticker universe) would see `total_constituents_raw=903`, `total_post_filters=850`, `filter_rejection_counts` showing 30 rejections — and 23 tickers' worth of drop unexplained, with no way to distinguish "23 delistings (normal attrition)" from "23 rate-limited fetch errors (operational signal — Polygon issue / our API key issue)" from "23 JSON-parse failures (operational signal — Polygon API shape changed)." `console.warn` lines existed but were edge-function log noise, not structured queryable data. The survey reframed the gap from "404 attribution" to "all enrichment-skip attribution," found the survey-recommended fix (the fetcher-contract `EnrichmentResult { enriched, skipped }` with a closed-set of structural reasons), added the caller's `'fetch_error'` classification for thrown errors, and persisted the aggregate via the new MIG-061 `enrichment_skip_counts` column (mirroring MIG-053's `filter_rejection_counts` shape). |
| **Observation** | Resolved at FP-008.4 #23: (a)+(b)+(d) `UniverseEnrichmentFetcher.enrich()` contract widened from `Promise<EnrichedConstituent[]>` to `Promise<EnrichmentResult>` (new `enrichment/types.ts` exports `EnrichmentResult`, `EnrichmentSkip`, `EnrichmentSkipReason`); fetcher attributes both structural reasons (`'ishares_source'` + `'not_in_polygon_404'`) with ticker identity; caller adds `'fetch_error'` for thrown `ConstituentFetchError` (INC-24's per-ticker context now structurally captured, `console.warn` preserved for runtime debuggability); aggregate persists to `universe_refresh_log.enrichment_skip_counts` (MIG-061, jsonb nullable, no default — `NULL` = pre-MIG-061 untracked per INC-36 epistemic-honesty, `{…}` = post-MIG-061 tracked-zero-vs-populated). Tracked-zero convention: all three reasons appear with explicit `0` when they didn't fire (distinct from untracked-NULL). (c) iShares skip now attributed as `'ishares_source'` (benign-by-design but in the audit). (e) Orchestrator adapter at `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts:218` destructures `enriched` only — mechanical adapter, zero behavior change; persistence symmetry deferred as DW-088. Tests: 12/12 `polygon-enrichment-fetcher_test.ts` passes including new mixed-batch (1 ok + 1 404 + 1 iShares → enriched=1, skipped=2 with correct reasons); 26/26 `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator_test.ts` passes (mock shapes updated, no assertion changes); source-level sentinel pin in `enrich-and-filter/index_test.ts` asserts catch-block `reason: 'fetch_error'` + `enrichment_skip_counts: enrichmentSkipCounts` persistence + tracked-zero initialisation. |
| **Disposition** | Resolved at FP-008.4 #23. **Out-of-scope follow-ups explicitly registered**: DW-088 (orchestrator-side `enrichment_skip_counts` persistence symmetry — the orchestrator now receives `result.skipped` but discards it; persistence symmetry with the bootstrap caller is a separate scope of the same character as DW-087's migration-tree-vs-live-DB symmetry); DW-089 (enrichment-skip sanity-threshold — a 50× spike in `not_in_polygon_404` or `fetch_error` is operationally a signal, not just forensics, but the threshold envelope needs operational data to set; deferred to a future enhancement). **Caller-side behavioral-test orphan**: the worker-pool catch-block lives inside `Deno.serve(createHandler(...))` and is not unit-testable as a pure function without standing up a full Deno.serve + supabase-admin + Polygon HTTP harness; this commit uses a source-level sentinel pin (precedent: FP-008.4 Commit 3.5 STREAK_FAILURE_OUTCOMES sentinel) as the ONE behavioral assertion the new field requires, and rolls the broader caller-behavioral-coverage gap into the same orphan-class as INC-30 / DW-082 (the Gate 11 type-check anchor's broader sibling). |
| **Cross-references** | FP-008.4 #23 closure SHA (pending); MIG-061 (`enrichment_skip_counts jsonb` column add); INC-24 (the per-ticker fetcher-error context that was previously `console.warn`-only); INC-36 (the epistemic-honesty NULL-means-untracked principle reused for pre-MIG-061 rows — same shape as the audit-evidence trust boundary on `longshort_audit_logs` pre-MIG-057); INC-45 (FP-008.4 #13 — the analogous Wikipedia per-row silent-skip attribution gap on the constituent-ingestion path; same defect class, same survey-recommended fix pattern, different persistence model since #13's bounds fail in-process); DW-082 (the broader edge-function-behavioral-test-orphan gap into which the caller's behavioral-coverage gap is rolled); DW-087 (the precedent for the DW-088 follow-up's scope-boundary discipline); DW-088 (orchestrator-side persistence symmetry); DW-089 (enrichment-skip sanity threshold). |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-008.4 #23 SHA (pending CI green; 38/38 Deno tests passing locally; live-DB §22.5.1 verification post-MIG-061 apply: column shape + zero pre-MIG-061 backfill confirmed). |

### INC-49: GICS sector plumbing was missing through the entire Phase 1 universe-ingestion chain — Wikipedia + iShares parsers discarded the sector column, `UniverseConstituent` had no `gics_sector` field, `universe_membership` had no sector column; surfaced at FP-009 Phase 2.1 pre-investigation survey as the only architectural blocker for the 8-element template; closed at FP-009 Bucket 0 / MIG-063

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-49 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-05 (FP-009 Phase 2.1 pre-investigation survey). |
| **Classification** | Phase-1-residual-surfaced-by-Phase-2.1 — same shape as FP-008.4 Bucket A was Phase-0B-residual-surfaced-by-Phase-1 (the pattern is: a downstream phase's survey is the first natural test of whether the upstream phase's data plumbing is complete). Specifically: the Wikipedia constituent fetcher's parser located the `Symbol` column by header lookup and emitted ticker only, never reading the `GICS Sector` column that sits in the same table row; the iShares CSV parser located `Ticker` / `Name` / `Asset Class` but ignored `Sector`; the Polygon constituent fetcher does not carry GICS in its reference taxonomy (SIC only); `UniverseConstituent` and `EnrichedConstituent` had no `gics_sector` field; `universe_membership` had no `gics_sector` column. Phase 2.1's within-sector z-score normalization (template element 2, shared across all 9 signal sub-phases) requires sector per universe member — the absence is a hard blocker that would have surfaced mid-implementation as an architectural back-out had the pre-investigation survey not run. Survey-first paid off here: caught at zero cost; absorbed into Phase 2.1 as a prequel bucket (Option A operator-approved) rather than back-out or separate FP. |
| **Surface Story** | Pre-investigation survey for Phase 2.1 (Signal #6, Cross-sectional momentum) needed to confirm the 8-element signal-stack template's shared infrastructure dependencies. Template element 2 (within-sector GICS z-score normalization, per longshort.md §4.4.1) required `universe_member.gics_sector`. Source-code grep across `EnrichedConstituent` / `UniverseConstituent` / all four constituent fetchers / `universe_membership` schema returned zero hits for `gics_sector` or `sector` — the column was parsed-and-discarded at the source layer, never reached the type layer, never reached the persistence layer. Operator decisions: (Q1) Polygon Stocks Advanced tier confirmed; (Q2) backfill policy locked to NULL-forward (no backfill) per MIG-061 / INC-36 epistemic-honesty precedent — historical universe rows are forensic-not-financial; GICS reclassifications happen (2018 Communication Services creation is the canonical case) and a current sector stamped onto a 2017 `as_of_date` is a quiet lie of the MIG-061 class; no Phase 2.1+ consumer needs historical sector; if a future need emerges it gets its own DW + deliberate point-in-time backfill migration; (Q3) FP-009 bucket structure approved as Bucket 0 (this prequel) → A (shared infrastructure) → B (signal-specific) → C (production wiring + observational gate) → D (template-lock closure doc — meta-deliverable per A2). |
| **Observation** | Resolved at FP-009 Bucket 0 / MIG-063: (a) **Wikipedia parser** extended to surface `gics_sector` from the `GICS Sector` header (defense-in-depth: missing header throws `ConstituentFetchError` with `layout likely changed` diagnostic, same shape as the Symbol-column structural throw); per-row empty cell yields `null` (typed-absence per §2 axiom 3). Alternate header `Sector` (older S&P 400 revisions) accepted. (b) **iShares parser** extended to surface `Sector` column verbatim — treated as GICS-equivalent for v1 (taxonomic cross-check between Wikipedia GICS and iShares Sector is a future enhancement, DW-able if and when needed); empty/sentinel cells (`""`, `-`, `--`) → `null`. (c) **Polygon constituent fetcher** emits `gics_sector: null` uniformly (Polygon reference data does not carry GICS — honest typed-absence, NOT a silent default that would mislead z-score consumers downstream). (d) **Seeded-membership fetcher** emits `gics_sector: null` (rehydrator path; reads only ticker from `universe_membership`). (e) **Type contract**: `UniverseConstituent.gics_sector: string \| null` added once at the base; propagates through `EnrichedConstituent` (spread `...c` in the polygon-enrichment-fetcher) and `EligibleConstituent` (extends `EnrichedConstituent`) automatically — no shadow type definitions found. (f) **Persister thread**: single-site change at `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts:249-254` (one new `gics_sector: e.gics_sector` line on the rows-map); `UniverseMembershipPersisterInput.rows[].gics_sector` typed; persister UPSERT includes the column (last-writer-wins, same convention as `refresh_id`). (g) **MIG-063**: additive nullable `text` column, no default, no backfill UPDATE, no CHECK constraint (free-text per source); mirrors MIG-061 verbatim. (h) **Tests**: 4 new Wikipedia (positive verbatim propagation, missing-`GICS Sector`-header structural throw, empty-cell typed-absence, mixed-batch verbatim propagation, "Sector"-alone alternate header), 2 new iShares (sentinel/empty cells → null; CSV without Sector column → uniform null), 1 polygon-constituent assertion (uniform null); all touched test files pass. |
| **Disposition** | Resolved at FP-009 Bucket 0 SHA (pending CI green; live-DB §22.5.1 verification post-MIG-063 apply: column shape + zero pre-MIG-063 backfill confirmed). **Forward-binding observation (not a deferred-work entry)**: the quarterly-refresh orchestrator currently uses Polygon as primary (line 184-191); Polygon emits `gics_sector: null`, so `universe_membership.gics_sector` rows persisted by the quarterly orchestrator will remain NULL in production until a future bucket (likely FP-009 Bucket A's z-score-companion or a separate Phase 2.1 mini-bucket) wires Wikipedia as a sector enrichment step on the primary path. Bucket 0 lands the type + column + persister contract — the data-source-wiring layer is explicitly out of Bucket 0 scope. This is honest plumbing-before-source, NOT a defect: the column shape is the gate that lets Bucket A's z-score module compile against the real contract (z-score tests use synthetic fixtures with sector populated; production population follows when the source-wiring lands). **Anti-pattern guard observations**: backfill explicitly rejected (Q2); CHECK constraint explicitly rejected (taxonomy free-text per source); iShares-vs-Wikipedia sector taxonomic cross-check explicitly out of scope for v1 (future enhancement). |
| **Cross-references** | FP-009 Bucket 0 closure SHA (pending); MIG-063 (`gics_sector text` column add); FP-009 Phase 2.1 pre-investigation survey (the surfacing event); INC-36 (the epistemic-honesty NULL-means-untracked principle reused for pre-MIG-063 rows); MIG-061 (the precedent column-pattern this migration mirrors verbatim — there `jsonb` nullable, here `text` nullable); INC-45 (FP-008.4 #13 — the analogous Wikipedia per-row silent-skip attribution gap on the constituent-ingestion path; same parser, different defect class); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe-interfaces.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/constituent-ingestion/wikipedia-constituent-fetcher.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/constituent-ingestion/ishares-constituent-fetcher.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/constituent-ingestion/polygon-constituent-fetcher.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/constituent-ingestion/seeded-membership-fetcher.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/universe-membership-persister.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/refresh-jobs/quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/verify-membership/types.ts` (`UniverseMembershipPersisterInput` row shape). |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-009 Bucket 0 SHA (pending CI green; live-DB §22.5.1 verification post-MIG-063 apply: column shape + zero pre-MIG-063 backfill confirmed). |

### INC-50: FP-009 Bucket 0.1 — source-wiring closure for the INC-49 sector-plumbing arc via pure 3-line projection over already-fetched Wikipedia data (no new I/O); latent naming-debt observation captured on the legacy `ctx.iSharesConstituents` field name now holding `WikipediaConstituentFetcher`

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **ID** | INC-50 |
| **Date Surfaced** | 2026-06-05 (FP-009 Bucket 0.1 pre-task verification 5). |
| **Classification** | Phase-2.1-prequel-source-wiring-closure — completes the INC-49 sector-plumbing arc that Bucket 0 explicitly scoped to "type + column + persister contract, source-wiring out of scope." The forward-binding observation in INC-49's Disposition ("`universe_membership.gics_sector` rows will remain NULL in production until a future bucket wires Wikipedia as a sector enrichment step on the primary path") is the precise gap Bucket 0.1 closes. Secondary: a latent naming-debt observation surfaced during pre-task verification 5 of the original Bucket 0.1 draft (named below). |
| **Surface Story** | The original FP-009 Bucket 0.1 draft proposed a new `WikipediaSectorEnrichmentFetcher` class with its own dedicated Wikipedia HTTP call, mirroring the survey's framing. Pre-task verification 5 surfaced that the orchestrator already calls Wikipedia twice per quarterly refresh — the `ctx.iSharesConstituents` field is wired with `new WikipediaConstituentFetcher()` at `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh/index.ts:209` per FP-008.2 Step C (the iShares→Wikipedia cross-check secondary swap; storage-layer field-rename explicitly deferred at that step per the in-file comment block at lines 233-237). The Wikipedia-sourced array (`ishares_cross_check` at orchestrator line 196) already carries `gics_sector` per Bucket 0's parser work, and is in scope at the projection insertion point (between Polygon enrichment at line 225 and `applyFilters` at line 228). Operator Q3-refinement-honest application: dedupe to a pure 3-line in-memory projection over the already-fetched data; no new fetcher class, no new HTTP call, no new file under `enrichment/`, no new failure surface. Survey-first + pre-task-verification discipline caught the architectural mistake at zero cost — the original draft would have added a wasteful third Wikipedia round-trip per refresh and a second failure surface on the same upstream source. |

This created a search-blind-spot that initially mislead the original Bucket 0.1 draft, the FP-009 Phase 2.1 survey, AND the Phase 1 exit-gate sector-coverage check — three independent reviews missed it. ### INC-52 — Polygon price-history fetcher mirrors the enrichment fetcher hardening pattern (FP-009 Bucket A Commit A2)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced at** | FP-009 Bucket A Commit A2 — Polygon price-history fetcher introduction. |
| **Classification** | Pattern-reuse observation, not a defect. Documented for forensic traceability and to anchor the "promote to `_shared/polygon-constants.ts` on third consumer" trigger. |
| **Observation** | `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/polygon-price-history-fetcher.ts` is a sibling to `_shared/longshort-universe/enrichment/polygon-enrichment-fetcher.ts`. Shape mirrored verbatim: (a) constructor takes `apiKey` + injected `httpFetch` + optional `timeoutMs`; (b) `fetchWithTimeoutAndRetry` from `_shared/longshort-universe/shared/fetch-with-timeout.ts` is reused unchanged (cross-tree import is the third call site after the three universe fetchers — file is genuinely shared infrastructure and the import path works; no extraction to a higher `_shared/longshort-shared/` location, scope creep avoided); (c) HTTP 404 → `null` typed-absence (ticker not in Polygon reference); non-404 errors throw the signal-stage analog `SignalComputationError` (Commit A1 class) rather than `ConstituentFetchError` (the universe-stage class) — this carries `signal_id` + `ticker` matching the `SignalSkip 'fetch_error'` attribution shape; (d) per-ticker context preserved in every throw per INC-24; (e) `adjusted=true` server-side split/dividend adjustment, no client-side math; (f) `as_of: Date` injected-clock chokepoint per DEC-038 (6) — `new Date(as_of.getTime() - lookback*MS_PER_DAY)` is arithmetic, not a wall-clock read. Stage difference: enrichment uses 60 trading-day lookback for filter-input data; signal stage uses default 280 calendar-day lookback (Signal #6 needs 252 trading days + skip-month buffer; 280 covers it with weekend/holiday padding). Both share `POLYGON_API_KEY` and `POLYGON_BASE_URL` (the constant is duplicated as a 25-character local string literal with a comment pointing to the enrichment fetcher's identical declaration — promoting to a shared constant would force a cross-import for a string literal, which is more friction than the duplication; promote on third consumer). |
| **Disposition** | No action required. The duplication of `POLYGON_BASE_URL` and `isoDate` (~6 lines each) is intentional per anti-premature-abstraction discipline; promote both when a third Polygon consumer lands. The error-class split (`ConstituentFetchError` at universe stage / `SignalComputationError` at signal stage) is intentional per FP-009 Bucket A Commit A1 typed-attribution design — each error class carries the stage-appropriate context fields (`source`/`index` vs `signal_id`/`ticker`). 12 Deno tests at `polygon-price-history-fetcher_test.ts`, all green: constructor-validation, happy-path (280 ascending bars, adjusted closes threaded, last-bar ts = as_of), 404→null, 401→throw with ticker context, 500→throw-after-retries with status preserved, timeout (AbortError)→throw with timeout message, JSON parse→throw, empty results→`[]` not null (distinct from 404 typed-absence), `lookbackDays` parameter URL date-range verification, default-lookback constant assertion (280), determinism (JSON-equality across two calls), malformed-bar drop (not throw). |
| **Cross-references** | INC-24 (ticker-context-in-throws discipline); FP-009 Bucket A Commit A1 (`SignalComputationError` introduction); FP-009 Bucket A Commit A2 closure SHA (pending); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/polygon-price-history-fetcher.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/polygon-price-history-fetcher_test.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/enrichment/polygon-enrichment-fetcher.ts` (sibling pattern); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/shared/fetch-with-timeout.ts` (shared infrastructure — third call site). |
| **Status** | Documented. No defect; no follow-up. Promote `POLYGON_BASE_URL` + `isoDate` to `_shared/polygon-constants.ts` when a third Polygon consumer is added. |

No signal has yet been wired to call `captureSignalObservations` in production. First observations arrive at Bucket C when the momentum signal runs against the live universe. Bucket A close criterion is therefore "table accepts inserts cleanly in a synthetic test invocation" (verified via the 8-test mock-SupabaseClient suite at `missingness-capture_test.ts`), NOT "observations exist in production" (that is Bucket C's observational gate, parallel in shape to Bucket 0.1's "next quarterly refresh observably populates `gics_sector`"). If post-Bucket-C the table remains empty, that is an INC-worthy observation indicating the orchestrator's end-of-tick capture call is broken or omitted. |
