### INC-54 — Cross-sectional momentum spec-vs-academic-interpretation gotcha + reconciliation hook deferred from Bucket B Commit B1 (FP-009 Bucket B Commit B1)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced at** | FP-009 Bucket B Commit B1 — pre-task verification by executor (Lovable) on the original B1 draft. |
| **Classification** | Two-part: (a) spec-vs-academic-interpretation correction on the momentum threshold — would have silently tightened a §14 ROI guardrail; (b) reconciliation-hook deferral on architectural-choice grounds — Option A per executor recommendation, accepted. |
| **Surface story (a) — indexing** | Original B1 draft set `MOMENTUM_MIN_BARS = 273`, anchored on the academic-quant "12-1 momentum" interpretation: full 252-day return AFTER a 21-day skip (formula `(P[T-21] / P[T-273]) - 1`, requiring 273 bars). Pre-task verification against CROSSWIND §4.4.1 verbatim found the spec literal is `(P[T-21] / P[T-252]) - 1` with "fewer than 252 trading days of history" as the exclusion threshold — a 231-day return whose tail ends 21 days before T, requiring only `bars[T-252]` access (253-bar minimum, not 273). The two interpretations differ by 20 bars per ticker: 273 would silently over-exclude ~20 days' worth of universe names beyond the spec-stated minimum. Per the project's §14 ROI guardrails, a silent threshold tightening is forbidden as an action (acceptable only with an explicit ROI memo). Corrected to spec-literal `MOMENTUM_MIN_BARS = 253`; no memo required because the change matches the spec rather than diverging from it. The §4.4.1 "skip-21-day" language is preserved — the window still ends 21 days before T — but the return-period math is 231 days, not the academic 252. Forward-binding lesson for sibling signals: Signal #7 (reversal, §4.4.2) and others must follow spec formulas verbatim, not academic-name conventions; off-by-one sentinel tests should lock the explicit `bars[T-N] / bars[T-M]` indexing per signal. |
| **Surface story (b) — reconciliation deferral** | Original B1 draft also proposed `momentum-reconciliation.ts` building a `ReconcileCallSpec` for primary-vs-backup-source comparison per CROSSWIND §11.0.5 template element 4. Pre-task verification against `_shared/longshort-reconciliation-types.ts:99-142` found the draft's spec shape (`{ call_name, expected, observed, tolerance_classifier, metadata }`) did not match the actual `ReconcileCallSpec` interface (which has `operator_id`, `symbol`, `tier`, `tolerance_class`, `tolerance`, `compute_divergence`, `classify_outcome`, `failure_action` — and a *closed* `VerifyCallName` union for `call_name` that does NOT include signal-specific names; DW-069 territory). Two integration paths exist: **Option B** = build a parallel `SignalReconcileSpec` type local to `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/` (avoids editing the existing reconciliation types; creates a second reconciliation contract); **Option C** = widen `VerifyCallName` to add 9 signal-specific names + populate the full `ReconcileCallSpec` (single contract; mutates a shared type consumed across the reconciliation engine). The architectural choice between B and C has consequences for the next 8 signals (template 2.2-2.9) and for Phase 3's combiner reconciliation pattern. **Option A — defer entirely from B1 — was selected** for three reinforcing reasons: (i) no backup source exists in the repo (Yahoo / Tradier / IBKR per §4.4.1 are spec-named but never wired; first plausible landing is Phase 7 paper-trading-broker data); a reconciliation hook built now returns `expected_divergence_handled` on every invocation until a backup source surfaces — ~100 lines of placeholder scaffolding, not load-bearing infrastructure. (ii) The B-vs-C choice is a real architectural commitment, not a stylistic one; locking it without operational pressure (i.e., without an actual backup source forcing the decision) is premature commitment. Deferring lets the choice be made when operational data forces the right answer. (iii) Bucket B's exit gate is "momentum signal observably producing values in production" — reconciliation is template element 4 of 8 but can be locked at Bucket D (template-lock closure) rather than Bucket B (signal-running); the decoupling is honest because element 4's purpose has no current consumer. |
| **Disposition** | Resolved at B1 for both parts. (a) `MOMENTUM_MIN_BARS = 253` in `compute-momentum.ts`; test (11) is the explicit off-by-one sentinel locking `bars[T-21]` / `bars[T-252]` indexing with T=252 for 253-bar input; the file-header comment explicitly names the 273-vs-253 trap and cross-references this INC. (b) Reconciliation hook deferred from B1; landing target is Bucket B Commit B3 OR Bucket D (template-lock closure) — whichever surfaces operational pressure first. `_shared/longshort-reconciliation-types.ts` was NOT touched at B1 (preserves the option space for the B-vs-C choice). Forward-binding gate: when a backup price-history source wires up (likely Phase 7 paper-trading-broker work), reconciliation hook authoring must (1) re-read this INC for the B-vs-C framing, (2) re-read `longshort-reconciliation-types.ts:99-142` for the current interface shape, (3) commit to B or C explicitly with rationale before writing the hook. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-009 Bucket B Commit B1 closure SHA (pending); CROSSWIND §4.4.1 (the spec verbatim); §4.3.5 (critical-signal classification); §11.0.5 (ingestion-time reconciliation template element 4); §14 (ROI guardrails); DW-069 (`VerifyCallName` naming-vs-scope discrepancy — the closed-union constraint that drives the B-vs-C choice); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/cross-sectional-momentum/compute-momentum.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/cross-sectional-momentum/compute-momentum_test.ts` (test 11 is the off-by-one sentinel); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-reconciliation-types.ts` (the interface that drove the B vs C framing). |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-009 Bucket B Commit B1 for (a); deferred-with-named-landing-targets for (b). |

### INC-55 — Momentum orchestrator lands as the universe-orchestrator parallel; `pLimitedMap` extracted to shared on its second consumer (FP-009 Bucket B Commit B2)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced at** | FP-009 Bucket B Commit B2 — first runnable end-to-end signal pipeline. |
| **Classification** | Pattern-reuse + leaf-utility-extraction observation; not a defect. Captures the architectural-parallel decision and the second-consumer extraction trigger. |
| **Observation** | (a) `momentum-orchestrator.ts` mirrors `quarterly-refresh-orchestrator.ts` verbatim in shape: DI-injected `SignalOrchestratorContext` (parallel to `RefreshExecutionContext`), numbered try/catch steps, structured `SignalOrchestratorResult` with `outcome ∈ {completed, failed}` + per-ticker `skipped: SignalSkip[]` attribution. Signal-side is simpler (single data source — Polygon only — and single-table persistence — `signal_observations` only) because (i) the primary-vs-backup reconciliation hook is deferred from B1 per INC-54 Option A and (ii) the signal stage has no three-table flow analogous to the universe-side log + membership + coverage triad. (b) Per-ticker failure attribution mirrors FP-008.4 #23's `enrichment_skip_counts` pattern verbatim: each universe row yields either a `SignalRow` (success) or a `SignalSkip` (typed-absence with one of four reasons `'insufficient_history' | 'missing_sector' | 'fetch_error' | 'singleton_sector'`); orchestrator never throws on individual-ticker failure (preserving the universe-side discipline of letting downstream observability surface the skip-rate rather than letting one bad ticker abort an entire run). Only catastrophic failures (universe-read error) throw; persistence error becomes `outcome='failed'` with reason captured — no partial-success state. (c) `pLimitedMap` extracted from its function-local placement at `longshort-universe-enrich-and-filter/index.ts:48` (FP-008.4 #23 vintage) to `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/p-limited-map.ts`. The momentum orchestrator is the second consumer, the boundary at which extraction wins over copy-paste. Original site updated to import from the shared location; call sites and behavior unchanged. The shared location is `longshort-signals/shared/` rather than a new `longshort-shared/` directory — creating a new top-level shared directory for a single utility would be premature; the universe layer already cross-imports `fetch-with-timeout` from `longshort-universe/shared/` in the opposite direction, so the cross-tree leaf-utility import pattern is established. Promote to a higher `_shared/` location when a third consumer emerges outside the universe/signals trees. (d) Universe-read query: two-step (latest `as_of_date` lookup → membership rows at that date) rather than the draft's `order().limit(1000)` shape, which would have pulled historical rows mixed with current. The cleaner query shape ensures the signal operates on the operator's current universe snapshot, not a mix of historical snapshots. (e) Empty-universe path returns `outcome='failed'` with `failure_reason='empty_universe'` rather than silently producing zero rows — a silent empty output would mask a serious upstream failure (e.g., universe pipeline broken; operator misconfigured). |
| **Disposition** | No action required. Forward-binding watch at Bucket C: production wiring will need an edge function + manual-trigger function + `job_registry` row + component doc; the orchestrator API (`createMomentumOrchestrator(ctx).run(as_of)`) is locked as the entry shape for those callers. ### INC-57 — Calendar-vs-trading-day units confusion in `PRICE_HISTORY_LOOKBACK_DAYS` (caught by C2a observational gate; hotfix bundled)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced at** | FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2a observational gate fire 2026-06-05 — manual invocation of `longshort-momentum-compute-manual` (run_id `f8e10475-711f-4a8f-9cf8-b9a172b10f01`, `as_of=2026-06-05`). |
| **Classification** | Correctness defect (numeric constant); priority/critical-class — every ticker tripped `insufficient_history`, persisted zero rows. Caught pre-enable-flip by exactly the layer designed to catch it (disarmed-cron + observational gate). |
| **Observation** | `signal_compute_log` row returned `outcome='completed'`, `universe_size=839`, `persisted_count=0`, `skip_counts.insufficient_history=839`. Root cause: `PRICE_HISTORY_LOOKBACK_DAYS = 280` at `momentum-orchestrator.ts:50` (and parallel default `DEFAULT_PRICE_HISTORY_LOOKBACK_DAYS = 280` at `polygon-price-history-fetcher.ts`) is too narrow to span the trading-day `MOMENTUM_MIN_BARS=253` threshold. Calendar/trading-day ratio is ~252/365 ≈ 0.69, so 280 calendar days yields only ~193 trading days — short of 253 by ~60 bars. The B2 draft comment ("280 covers 252 trading days + 28-day buffer for weekends/holidays + 1-month skip") propagated the wrong arithmetic: subtracting a 28-day "buffer" from 280 calendar days still leaves calendar days, not trading days; the unit confusion was subtle and the comment didn't flag it. Originated in Bucket A Commit A2 (fetcher default) and propagated through Bucket B Commit B2 (orchestrator constant) and the B1 docstring — not caught by any code-shape sentinel because no sentinel exists for numeric-units correctness. |
| **Fix** | Single-commit hotfix bundled into this C2a closure: `PRICE_HISTORY_LOOKBACK_DAYS = 400` at the orchestrator; `DEFAULT_PRICE_HISTORY_LOOKBACK_DAYS = 400` at the fetcher; in-source docstrings rewritten with explicit calendar-vs-trading-day arithmetic (`400 × (252/365) ≈ 276 trading days`; 23-day headroom for holiday clusters above `MIN_BARS=253`). Test `(10) default lookbackDays` updated to assert 400 and the corresponding URL date range (`/range/1/day/2025-04-20/2026-05-25` for `AS_OF=2026-05-25`). Component doc updated. `MOMENTUM_MIN_BARS` unchanged (spec-locked at 253 per §4.4.1 / INC-54); orchestrator shape unchanged; fetcher contract unchanged; no MIG, no schema. The `limit=5000` query parameter on the Polygon URL comfortably accommodates 400 × 0.69 ≈ 276 bars. |
| **Forward-binding observation** | Any future signal sub-phase (2.2-2.9) that introduces a lookback constant MUST include explicit calendar-vs-trading-day arithmetic in the in-source comment (e.g., `N calendar days × (252/365) ≈ M trading days ≥ MIN_BARS_K + headroom`). The Phase 2.1 template-lock at Bucket D should codify this as an explicit element of the template documentation: never ship a `PRICE_HISTORY_LOOKBACK_DAYS = N`-style constant without inline arithmetic showing the units math. Validates the disarmed-cron + observational-gate + enable-flip discipline — the observational gate fire is exactly the layer that caught this; without it, the defect would have shipped to production-enabled cron and produced zero rows nightly until a downstream consumer (Phase 3 combiner) failed loudly. |
| **Disposition** | Resolved at FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2a hotfix. Re-fire of the observational gate is pending operator action after this commit lands and CI greens; C2b enable-flip (MIG-067) follows once the re-fire shows `persisted_count / universe_size ≥ 80%`. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2a closure SHA (pending); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/cross-sectional-momentum/momentum-orchestrator.ts:50`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/polygon-price-history-fetcher.ts`; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/polygon-price-history-fetcher_test.ts` (test 10); `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/cross-sectional-momentum.md`; INC-54 (`MOMENTUM_MIN_BARS=253` spec lock); FP-009 Bucket A Commit A2 (fetcher origin); FP-009 Bucket B Commit B2 (orchestrator origin); CROSSWIND §4.4.1. |
| **Status** | Resolved at FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2a hotfix; observational gate re-fire pending. |

## INC-58 — First tradable signal value in production; observational gate as defect-catching layer; forward-binding multi-platform-constraint discoveries

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-06 during FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2b execution (MIG-067 enable-flip of `longshort.momentum.compute`). |
| **Class** | Forward-binding observation + governance-record milestone (not a defect). |
| **Symptom / event** | After the post-hotfix (commit 61ce662) redeploy, the C2a observational gate re-fire on as_of=2026-06-05 (run_id `59946ae5-57cd-485a-9cc4-5dcd17d15925`) produced `outcome=completed`, `universe_size=839`, `persisted_count=834` (99.4% coverage), `skip_counts={insufficient_history:4, missing_sector:1, fetch_error:0, singleton_sector:0}`, z-score distribution `min=-2.69, max=+3.0, mean=-0.0225` with `at_clip_bound=7` (clipped asymmetric on the +3 side — real market signature; the symmetric clip in `z-score-normalize.ts` working as designed). All 11 GICS sectors represented. MIG-067 then flipped `enabled=true`. This is **the first tradable signal value the platform has ever produced in production** — the load-bearing Phase 2.1 artifact (the within-sector z-score signal value at the ranking layer) now exists. |
| **Significance — what this means** | (1) Phase 2.1's load-bearing artifact — the within-sector z-score signal value at the Phase 3 combiner ranking layer — now exists in production with statistically coherent distribution across 11 sectors. (2) Phase 2.2 through 2.9 (sibling signals) can mechanically inherit the locked template (8-element file pattern + 5-step orchestrator pipeline + signal_observations / signal_compute_log row shapes + disarm-then-fire-then-enable cron discipline + observational-gate defect-catching layer). (3) The work to lock that template for inheritance is Bucket D (template-lock closure document citing the locked architectural decisions so 2.2–2.9 inherit them without re-litigation). |
| **Observational gate as defect-catching layer** | The disarm-then-fire-then-enable discipline (MIG-066 disarmed seed → C2a manual-trigger observational gate fire → MIG-067 enable-flip) is now validated by both a clean fire AND a caught-defect-then-clean-fire cycle. The first pre-hotfix fire surfaced `skip_counts.insufficient_history=839` (whole universe excluded) — caught by the observational gate before MIG-067 could authorize cron traffic on a broken pipeline. No code-shape sentinel (typecheck, lint, sentinel-script, Gate-15 handler-coverage check) could have caught the calendar-vs-trading-day unit confusion at the `PRICE_HISTORY_LOOKBACK_DAYS=280` line: the constant typed cleanly, the orchestrator shape was correct, the handler was wired correctly, the migration was idempotent — the defect lived in semantic-units arithmetic that only an end-to-end fire against real data could surface. Generalizable rule: every signal sub-phase MUST include a disarmed-then-observational-fire-then-enable cycle as a load-bearing defect-catching layer, not as ceremonial gating. |
| **Forward-binding multi-platform-constraint discoveries** | C2a → C2b execution surfaced three Supabase-edge-function platform constraints not previously documented in repo, captured here for forward binding across all sibling signal sub-phases: (a) **Cross-function imports not supported** at the edge-function deployment layer — code shared between sibling handlers MUST be extracted to `supabase/functions/_shared/` (the canonical pattern; `parse-as-of-date.ts` and `persist-signal-compute-log.ts` were moved/extracted accordingly during C1 deployment iteration). Sibling-handler architecture (universe-cron + universe-manual, momentum-cron + momentum-manual, future signal cron + manual pairs) MUST plan for `_shared/` extraction from inception. (b) **Secret rotation requires cold-start trigger via redeploy** — env vars (`POLYGON_API_KEY`, etc.) are bound at deploy time; rotating a secret without redeploying the consuming function leaves the function running with the stale value. Operator runbooks for secret rotation MUST include the redeploy step. (c) **Code-commit ≠ runtime deploy** — pushing code to main does not update the deployed runtime; the C2a hotfix at commit 61ce662 was correct in source but the runtime served pre-hotfix code until explicit redeploy via `supabase--deploy_edge_functions`. Same defect class as the Bucket 0.2 "function in dashboard but not routed at gateway" finding. All three constraints are forward-binding for Phase 2.2–2.9 (mirrored manual-trigger pattern, secret-rotation runbooks, deploy-after-hotfix discipline). |
| **Cross-references** | MIG-067 (the enable-flip); MIG-066 (the disarmed seed); INC-57 (the hotfix lineage that gave the observational gate its clean fire); FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2b closure; `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/cross-sectional-momentum.md`; Bucket 0.2 universe-manual sibling-handler precedent (where the cross-function-imports + redeploy constraints first surfaced operationally); Bucket D (template-lock — will codify the disarm-then-fire-then-enable cycle as a locked element of the signal template). |
| **Disposition** | Forward-binding observation captured; no fix required (this is a milestone + a record of platform constraints, not a defect). Bucket D template-lock MUST cite this INC and codify the three platform constraints as locked template elements. |
| **Status** | Captured at FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2b. |

## INC-59 — Gate-15 offline-replay sentinel INSERT parser drops `handler_path`; latent until enable-flip

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-06 immediately after MIG-067 (`longshort.momentum.compute.enabled` flip true) at the FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2b post-CI cycle. |
| **Class** | Verification-infrastructure state-reconstruction gap — **distinct** from prior FP-009 defect classes (wall-clock in kernels, `any` types, calendar-vs-trading-day units). This one lives in the offline-replay sentinel itself, not in production code. The defect class is: "offline-replay sentinel diverges from live-DB ground truth." |
| **Symptom** | Gate-15 CI test `resolveJobStateFromMigrations — current repo` flipped red on 8656d7b after MIG-067 landed. Violation: `{ rule: "P2-null-handler", job_id: "longshort.momentum.compute", handler_path: null }`. Live DB §22.5.1 verification at MIG-067 apply time confirmed `handler_path = 'supabase/functions/longshort-momentum-compute/index.ts'` correctly populated — i.e., the violation existed only in the offline-replay reconstruction, not in production state. |
| **Root cause** | `scripts/check-handler-liveness-markers.ts:178-211` — the INSERT parser reads only `enabledIdx` and `triggerIdx` from the column list and hardcoded `handler_path: null` at the state.set call (line 211 pre-fix). The UPDATE parser at line 247 correctly honors `handler_path`. The script header at lines 20-21 explicitly admitted the design: "handler_path comes from the MIG-060 backfill UPDATEs in the same migration tree" — written when every job's handler_path was retrofitted via UPDATE, never extended to read handler_path from a fresh INSERT VALUES list. MIG-066 was the first migration to follow the FP-008.4 Commit 8 canonical pattern (INSERT with handler_path already in VALUES); the resolver gap silently mis-reconstructed the row's handler_path as null. The gap was latent and harmless while the affected job was `enabled=false` (both P1 and P2 short-circuit on `enabled=true`); MIG-067's enable-flip moved the row into the P2 detection window and exposed it. |
| **Fix** | Single-file tail-hotfix at `scripts/check-handler-liveness-markers.ts`: added `const handlerPathIdx = cols.indexOf('handler_path')` parallel to `enabledIdx`/`triggerIdx` and read it with `handlerPathIdx >= 0 ? unquote(cells[handlerPathIdx]) : existing?.handler_path ?? null` before the `state.set` call; replaced the hardcoded `null` literal at the `state.set` with the parsed value. The `if (existing) continue` ON-CONFLICT-DO-NOTHING gate unchanged (re-INSERTs into existing rows remain no-ops). Regression test added at `check-handler-liveness-markers_test.ts` pinning "INSERT with handler_path in VALUES populates handler_path" so the gap cannot silently re-emerge if the resolver is refactored. No migration, no handler, no production state touched — purely a CI-sentinel correctness fix that aligns offline replay with live-DB ground truth. All 17 sentinel tests green post-fix (16 prior + 1 new regression). |
| **Forward-binding observation (Phase 2.2-2.9 + Bucket D template-lock)** | (1) Every future signal sub-phase's `job_registry` INSERT MUST include `handler_path` directly in VALUES (the canonical FP-008.4 Commit 8 pattern MIG-066 followed). The resolver now handles this correctly — no further sentinel changes needed for sibling signals 2.2-2.9. (2) The disarm-then-fire-then-enable cycle's **third step** (enable-flip) is itself a load-bearing defect-catching layer, parallel in role to the **second step** (manual-fire) captured in INC-58. Manual-fire exercises the signal-pipeline layer end-to-end against real data; enable-flip exercises the verification-infrastructure layer (Gate-15 + any future state-driven sentinels) by moving the row out of the `enabled=false` short-circuit zone. Both steps are load-bearing defect-catching, not ceremony. The Bucket D template-lock document MUST codify both observations under a single "disarm-then-fire-then-enable as multi-layer defect-catching" element of the signal template, citing INC-58 (manual-fire layer) and this INC (enable-flip layer) together. (3) The broader pattern surfaced across C2a → C2b is: each layer in the multi-step rollout cycle exists specifically to surface a class of defect no earlier layer could surface — the calendar-vs-trading-day units bug (INC-57) needed a live Polygon call against real data to surface (no code-shape sentinel could catch it); the resolver INSERT-drops-handler_path bug (this INC) needed an `enabled=true` state to surface (no `enabled=false`-era CI run could catch it). Forward-binding: Phase 2.2-2.9 sibling sub-phases MUST plan for both layers, not collapse the cycle on the assumption that earlier successful fires generalize. |
| **Cross-references** | INC-58 (parallel observation for the manual-fire layer); MIG-066 (the canonical INSERT-with-handler_path pattern that exposed the resolver gap); MIG-067 (the enable-flip that surfaced the gap); FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2b tail-hotfix closure; `scripts/check-handler-liveness-markers.ts:178-211` (the fixed INSERT parser); `scripts/check-handler-liveness-markers_test.ts` (regression test pinning the fix); FP-008.4 Commit 8 (the canonical disarm-at-INSERT pattern this resolver gap silently failed to support); ADR-004 (live-DB-verification-discipline — the live §22.5.1 verification at MIG-067 apply was what proved the violation was an offline-replay false positive rather than a real production-state issue). |
| **Disposition** | Resolved at FP-009 Bucket C Commit C2b tail-hotfix. Bucket D template-lock document MUST cite this INC. |
| **Status** | Resolved. |

### INC-61 — FP-010 Bucket A Commit A3 — Signal monitor cron handler + canonical-pattern adaptations + Tuesday-holiday false-positive deferral

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced at** | FP-010 Bucket A Commit A3 — `longshort-signal-monitor/index.ts` + `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/job-signal-mapping.ts` + 25 source-sentinel + mapping tests. Third and largest source commit of FP-010 execution. |
| **Classification** | Three-part: (a) **A3 closure milestone** — handler landed with full canonical-pattern conformance vs FP-009 C1; (b) **5 canonical-pattern adaptations from supervisor sketch** — surfaced at pre-task verification ladder before any source file was written, materially reshaping the sketch (T4 audit-writer trap was the load-bearing one — sketch's `logAuditEvent` import would have CI-failed at the DEC-034 clause 5 strategy-audit-table-misroute sentinel after wasteful round-trip); (c) **Tuesday-after-Monday-market-holiday false-positive deferral** — explicit pattern citation following the FP-009 B1 reconciliation-hook Option-A defer precedent; and (d) **A2 supervisor-draft schema-drift forward-binding observation** — future MIGs adding rows to existing tables MUST grep-verify column shape before drafting (the `alert_configs` `name`/`description` columns the A2 sketch assumed do not exist in the live schema; same risk surface lurks for future row-INSERT MIGs against pre-existing tables). |
| **Observation (a) — A3 deliverables** | Four source files: (1) `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/job-signal-mapping.ts` — `Readonly<Record<string,string>>` registry with single momentum entry at A3 (`'longshort.momentum.compute' → 'cross_sectional_momentum_12_1'`); Phases 2.2-2.9 each add one entry in the same PR as their compute job registration; (2) `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/job-signal-mapping_test.ts` — 6 Deno tests (entry presence + cross-reference vs momentum-orchestrator SIGNAL_ID drift sentinel + accessor branches + single-entry guard + immutability); (3) `longshort-signal-monitor/index.ts` — ~280-line cron handler consuming A1 predicates + A2 alert_configs UUIDs + A3 mapping; (4) `longshort-signal-monitor/index_test.ts` — 19 source-sentinel tests across 5 categories (A-F). All 25 tests green. Runbook landed at `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/runbooks/signal-monitor-alerts-runbook.md` with 8 sections covering operator response per alert_type + monitor liveness check + Tuesday-holiday handling. |
| **Observation (b) — 5 canonical-pattern adaptations** | Surfaced at pre-task verification ladder per §22.3(g) BEFORE handler code was written (saving an estimated round-trip of ~600 LOC against the wrong canonical pattern): (i) **T4 audit-writer trap**: sketch imported `logAuditEvent` from `_shared/audit-event.ts` (a file that does not exist; only `_shared/audit.ts` does, and it's hardcoded to platform `audit_logs`). DEC-033 v4.1 clause 4 + T4 ban strategy code from `logAuditEvent`. Adapted to `writeStrategyAuditEvent({ strategyKey: 'longshort', action, correlationId, metadata })` for all 4 events. Storage table is `longshort_audit_logs` not platform `audit_logs` — strengthens T6 removability (deleting the longshort strategy means dropping `longshort_audit_logs` cleanly, taking signal-monitor events with it). (ii) **`verifyCronSecret` return shape**: returns `Response | null` (null=valid), NOT `{ ok, error }`. Adapted to canonical early-return: `const cronAuthError = verifyCronSecret(req); if (cronAuthError) return cronAuthError;`. Import path is `_shared/cron-auth.ts` not `_shared/verify-cron-secret.ts`. (iii) **`createHandler` wrapper + `apiError`/`apiSuccess` helpers**: sketch's raw `Deno.serve` + manual `new Response(JSON.stringify(...))` + manual `Content-Type` headers would produce responses with no CORS headers and diverge from canonical. Adapted to `Deno.serve(createHandler(async (req) => { ... }))` + `apiSuccess({ ... })` + `apiError(500, code, { correlationId })`. (iv) **`supabaseAdmin` singleton vs per-request `createClient`**: sketch's manual env-var check + `createClient(url, key)` reinvents infrastructure that already exists at `_shared/supabase-admin.ts`. Adapted to import the singleton; Gate-14 esm.sh→bare-specifier sentinel + first-access env validation are both centralized in the singleton. (v) **`productionClock` import path**: sketch used `_shared/clock.ts` (does not exist); the canonical path is `_shared/longshort-clock.ts` (the documented DEC-034 clause-4 chokepoint for the engine path). All 5 adaptations confirmed by operator before A3 wrote a single source file. |
| **Observation (c) — Tuesday-holiday false-positive deferral** | The 36h Tue-Fri staleness threshold works on weekdays-following-weekdays. On the Tuesday following a US market holiday that falls on Monday (MLK Day, Presidents' Day, Memorial Day, Labor Day, etc. — ≤10 days/year), the monitor's stale predicate sees no `signal_compute_log` row since Friday (~96h ago) and emits a false-positive `signal_compute_stale` alert. The momentum compute correctly skipped Monday (market closed), so there is no signal to re-compute. **Operator handling**: acknowledge via `alert_history.resolved_at = now()` on the Tuesday-after-Monday-holiday morning. **Future enhancement entry point**: FP-010 Point 4 option (c) — trading-calendar-aware staleness via external holiday-calendar feed or hardcoded US-market-holiday data. Pattern citation: mirrors FP-009 B1 reconciliation-hook Option-A defer (pattern-locked; execution deferred until operational pressure forces it). Runbook §6 documents the deferral explicitly so operators can disposition the alert without re-discovery. |
| **Observation (d) — A2 supervisor-draft schema-drift (forward-binding)** | A2's supervisor draft assumed `alert_configs` had `name`/`description` columns — the live schema does not have them; the alert-type identifier is encoded via `metric_key` and the predicate semantics via `(threshold_value, comparison)`. Lovable caught the drift at A2 pre-task verification by reading the migration history, but the supervisor sketch carried the wrong column assumption forward into the prompt. Forward-binding rule: when drafting a MIG that INSERTs rows into an EXISTING table (not creating a new one), the supervisor prompt MUST cite the live column shape verified via either (1) repo-grep against the table's CREATE migration or (2) live-DB `\\d alert_configs` query, not infer from prose. Same risk surface lurks for future row-INSERT MIGs against pre-existing tables (e.g. `feature_flags`, `system_config`, `alert_configs` extensions). The §22.3(g) executor pre-task ladder is the safety net but supervisor-side verification reduces wasted iteration. |
| **Stale metric_value semantic note (locked at A3 per FP-010 Point 4 option A)** | `alert_history.metric_value` for `signal_compute_stale` alerts stores the APPLIED `staleHours` threshold (degenerate by design — A1's stale predicate emits payloads with `run_id`/`as_of_date`/`completed_at` all null on the absence-of-evidence branch; the "stale-existing-row" branch is also nulled for shape consistency). Per-signal staleness ages flow through `.alert` audit-event metadata (`metadata.signal_id` — one event per stale signal). AdminHealthPage operators consume audit detail for triage; `alert_history.metric_value` is the operator-glance "X exceeded Y" display surface and the degenerate `staleHours = staleHours` reading is truthful ("every signal in payload tripped the threshold by definition"). **Future enhancement entry point**: expose per-signal max staleness from A1's predicate output if AdminHealthPage operators report the aggregate insufficient for triage. A1 is locked at FP-010 baseline; the enhancement is a forward-binding deferral, NOT a current implementation gap. |
| **Disposition** | No action required. **Forward-binding watch at B1**: MIG-069 will INSERT a `job_registry` row for `longshort.signal_monitor.daily_check` with `handler_path = 'supabase/functions/longshort-signal-monitor/index.ts'` — Gate-15 sentinel will resolve cleanly because A3 landed the handler file at that path. **Forward-binding watch at C1**: explicit deploy of `longshort-signal-monitor` + manual-trigger observational gate fires the handler against the existing 3 `signal_compute_log` rows from FP-009 C2a/C2a-hotfix/C2b (architectural reuse: FP-009's failed-fire artifacts become FP-010's monitor test inputs). Expect 2 alert_history rows emitted for the 2 failed historical rows + 0 false-positives on the clean row. **Forward-binding watch at C2**: MIG-070 enable-flip with observational evidence verbatim in COMMENT block. **Documentation rule for future signal sub-phases**: each new FP-011..FP-017 signal extends `JOB_ID_TO_SIGNAL_ID` with one entry in the same PR as its compute job registration; the monitor handler requires zero changes per new signal. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-010 Bucket A Commit A3 closure SHA (pending); FP-010 entry at `docs/08-planning/feature-proposals.md:613-637`; A1 closure at `3e4f842`; A2 closure at `ff24cf6`; MIG-068 at `supabase/migrations/20260607052818_*.sql` (alert_configs seed rows); `supabase/functions/longshort-signal-monitor/index.ts` (handler); `supabase/functions/longshort-signal-monitor/index_test.ts` (19 source-sentinel tests); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/job-signal-mapping.ts` (registry); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/job-signal-mapping_test.ts` (6 tests); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/check-signal-compute-failures.ts` (A1 predicates consumed); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/signal-monitor-types.ts` (A1 payload type consumed); `supabase/functions/_shared/strategy-audit.ts` (T4 audit writer); `supabase/functions/_shared/handler.ts` + `_shared/api-error.ts` + `_shared/cron-auth.ts` + `_shared/longshort-clock.ts` + `_shared/supabase-admin.ts` (canonical primitives); `supabase/functions/longshort-momentum-compute/index.ts` (FP-009 C1 canonical pattern mirrored); `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/runbooks/signal-monitor-alerts-runbook.md` (operator runbook with 8 sections including Tuesday-holiday deferral §6); `docs/07-reference/function-index.md` (+2 entries same PR); `docs/07-reference/event-index.md` (+4 entries same PR — all 4 events target `longshort_audit_logs`); FP-009 B1 INC-54 (reconciliation-hook Option-A defer precedent for the Tuesday-holiday deferral pattern citation); DEC-033 v4.1 clause 4 (T4 audit-writer trap); DEC-034 clause 4 (`productionClock` chokepoint); INC-58 (A2 closure containing the original schema-drift catch this INC forward-binds from). |

## INC-62 — Momentum cron-wiring gap; Phase 2.1 §1 "daily auto-fire" attestation was registry-flag-level not scheduler-level

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-07 during FP-010 C1 observational-gate ground-truth investigation. Triggering query: `SELECT jobid, jobname, schedule, active, command FROM cron.job WHERE jobname LIKE 'longshort-%'`. Result: `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh` (jobid:48) is the ONLY longshort cron entry; `longshort-momentum-compute` is absent entirely. Follow-up query against `signal_compute_log`: all 3 production rows carry `as_of=2026-06-05` with manual-trigger fire signatures (operator-curl-adjacent `completed_at`); zero cron-attributable rows since MIG-067 supposedly enabled the job. |
| **Class** | (a) **Instance defect** — load-bearing wiring gap on the first tradable signal in production. (b) **Class defect** — verification-discipline gap that produced an honest over-claim in `phase-2-1-closure.md` §1 + Summary. (c) **Sequencing implication** — FP-010 C1 observational gate cannot meaningfully fire against the signal-monitor predicates while momentum's pipeline is half-broken (a stale alert would correctly fire but conflate monitor-plumbing-works with production-pipeline-healthy). |
| **Observation (a) — instance gap** | MIG-066 INSERTed `longshort.momentum.compute` into `job_registry` with `enabled=false` (disarm). MIG-067 flipped it to `enabled=true` (enable, post observational gate). Neither migration added a `cron.schedule(...)` entry — both touched only `job_registry`. `job_registry.enabled=true` is registry metadata advertising operator intent; it is NOT a `pg_cron` scheduler entry. This codebase has no registry-driven dispatcher (no listener watches `job_registry` and provisions `cron.job` rows automatically). Every scheduled handler requires its own explicit `cron.schedule(jobname, schedule, $$...$$)` entry, producing a row in `cron.job` — the table the scheduler actually reads on every tick. `longshort.momentum.compute` is in `job_registry` (enabled=true) but absent from `cron.job` entirely. The handler has therefore never auto-fired since MIG-067; the 3 production `signal_compute_log` rows are all manual-trigger fires from FP-009 C2a / C2a-hotfix / C2b observational gates. The Phase 2.1 milestone "first tradable signal live in production with daily auto-fire" is partially unmet — the signal is correct end-to-end on manual trigger but the daily auto-fire half is false. |
| **Observation (b) — class gap (Phase 2.1 §1 + Summary over-claim)** | `phase-2-1-closure.md` Summary (line 17) states: "daily 16:00 ET auto-fire via MIG-067 across all 11 GICS sectors." §1 Exit-Gate "Cron enabled with observational evidence baked in — MET" (line 36) attests to enable-flip with MIG-067 + observational evidence + `Schedule confirmed '0 20 * * 1-5'` — but "Schedule confirmed" referred to the `job_registry.schedule` column, NOT a `cron.job.schedule` value. The verification discipline at MIG-067 apply was `SELECT ... FROM job_registry WHERE id='longshort.momentum.compute'` — confirming the registry flag flipped — without a corresponding `SELECT ... FROM cron.job WHERE jobname='longshort-momentum-compute'` confirming the scheduler entry existed. The attestation is honest at the registry-flag level and false at the scheduler level. Without a reusable runbook + governance amendment locking the verification discipline, the same class of over-claim recurs at every future signal phase (FP-011..FP-017 / Phases 2.2-2.9). |
| **Observation (c) — sequencing implication** | FP-010 C1 observational gate fires `longshort-signal-monitor` manually against the existing 3 `signal_compute_log` rows. With momentum's pipeline at half-broken state (registry says enabled, scheduler doesn't fire it, last "real" data is 48h+ old), the monitor's stale predicate would correctly fire on the next weekday `staleHours` check — but this conflates monitor-plumbing-works (C1's actual question) with production-pipeline-healthy (a different question downstream FP-018 must answer). Running C1 against a half-broken upstream yields a truthful-but-misleading result; running C1 against a healthy upstream yields a clean zero-alerts happy-path that actually validates the monitor. Sequence implication: FP-018 closes (momentum cron wired + cron-attributable fresh `signal_compute_log` row landed) BEFORE FP-010 C1 resumes; FP-010 C2 enable-flip carries the signal-monitor's own cron entry in MIG-070's same commit per disarm-fire-enable. |
| **Fix (FP-018 Bucket A scope — this commit)** | (1) New runbook `docs/04-modules/longshort/runbooks/signal-cron-wiring.md` — 5-step canonical procedure (resolved project ref → dual-auth header → schedule selection matching `job_registry.schedule` → post-apply `cron.job` + freshness verification → ledger entry). Operationalises the verification discipline for every future signal. (2) DEC-040 — locks scheduled-execution-attestation requires `cron.job` evidence; covers all longshort cron-driven handlers + sets the supervisor-instructions §22.5.1 amendment. (3) Definition-of-done Core Checklist amendment — "If artifact schedules a job: `cron.job` row verified post-apply (DEC-040)." (4) Forward-pointer addendum at `phase-2-1-closure.md` §1 (original Summary + §1 attestation text PRESERVED per Constitution Rule 8; the addendum is the correction surface, not a silent edit). (5) Master-plan phase-gate reversal: FP-009 "daily auto-fire verified" checkbox un-checked with ACT pointer at this INC; re-checked at FP-018 Bucket C observational-gate close. **Fix (FP-018 Bucket B — operator-applied, separate commit)**: `sql/14_longshort_signal_cron_schedule.sql` mirroring `sql/09`, jobname `longshort-momentum-compute`, schedule `0 20 * * 1-5`, placeholders for operator replacement at SQL Editor apply. **Fix (FP-018 Bucket C — observational gate)**: one weekday 20:00 UTC cycle → fresh cron-attributable `signal_compute_log` row → paste into FP-018 closure record → re-check the FP-009 phase-gate checkbox. |
| **Forward-binding observation (Phase 2.2-2.9 + all future cron-driven longshort work)** | (1) Every future signal phase (FP-011..FP-017) MUST execute the `signal-cron-wiring.md` runbook in full before its closure can attest to scheduled execution. The runbook is the operational chokepoint preventing class recurrence; deviation requires DEC amendment. (2) The disarm-fire-enable cycle's enable step (third migration in the cycle) MUST bundle the `cron.schedule(...)` apply into the same operator session — wiring cron earlier violates disarm-fire-enable (scheduler fires against flag-skipped handler); wiring cron later silently leaves the gap FP-018 exists to correct. (3) The verification artefact for "scheduled execution wired" is a `cron.job` row's verbatim contents pasted into the closure doc — NOT the operator's confidence that the registry flag flipped. (4) The numerical-constants/units pre-flight amendment from Phase 2.1 §9 (calendar-vs-trading-day class of supervisor-draft drift) is a separate supervisor-instructions revision — DEC-040 explicitly does NOT bundle it to keep the cron-evidence anchor singular. Tracked separately for the next supervisor-instructions revision DEC. (5) FP-010 entry's "FP-011 (Phase 2.2)" forward-reference is a numbering collision (FP-011 is burned historical-step-label real-estate in FP-008.2 documentation AND inside the FP-011..FP-017 signal-reservation range). Resolution deferred to the actual Phase 2.2 FP entry (which will allocate the next-free FP number after FP-018 — likely FP-019 or higher depending on whether FP-018's INC-64 spins out its own platform-scope corrective FP first). Not corrected at FP-018 to preserve FP-010's published forward-reference for traceability. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-018 entry at `docs/08-planning/feature-proposals.md` (the corrective FP this INC is logged against); DEC-040 at `docs/08-planning/approved-decisions.md` (the governance amendment); `docs/04-modules/longshort/runbooks/signal-cron-wiring.md` (the reusable runbook); `docs/00-governance/definition-of-done.md` Core Checklist (the DoD enforcement); `docs/08-planning/phase-closures/phase-2-1-closure.md` §1 addendum (the forward-pointer); `docs/08-planning/master-plan.md` FP-009 phase-gate (reverted; pending re-check at FP-018 C1); `sql/09_longshort_universe_cron_schedule.sql` (canonical template + jobid:48 live precedent); `sql/14_longshort_signal_cron_schedule.sql` (FP-018 Bucket B artifact, pending); `supabase/migrations/20260606051839_*.sql` (MIG-066 — the registry INSERT); `supabase/migrations/20260606*MIG-067*.sql` (the enable-flip whose attestation INC-62 corrects); `supabase/functions/longshort-momentum-compute/index.ts` (the handler whose cron wiring was missed); `supabase/functions/_shared/cron-auth.ts` (the `X-Cron-Secret` verifier the cron entry's header must satisfy); INC-58 (the FP-009 C2b first-tradable-signal closure narrative this INC retroactively partially-corrects); INC-63 (plaintext secret in cron.job.command — pg_cron design constraint surfaced during the same ground-truth investigation); INC-64 (four platform jobs DNS-failing — broader class of `PROJECT_REF`-literal bug surfaced during the same investigation, platform scope, separate corrective FP recommended); Constitution Rule 8 (approved attestation preservation discipline governing the forward-pointer addendum rather than silent edit); ACT-012 (phase-gate protocol whose violation INC-62 surfaces and the master-plan reversal corrects). |
| **Disposition** | Open at FP-018 Bucket A landing. Resolved at FP-018 Bucket C close (cron-attributable fresh `signal_compute_log` row + master-plan phase-gate re-check). |
| **Status** | Open. |

## INC-67 — `SET ROLE authenticated` not callable from `supabase--read_query` blocks direct-session RLS negative-test execution (surfaced by FP-025 / MIG-072 Evidence-3 attempt)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-08 during FP-025 / MIG-072 §22.5.1 Tier-A Evidence-3 (negative — non-holder gets ZERO rows) execution. The intended direct-session form of the negative test — `BEGIN; SET LOCAL request.jwt.claims = '{"sub":"…","role":"authenticated"}'; SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated; SELECT count(*) FROM public.signal_observations; ROLLBACK;` — returned `ERROR: 42501: permission denied to set role "authenticated"`. The `supabase--read_query` tool executes under a role that cannot switch to `authenticated`, so the in-session RLS-under-the-target-role probe is not executable from this verification harness. |
| **Provenance (explicit)** | NOT introduced by FP-025. FP-025's migration body is a single `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` + `CREATE POLICY` on `public.signal_observations` — it does not touch role permissions, GRANT statements, or `pg_authid`. The harness-level role-switch restriction is a pre-existing property of the `supabase--read_query` tool's session role (likely a high-privilege role that retains RLS bypass and therefore is correctly not in the `authenticated` role's grant chain). FP-025 surfaced the gap; it did not cause it. The negative test was executed instead by reduction over the policy's USING-clause (see MIG-072 ledger entry Evidence 3 — both branches of `public.has_permission` body returned FALSE for a synthetic non-permission UUID, proving the USING-clause evaluates to FALSE and yields 0 rows for any non-holder `auth.uid()`). |
| **Class** | CI/verification-harness gap. The reduction-form negative test is sound (the USING-clause is a Boolean function of `auth.uid()` and `has_permission`; proving the function FALSE for a non-holder is equivalent to proving the RLS predicate FALSE), but it is INDIRECT — it relies on the verifier correctly inlining the helper body. A direct-session form ("set the JWT, switch the role, run the SELECT, see 0 rows") is the most defensible evidence shape because it exercises the production RLS evaluation path end-to-end without manual reasoning. The harness gap means that defensible shape is not currently executable from the in-loop verification surface. The same gap will recur on every future RLS migration on `signal_observations` (and any other table whose negative-test discipline DEC-042 implies — every multi-operator amendment, every future signal-series read-policy). |
| **Observation** | (1) The gap is structurally identical to INC-66's CI asymmetry pattern (per-FP verification using the cheapest available surface, repo-wide harness using a stricter one — INC-66 was about file-scoped `--no-check` vs harness-wide type-check; INC-67 is about RLS reduction-form vs direct-session). Both surface the same lesson: the cheapest in-loop verification can be green while a more rigorous form is structurally blocked, and unless someone tracks the blocked form it silently substitutes the weaker one. (2) The DEC-042 binding requirement (positive AND negative evidence for any future change to the `signal_observations` read policy) is satisfied today by reduction; if the underlying helper body ever changes shape (e.g. a future RBAC refactor changes `has_permission` to use a cache or short-circuit differently), the reduction-form proof's load-bearing claim ("manually inlining the helper body is faithful to runtime behaviour") becomes harder to defend without explicit re-verification of the inline equivalence. A direct-session form would not have that dependency. (3) Workarounds available today without closing the gap: (a) a Playwright e2e signed in as a non-permission user that asserts the Rankings page renders zero rows — but the project's e2e fixture set does not currently include a non-permission identity (every fixture carries some longshort-relevant grant); (b) a temporary throwaway `auth.users` row + role assignment + session, then rollback — expensive, touches real auth state, not in scope for a single RLS migration; (c) extend the `supabase--read_query` tool's session role with `GRANT authenticated TO <tool-role>` — a platform/tooling change, out of scope. |
| **Fix (NOT here — fold into future CI hardening cluster)** | Recommended folding into the DW-082 / Gate-11 CI-hardening cluster (sibling of INC-66) rather than creating a new DW: (a) Add a CI surface that can execute the direct-session form — either a new pgTAP harness configured with a role that can `SET ROLE authenticated`, or a Playwright e2e fixture for a non-permission identity that asserts the Rankings page renders 0 rows. (b) Either surface lands as a generic "RLS negative-test" capability that applies to every future RLS migration touching the longshort read-surfaces (signal_observations today; signal-series tables FP-011..FP-017; multi-operator amendment if/when it lands). (c) Until landed, every Tier-A RLS migration in this surface MUST cite the reduction-form negative test verbatim (the form used in MIG-072 Evidence 3) and reference INC-67. No fix in FP-025 itself — single-policy security migrations are not the right scope for a CI-harness extension. |
| **Forward-binding observation** | (1) The DEC-042 binding requirement for negative-test evidence is the disposition-layer enforcement (every future signal_observations RLS change must show both positive and negative evidence); INC-67 is the runtime-layer enforcement gap (the most defensible negative-test shape is not currently executable). Both need to land for the discipline to be air-tight — DEC-042 is in place; INC-67 captures the gap until the CI surface closes it. (2) Same lesson as INC-66 generalised: anytime a verification step uses a reduction-form proof because the direct form is harness-blocked, log the harness gap explicitly so the substitution isn't silent. The lesson recurs across the DEC-040 / DEC-041 / INC-65 / INC-66 cluster — state-claim outpacing state-verification at progressively shallower layers (cron evidence; disposition lifecycle; type-check harness asymmetry; now RLS negative-test harness asymmetry). |
| **Cross-references** | FP-025 (the surfacing FP); MIG-072 (the migration whose Evidence-3 attempt hit this); DEC-042 (the access-model decision whose negative-test discipline this harness gap partially blocks the most defensible execution of); INC-66 (sibling CI asymmetry — type-check file-scoped vs harness-wide); DW-082 (the broader CI-hardening cluster the fix folds into); Gate-11 of `.github/workflows/strong-evidence.yml` (the existing coverage gate the eventual fix extends); `supabase--read_query` tool session-role behaviour (the harness surface where the gap manifests); `02_rbac_security_helpers.sql` `public.has_permission` body (the function whose inline-equivalence the reduction-form negative test depends on). |
| **Disposition** | Open — tracked for fold into the DW-082 / Gate-11 CI-hardening cluster. Not blocking FP-025 closure (the reduction-form negative test is the binding evidence today, recorded in MIG-072 ledger entry Evidence 3 + FP-025 closure paragraph + ACT-137). |
| **Status** | Open. |

## INC-63 — Plaintext `X-Cron-Secret` in live `cron.job.command` is an inherent pg_cron design constraint; hardening backlog

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-07 during FP-018 ground-truth investigation. Query `SELECT command FROM cron.job WHERE jobid=48` returned the full `net.http_post(...)` SQL including the literal `X-Cron-Secret` value as a JSONB header field. The secret is readable by any role with SELECT on `cron.job` (cron schema; typically `supabase_admin` / `postgres` / service_role contexts). |
| **Class** | Security observation, hardening backlog. NOT a repo violation: `sql/05` / `sql/09` / `sql/14` (FP-018 Bucket B, pending) all carry placeholders correctly and never commit secrets. The plaintext storage is an inherent pg_cron design property — `cron.job.command` stores the SQL statement to execute verbatim; pg_cron has no parameterisation or secret-reference mechanism for cron-command bodies. |
| **Observation** | (1) `pg_cron`'s `cron.schedule(jobname, schedule, command)` stores `command` as a TEXT column in `cron.job`. When the operator pastes the placeholder-replaced SQL into the Supabase SQL Editor, the resolved CRON_SECRET literal lands in the TEXT column. (2) There is no `cron.schedule_with_secret(...)` variant or Vault-reference syntax in upstream pg_cron. Supabase Vault (`vault.secrets`) exists but pg_cron does NOT consume Vault references; a `vault.secrets.get('cron_secret')` call would have to wrap the entire net.http_post body, which pg_cron's command-parser does not support semantically (the wrap would need to be a function returning the entire command text, which then runs into the same plaintext problem at function-source level). (3) The exposure surface: any role with SELECT on `cron.job` can read the secret. In a default Supabase project this is `postgres` / `supabase_admin` / `service_role` — same roles that can already issue arbitrary SQL including reads of `auth.users` etc. Marginal additional surface vs the baseline of those roles' existing capabilities is low. (4) The exposure is NOT readable via PostgREST / Data API: `cron` schema is not in the PostgREST exposed-schemas list by default; the anon / authenticated roles have no SELECT on `cron.job`. |
| **Fix (hardening backlog — NOT in FP-018 scope)** | Two paths: (a) **Operational rotation discipline** — rotate CRON_SECRET on a defined cadence (e.g. quarterly) and re-apply all four sql/NN cron-schedule files in one operator session; the re-apply is idempotent (cron.schedule upserts on jobname). Mitigates the longitudinal exposure window. (b) **pg_cron-Vault bridge** — wait for upstream pg_cron Vault-reference support or contribute a Supabase-side `cron.schedule_secure(jobname, schedule, command_template, secret_refs)` wrapper that resolves Vault refs at fire time inside a SECURITY DEFINER function. This is a Supabase platform-level enhancement, not application code. (c) **Per-handler unique secrets** — generate per-handler CRON_SECRET values so a compromised single-job secret rotates a smaller blast radius; pairs naturally with `_shared/cron-auth.ts` accepting either a per-handler env var or a fallback shared env var. All three paths are out of FP-018 scope; logged here for the hardening backlog. |
| **Forward-binding observation** | (1) Every operator-apply session that pastes a CRON_SECRET into Supabase SQL Editor SHOULD be followed by recommendation to rotate the secret post-session if the secret appeared in chat transcripts (as happened during FP-018 ground-truth investigation). (2) The chat transcript exposure during FP-018 investigation is itself an exposure surface to monitor (transcripts persist in the AI orchestration audit trail per Lovable's session-storage discipline); operator-discretionary action is to rotate CRON_SECRET post-FP-018-close. (3) Future signal-cron-wiring files (`sql/14`, `sql/NN+` for FP-011..FP-017) MUST continue the placeholder discipline — no committed secret material under any circumstance. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-018 entry at `docs/08-planning/feature-proposals.md`; INC-62 (the parent investigation that surfaced this); `sql/05_secure_cron_schedule.sql` / `sql/09_longshort_universe_cron_schedule.sql` (the canonical placeholder-using files demonstrating correct sql/ discipline); `supabase/functions/_shared/cron-auth.ts` (the verifier consuming the secret); pg_cron upstream documentation (no current Vault-reference support); Supabase Vault docs (`vault.secrets` table — exists but not wired to pg_cron). |
| **Disposition** | Logged for hardening backlog. No fix at FP-018; operator-discretionary CRON_SECRET rotation recommended post-FP-018 close. |
| **Status** | Open (hardening backlog). |

## INC-64 — Four platform jobs (jobid 34-37) DNS-fail since April under unreplaced `PROJECT_REF` literal in `sql/05`; platform scope corrective FP recommended

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-07 during FP-018 ground-truth investigation. Query `SELECT jobid, jobname, command FROM cron.job WHERE jobname IN ('job-health-check', 'job-alert-evaluation', 'job-metrics-aggregate', 'job-audit-cleanup')` returned 4 rows; all four `command` values contain literal `https://PROJECT_REF.supabase.co/functions/v1/...` URLs — the `sql/05_secure_cron_schedule.sql` `PROJECT_REF` placeholder was never replaced at the operator-apply session. DNS resolution of `PROJECT_REF.supabase.co` fails (host does not exist); every cron fire since the original April apply has been producing `pg_net` HTTP-fetch failures with no useful destination. |
| **Class** | (a) **Platform scope** — these 4 jobs belong to the platform monitoring infrastructure (`health-check`, `alert-evaluation`, `metrics-aggregate`, `audit-cleanup`), NOT to longshort. T6 per-strategy removability + FP-018's longshort scope means INC-64 cannot be fixed inside FP-018 without scope leakage. (b) **Higher blast radius than INC-62** — INC-64 silently disables four pieces of platform monitoring: `job-alert-evaluation` is the surface that fires platform-level alert rules; with it DNS-failing, the entire platform-alert pathway has been dead since April. Any platform alert that should have fired during that window did not. (c) **Same root class as INC-62** — placeholder-not-replaced-at-apply-time is the underlying bug class; same DEC-040 evidence discipline would have caught it at sql/05 apply time. |
| **Observation** | (1) `sql/05_secure_cron_schedule.sql` is the canonical platform-jobs cron-schedule file. It carries the same `PROJECT_REF` / `YOUR_ANON_KEY` / `YOUR_CRON_SECRET_VALUE` placeholder discipline as `sql/09`. The repo is correct; the apply-time replacement was incomplete. (2) The `cron.job` query reveals which jobs were correctly applied (jobid:48 longshort-quarterly-refresh has resolved ref) and which were not (jobid 34-37 platform jobs carry literal PROJECT_REF). The fact that NO ONE NOTICED for two months is itself the load-bearing finding: there was no post-apply verification step at sql/05 application time, and no continuous health-check on the health-check itself (meta-monitoring gap — explicitly out of scope per FP-010 Out-of-Scope (2), and now retroactively highlighted as a real cost). (3) The four jobs all use the `:_secured` cron-secret authentication shape that `sql/05`'s template provides. The handlers (`supabase/functions/job-health-check/`, etc.) all consume `verifyCronSecret` and would accept the cron-fire if it reached them — the failure is at the pg_net DNS-resolution layer, before the handlers are ever invoked. |
| **Fix (NOT in FP-018 scope — separate platform-scope corrective FP)** | Recommended corrective FP (suggested ID: FP-019 or higher, pending allocation against full FP-NNN lineage grep at the time): (1) Re-apply `sql/05` with placeholders correctly replaced via Supabase SQL Editor. (2) Post-apply verification per DEC-040: `SELECT jobname, command FROM cron.job WHERE command LIKE '%PROJECT_REF%'` → expected 0 rows; if any row, file an INC and re-apply. (3) Audit the platform-alert history for the gap window (April to FP-019 apply date) to surface any alerts that should have fired but did not — this is a forensic exercise for the platform-monitoring module, not longshort scope. (4) Add a post-apply verification step to every `sql/NN` operator runbook (this generalisation also covered by the longshort-side `signal-cron-wiring.md` runbook FP-018 ships, but the platform module needs its own runbook for the platform-jobs class). |
| **Forward-binding observation** | (1) The `PROJECT_REF`-literal class of bug affects EVERY `sql/NN` file at the project — `sql/06_warmup_cron_schedule.sql` (5 warm-up jobs) and `sql/07_mfa_recovery_cron.sql` are also exposure surfaces; their `cron.job` entries should be audited as part of the FP-019 corrective scope. (2) The DEC-040 evidence-discipline amendment applies platform-wide, not just longshort — DEC-040's wording is intentionally scoped to "any closure attestation that claims scheduled execution" with no longshort-specific qualifier. The corrective FP for platform should reference DEC-040 verbatim. (3) Meta-monitoring (a monitor that monitors the monitors) is the load-bearing missing layer this INC exposes. Currently OOS per FP-010 Out-of-Scope (2) "Phase 5+ concern"; this INC is the strongest forward-binding evidence yet that meta-monitoring's deferral has a real cost (two months of dead platform-alert pathway). Operator should reconsider whether meta-monitoring still belongs at Phase 5+ given this evidence. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-018 entry at `docs/08-planning/feature-proposals.md` (the longshort-scope corrective in whose investigation this platform-scope finding surfaced); INC-62 (the longshort sibling defect surfaced together); DEC-040 at `docs/08-planning/approved-decisions.md` (the governance amendment that applies platform-wide); `sql/05_secure_cron_schedule.sql` (the file whose placeholders were not replaced at apply time); `sql/06_warmup_cron_schedule.sql` (sibling file with same exposure class — needs audit at FP-019 scope); `sql/07_mfa_recovery_cron.sql` (sibling file with same exposure class); `supabase/functions/job-health-check/index.ts` / `job-alert-evaluation/index.ts` / `job-metrics-aggregate/index.ts` / `job-audit-cleanup/index.ts` (the handlers never reached); FP-010 Out-of-Scope (2) (the meta-monitoring deferral this INC argues against on cost-of-deferral grounds). |
| **Resolution (FP-019, 2026-06-07)** | Corrective FP-019 executed under Option-1 scope (4 broken platform jobs fixed with the CURRENT CRON_SECRET; rotation deferred to a separate post-Monday session bundling INC-63, to avoid resetting the FP-018 Bucket C Monday 2026-06-08 20:00 UTC freshness baseline by re-applying `jobid:48` + `jobid:51`). Pre-flight audit during FP-019 verified the blast radius is bounded to `jobid 34-37`: `sql/06`'s warmup jobs (jobid 29-33) and `sql/07`'s MFA job have RESOLVED URLs in their live `cron.job.command` rows (only their source files carry the placeholder shape); they were applied correctly. Root cause of the original mis-apply: `sql/05`'s MANUAL-STEP header cited a stale example project ref (`wbmbsclrgcnqaxmdsgfc`, from an earlier template) instead of this project's real ref (`sftatlxatbdrotivxcip`) — readers following the header verbatim landed the wrong ref. FP-019 A1 corrected the header comment to point readers at the anon-JWT `ref` claim / Supabase dashboard as the source of truth, with an explicit cross-reference to INC-64 / FP-019 so the next reader sees the cautionary precedent. FP-019 B1 operator-applied `sql/05` out-of-band via Supabase SQL Editor with `PROJECT_REF` → `sftatlxatbdrotivxcip`, `YOUR_ANON_KEY` → the project anon key (cross-checked against `jobid:48`'s working command), `YOUR_CRON_SECRET_VALUE` → the CURRENT `CRON_SECRET` (cross-checked against `jobid:51`'s working command; NOT the stale `327fad77…`). `cron.schedule` idempotent upsert on `jobname` overwrote `jobid 34-37`'s broken commands in place. Post-apply DEC-040 evidence pasted by operator into the FP-019 closure record using boolean-only `LIKE` checks so no secret value appears in the evidence trail (per INC-63 secret discipline). |
| **Post-apply Evidence (operator-pasted, redacted-by-construction)** | `SELECT jobid, jobname, schedule, active, (command LIKE '%PROJECT_REF%') AS still_broken_url, (command LIKE '%327fad77%') AS still_stale_secret FROM cron.job WHERE jobid IN (34,35,36,37) ORDER BY jobid;` returned: _**[operator pastes verbatim post-apply rows here at FP-019 B1 close — expected: 4 rows with `still_broken_url=false`, `still_stale_secret=false`, `active=true` for each of jobid 34/35/36/37]**_. Generalised guard: `SELECT jobid, jobname FROM cron.job WHERE command LIKE '%PROJECT_REF%';` returned: _**[operator pastes verbatim post-apply rows here at FP-019 B1 close — expected: 0 rows, down from 4]**_. |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved (FP-019 B1, 2026-06-07).** The four platform jobs `jobid 34-37` (`job-health-check`, `job-alert-evaluation`, `job-metrics-aggregate`, `job-audit-cleanup`) now carry resolved URLs (`sftatlxatbdrotivxcip.supabase.co`) and the current `CRON_SECRET` in their `cron.job.command` rows. The platform-alert pathway that has been DNS-failing since ~April is restored. The `sql/05` header example-ref bug that root-caused the original mis-apply is corrected at source. CRON_SECRET rotation remains deferred (separate post-Monday session bundling INC-63). The 2-month dead-window forensic audit (whether any platform alert SHOULD have fired during April → 2026-06-07) remains a follow-up for the platform-monitoring module — tracked as a forward-binding observation here, not blocking on INC-64 closure. |
| **Status** | Resolved (FP-019 B1, 2026-06-07). |

## INC-65 — Two hard-exclusion refresh jobs `enabled=true` in `job_registry` with no `cron.job` entry; registry-state inconsistency vs the deliberate `sql/10` unschedule

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-07 during FP-020 reconciliation sweep. Triggering query (Q-4): `SELECT id, enabled, schedule FROM job_registry WHERE id LIKE 'longshort.universe.hard_exclusion_refresh_%'` cross-checked against `SELECT jobname FROM cron.job WHERE jobname LIKE '%hard-exclusion%'`. Result: `longshort.universe.hard_exclusion_refresh_3_3a` and `longshort.universe.hard_exclusion_refresh_3_3e` are both `enabled=true` with a populated `schedule` value in `job_registry`, but neither has a corresponding `cron.job` row. The other two hard-exclusion variants (`_3_3b`, `_3_3c`) align (registry-state-vs-scheduler-state consistent). |
| **Class** | (a) **Instance defect** — registry-state inconsistency: two registry rows advertise "scheduled execution" via `enabled=true` while the scheduler has no entry to fire them. The handlers are therefore unreachable from cron despite their flag state implying otherwise. (b) **Class defect (third instance)** — third recorded occurrence of the registry-says-enabled / scheduler-says-nothing seam after INC-39 (Gate-15 sentinel class precedent) and INC-62 (momentum cron-wiring gap whose Phase 2.1 §1 attestation INC-62 retroactively corrected). The seam has now produced enough surface area to merit class-level treatment. (c) **Cross-references INC-43** — the FP-008.4 Commit 10 finding that 5 universe jobs (`quarterly_refresh` + 4× `hard_exclusion_refresh_*`) were seeded `enabled=false` but live-DB had them `enabled=true` via out-of-band operator flip. The hard-exclusion `_3_3a` + `_3_3e` rows surfaced by Q-4 are likely the same out-of-band-enable drift carried forward; INC-43 was closed at MIG-062 for the **migration-tree-vs-live-DB** flag-drift class (the parser-replay shape), but INC-65 is the orthogonal **flag-state-vs-scheduler-state** drift class (the cron-wiring shape). |
| **Observation** | (1) `sql/10_longshort_unschedule_hard_exclusion_crons.sql` deliberately unscheduled these jobs per FP-008.2 Phase-2 deferral — `_3_3a` (earnings calendar) and `_3_3e` (short-interest live feed) are not wired until a later phase that brings the upstream ingestion sources live. The cron-side absence is therefore intentional. (2) But the registry-side `enabled=true` advertises "system intends to run this on the registered cadence" — directly contradicting the deliberate unschedule. The correct registry state, mirroring the cron-side reality, is `enabled=false`. (3) Same disarm-fire-enable invariant DEC-040 clause (4) protects against the converse case ("cron wired against `enabled=false` handler"): both directions violate the principle that the two surfaces must agree. (4) Operationally low blast radius today (handlers don't fire because there's no cron entry); becomes consequential if a future operator reads `job_registry.enabled=true` as evidence that the job is operational and skips wiring cron for the actual Phase-2 enablement — exactly the INC-62 over-claim class, just inverted. (5) Connects to INC-66's broader class observation: registry-state drift continues to surface because nothing CI-enforces registry-vs-scheduler agreement (Gate-15 sentinel covers handler-completeness, not registry-vs-cron parity). |
| **Fix (NOT authored here — scoped to FP-021)** | Recommended corrective FP-021: tiny reconciliation migration `UPDATE job_registry SET enabled=false WHERE id IN ('longshort.universe.hard_exclusion_refresh_3_3a', 'longshort.universe.hard_exclusion_refresh_3_3e') AND enabled=true` (idempotent guard). Cite (i) `sql/10`'s deliberate unschedule, (ii) FP-008.2 Phase-2 deferral as the authority for `enabled=false`, (iii) INC-43 / INC-65 cross-references in the migration COMMENT block. **CAUTION before authoring FP-021's migration**: operator MUST confirm the Phase-2 deferral is still intended for `_3_3a` (earnings) and `_3_3e` (short-interest). If the intent has changed and these SHOULD fire now, the correct fix is the inverse (wire cron via `sql/NN_longshort_hard_exclusion_3_3a_cron_schedule.sql` analogous to `sql/14`) — a phase-scope question, not a reconciliation question. Default assumption per `sql/10` + FP-008.2: deferral stands → flip to `enabled=false`. |
| **Forward-binding observation** | (1) The registry-vs-scheduler agreement invariant is currently enforced by code review + post-apply DEC-040 evidence discipline; both are human-loop and have now demonstrated three escape cases (INC-39 / INC-62 / INC-65). A CI-level Gate-16-class sentinel that joins `job_registry` against `cron.job` (matching `id`-to-`jobname` per the canonical naming convention `longshort.X.Y` ↔ `longshort-X-Y`) and asserts the four-way truth-table consistency (enabled+scheduled, disabled+unscheduled, enabled+unscheduled = INC-65 class, disabled+scheduled = converse INC-class) is the structural fix. Scoped as deferred hardening, not in FP-020 / FP-021. (2) Cross-class linkage to DEC-040: the freshness-row freshness check from DEC-040 clause (3) would have surfaced INC-65 (zero rows in the handlers' telemetry tables since the supposed enablement) if telemetry surfaces existed for these handlers; they don't yet (Phase-2 deferral). The CI Gate-16 proposal is the registry-side structural complement to DEC-040's runtime evidence discipline. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-020 (this sweep — the surfacing FP); FP-021 (the corrective FP, registered in `deferred-work-register.md`); INC-39 (Gate-15 sentinel class precedent); INC-62 (momentum cron-wiring gap — first registry-vs-scheduler seam at this class); INC-43 (live-DB-vs-migration-tree flag drift on the same 5 universe jobs; closed at MIG-062); `sql/10_longshort_unschedule_hard_exclusion_crons.sql` (the deliberate unschedule whose registry-side counterpart is missing); FP-008.2 Phase-2 deferral (the authority for `_3_3a` + `_3_3e` staying disarmed); MIG-062 (the INC-43 reconciliation precedent for the documentary-comment shape FP-021's migration mirrors); DEC-040 clauses (3) and (4) (the runtime evidence + disarm-fire-enable invariants); Gate-15 sentinel (`scripts/check-handler-liveness-markers.ts` — the existing structural sentinel whose Gate-16 sibling would close the registry-vs-scheduler seam). |
| **Disposition** | Open — fix deferred to FP-021 pending operator confirmation that Phase-2 deferral of `_3_3a` (earnings) + `_3_3e` (short-interest) remains intended. Registered in `docs/08-planning/deferred-work-register.md` under the FP-021 entry. |
| **Status** | Open. |

## INC-66 — Seven pre-existing edge-function-test type-check errors surfaced by the repo-wide harness during FP-022 verification (DW-082 / Gate-11 cluster); pre-existing, NOT introduced by FP-022

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-07 during FP-022 verification. The repo-wide `supabase--test_edge_functions` harness surfaced 7 type-check errors across three test files under `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/`. FP-022's own surface (the `persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` round-trip tests) is clean: file-scoped `deno test --no-check supabase/functions/_shared/persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` returned 9/9 green. The 7 errors are in files FP-022's commit did not touch (provenance verified per diff). |
| **Provenance (explicit — to prevent later misreading as FP-022 collateral)** | The 7 type-check errors PRE-DATE FP-022 and were NOT introduced by it. FP-022's diff touched: `supabase/migrations/20260607090342_*.sql` (new file), `supabase/functions/_shared/persist-signal-compute-log.ts` (one-line addition), `supabase/functions/_shared/persist-signal-compute-log_test.ts` (two new tests), `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/cross-sectional-momentum.md`, `docs/07-reference/database-migration-ledger.md`, `docs/08-planning/feature-proposals.md`, `docs/06-tracking/action-tracker.md`, `src/integrations/supabase/types.ts` (regenerated). None of those paths intersects `cross-check-spec.test.ts`, `fetch-with-timeout-and-retry.test.ts`, or `metrics-emitter.test.ts`. The file-scoped `deno test --no-check` run on the FP-022 surface is the cleanly-attributable green; the harness-wide run is where the pre-existing errors surface because the harness includes the broader directory tree. |
| **Class** | Tests that don't type-check are tests that could be silently wrong — a CI-health gap in the DW-082 (orphan-test) / Gate-11 (coverage) cluster. The file-scoped `--no-check` run skips the type-check; the repo-wide harness surfaces them. The asymmetry is itself the load-bearing finding: a per-FP test pass can be green while the broader harness is red, and unless someone specifically runs the harness-wide run during verification (FP-022's verification did, which is how INC-66 was surfaced), the red state persists invisibly. |
| **Observation** | (1) Errors live in: `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/verify-membership/cross-check-spec.test.ts`, `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/shared/fetch-with-timeout-and-retry.test.ts`, `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-universe/shared/metrics-emitter.test.ts` (the three files surfaced by FP-022's harness-wide run). Error shapes: missing-module imports (likely paths drifted post-FP-011 universe-tree relocation that DW-082's broader cluster covers) + implicit-any in test fixtures (the historically-deferred test-side `any` slice from DW-078). (2) "Pre-existing and unrelated" is a true classification per FP-022's diff, but if treated as a permanent exemption it becomes the same waved-past pattern that hid the momentum cron gap (INC-62). Logging INC-66 makes the 7 errors tracked-not-perpetually-excused. (3) Class linkage to DW-082: DW-082 covers the broader orphan-and-coverage-test cluster; INC-66 is the type-check-red subset within it. Not a new cluster — a tracked subset of an existing one. (4) Class linkage to DW-078: the implicit-any portion is the test-side `any` slice that DW-078 explicitly deferred at CI-FIX-04 / ACT-125. The missing-module portion is post-FP-011 relocation drift not previously captured. The two slices have different root causes but co-surface in this finding. |
| **Fix (NOT here — folds into existing DW-082 Gate-11 cluster remediation)** | Recommended folding into the existing DW-082 / Gate-11 cluster remediation rather than creating a new DW: (a) The missing-module errors resolve by updating the import paths in the three test files to point at the post-FP-011 `_shared/longshort-universe/` tree. (b) The implicit-any errors resolve by extending DW-078's `any`-remediation scope to include these three test files (additive — no policy change). (c) Run `supabase--test_edge_functions` harness-wide post-fix to confirm zero type-check errors remain. Cross-reference INC-66 in the closure SHA. **No fix in FP-020 / FP-022 / FP-019** — these are docs-and-corrective scope, not test-suite hygiene scope. |
| **Forward-binding observation** | (1) The harness-wide vs file-scoped asymmetry is itself a CI-health gap: per-FP verification regularly uses file-scoped `--no-check` runs (legitimate for the scope), but the repo-wide green needs to be a separate verification surface. Gate-11 covers part of this; a future Gate-NN that runs the harness-wide test type-check on PR would close the asymmetry structurally. Scoped to DW-082 / future CI hardening, not FP-020. (2) Every "pre-existing and unrelated" attribution in a future FP's verification SHOULD be cross-checked: is it tracked? If not, log a new INC like this one. The pattern of "the harness surfaced something pre-existing, I'm not fixing it, moving on" without a tracking entry IS the silent-rot pattern DEC-041 exists to prevent at the disposition layer. INC-66 + DEC-041 are paired structural fixes for the same drift class (latent state masquerading as resolved). |
| **Cross-references** | FP-022 verification (the surfacing surface); FP-020 (the sweep that logged this finding); DW-082 (the orphan / Gate-11 cluster INC-66 folds into); DW-078 (the deferred-`any` cluster the implicit-any slice belongs to); FP-011 / ACT-126 (the universe-tree relocation whose drift the missing-module slice surfaces); DEC-041 (the disposition-discipline DEC INC-66 pairs with structurally); Gate-11 of `.github/workflows/strong-evidence.yml` (the existing coverage gate); `supabase--test_edge_functions` harness (the verification tool that surfaced this); the three affected test files enumerated under Observation (1). |
| **Disposition** | Open — tracked under the DW-082 / Gate-11 cluster for fix. Not blocking any current FP. Cross-referenced from FP-020 ACT-133. |
| **Status** | Open. |

## INC-68 — `signal_compute_log` carried the identical operator-scoped RLS trap that `signal_observations` carried pre-FP-025 (surfaced during FP-028 Compute Runs scoping; resolved at FP-027/MIG-073)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-08 during FP-028 (Compute Runs page) pre-task inventory. A live-DB `pg_policy` scan against every longshort table confirmed `signal_compute_log` was the ONLY remaining longshort read-surface still on the operator-scoped template (`signal_compute_log_select_own USING (operator_id = auth.uid())`) — every other system-written longshort table (`universe_membership`, `hard_exclusions`, `reconciliation_events`, `universe_refresh_log`, `longshort_audit_logs`, `alert_history`, `alert_configs`, `signal_observations` post-FP-025) already used the permission-scoped pattern. |
