| **Provenance** | Same root cause as the FP-025 finding on `signal_observations`: template-copy at table creation (MIG-065 `signal_compute_log` CREATE inherited the operator-scoped read policy from a now-superseded template, despite all sibling system-written tables having moved to permission-scoped reads). |
| **Class** | Structural RLS template drift on system-written tables — twice surfaced (FP-025 / FP-027), now structurally addressed by the pattern note added to `docs/04-modules/longshort/longshort.md` ("System-Written Tables: Permission-Scoped Reads Only"). |
| **Resolution (FP-027, 2026-06-08)** | MIG-073 swapped the operator-scoped policy for `has_permission(auth.uid(),'longshort.view')` — byte-equivalent to MIG-072. §22.5.1 Evidence 1/2/3 all green (see MIG-073 ledger entry). The longshort module doc now binds future system-written tables to the permission-scoped pattern at creation; recurrence is a CI-class regression. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-027 (the corrective FP); MIG-073 (the migration); FP-025 / MIG-072 (the matched-pair precedent); DEC-042 (the access-model decision applied); INC-67 (re-cited — same harness-gap manifests during Evidence 3); FP-028 (the surfacing FP — Compute Runs page would have rendered blank without FP-027). |
| **Disposition** | Resolved (FP-027 / MIG-073). |
| **Status** | Resolved. |

## INC-69 — Total cron-auth outage: every cron consumer (jobids 34/35/36/37/48/51) returned 401 Unauthorized; `cron.job_run_details.status='succeeded'` concealed the failure for an unknown duration

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-08 during an FP-018 Bucket C controlled test-fire of `longshort-momentum-compute` (jobid:51). The test-fire returned 401 `{"error":"Unauthorized"}`. Follow-on `net._http_response` scan across the retention window returned **793× 401, 450× 405 (warmup), zero 200s** for every cron consumer — `job-health-check` (34), `job-alert-evaluation` (35), `job-metrics-aggregate` (36), `job-audit-cleanup` (37), `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh` (48), `longshort-momentum-compute` (51). **No cron job had EVER authenticated** in the retention window. |
| **Detection path (load-bearing)** | A controlled test-fire of jobid:51 (for FP-018 Bucket C end-to-end attestation) surfaced the 401. Passive observation of `cron.job_run_details` would NOT have surfaced it — `status='succeeded'` was uniformly green because that column proves only that `net.http_post` DISPATCHED, NOT that the HTTP response was 2xx. The 20:00 UTC passive-observation gate originally planned for FP-018 Bucket C would have continued to read green against a totally-broken auth path. **Class lesson: `cron.job_run_details.status='succeeded'` is necessary-but-insufficient evidence for "cron job is working" — see DEC-043.** |
| **Class** | Total cron-auth outage — multi-job, cross-module (platform monitoring + longshort), Tier-A blast radius. **Distinct from INC-62 / INC-64 / INC-63**: INC-62 was the missing `cron.job` row (scheduling gap); INC-64 was unreplaced `PROJECT_REF` (URL DNS-fail); INC-63 was the plaintext-secret design constraint. INC-69 is the **authentication** gap — `cron.job` rows existed, URLs resolved, but the `X-Cron-Secret` header value sent by every cron command did not match the deployed `CRON_SECRET` env var, AND the env var itself did not match either of the two divergent secret values being sent (jobs 34/35/36/37/51 sent `ee867b97…`; job 48 sent `076426…`; deployed env held a third value). Compounded by INC-63's redeploy-required constraint: env binds at deploy time, so even after the env was set to a matching value, deployed function instances continued to hold the old value until redeployed. |
| **Blast radius** | (a) Platform monitoring silently dead — `job-health-check`, `job-alert-evaluation`, `job-metrics-aggregate`, `job-audit-cleanup` returned 401 on every fire for the entire retention window. Any platform alert that should have fired during that window did not. (b) Longshort universe-quarterly (jobid:48) cron auth never worked end-to-end; all `universe_refresh_log` rows in the retention window are MANUAL signatures, never cron-attributable (forward-pointer correction to FP-009's prior "live and working" attestation — see FP-009 Addendum below). (c) Longshort momentum (jobid:51) cron auth never worked end-to-end until 2026-06-08; FP-018 Bucket C attestation against passive-observation evidence would have been false-green. (d) Concealing-metric finding compounds (a)+(b)+(c): the team's existing operational dashboards (which surface `cron.job_run_details.status`) read green throughout, providing zero signal of the underlying outage. |
| **Root cause** | Three concurrent defects, none alone sufficient, all three together produced the 401-for-everything state: (1) **Secret divergence across cron commands.** Five jobs (34/35/36/37/51) sent `ee867b97…`; jobid:48 sent `076426…`. No single canonical secret was being sent. (2) **Env-var ≠ either command secret.** The deployed `CRON_SECRET` env var held a third value matching neither cron command's header. (3) **INC-63 redeploy-required constraint.** Even when the env var was updated to match, deployed function instances continued to read the OLD value at runtime — `Deno.env.get('CRON_SECRET')` resolves at function-instance startup, not per-request. A redeploy is required for the new env value to bind. The earlier FP-019 corrective aligned 5 command-sides to `ee867b97…` but (a) did not touch jobid:48, and (b) did not complete the env-var + redeploy step — net effect was 0% cron auth success until the FP-039 unified remediation. |
| **Resolution (FP-039, operator-run + verified 2026-06-08)** | One atomic operator-run remediation per FP-039 scope: (1) generated a new canonical `CRON_SECRET` value; (2) updated the Supabase Edge Function secret to that value; (3) redeployed all 6 consumers (`longshort-momentum-compute`, `longshort-universe-quarterly-refresh`, `job-health-check`, `job-alert-evaluation`, `job-metrics-aggregate`, `job-audit-cleanup`) so they bind the new env at runtime; (4) reconciled all 6 cron commands via `cron.schedule(...)` idempotent upserts so every `X-Cron-Secret` header carries the canonical value. Per §22.5.3, this was a plaintext-secret out-of-band operator session — no MIG allocation. |
| **End-to-end evidence (the new DEC-043 standard, verbatim)** | (a) **Platform jobs 34/35** — returned **200 `{"state":"succeeded","success":true}`** on their normal schedule 2026-06-08 10:02–11:58 UTC (first cron-attributable 200s in the retention window). (b) **Momentum jobid:51** — test-fired and produced **two cron-attributable `signal_compute_log` rows**: `completed_at` 2026-06-08 11:52:00 UTC and 11:54:00 UTC, `as_of_date` 2026-06-08, `outcome=completed`, `persisted_count=834`. These are **real wall-clock timestamps**, distinct from the midnight-manual signature shape (`completed_at`-at-`as_of`-midnight) of every prior `signal_compute_log` row. (c) **Byte-match attestation (jobid:51)** — exactly 1 `cron.job` row; `schedule = '0 20 * * 1-5'` byte-identical to `job_registry.schedule` for `longshort.momentum.compute` (`enabled=true`); `active=true`; `command` carries resolved project ref (no `PROJECT_REF` literal) and a real secret (no `<YOUR_CRON_SECRET>` placeholder). DEC-040 + DEC-043 satisfied. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-039 (the corrective FP this INC is logged against); DEC-043 (the new attestation-discipline DEC this incident motivates); DEC-040 (the prior `cron.job`-evidence DEC, retroactively reframed: necessary-but-insufficient — DEC-043 adds the end-to-end clause); INC-63 (the plaintext-secret + redeploy-required parent constraint); INC-62 (the prior `cron.job`-missing class — distinct class from INC-69); INC-64 (the prior `PROJECT_REF`-unreplaced class — distinct class from INC-69); FP-018 Bucket C (the surfacing surface; Bucket C now closeable per ACT-130 on this end-to-end evidence instead of the originally-planned passive 20:00 observation); FP-019 (the partial earlier corrective whose ineffectiveness motivated FP-039 — see FP-019 Addendum); FP-009 (whose prior "live and working" cron attestation was MANUAL-only — see FP-009 Addendum); DW-091 (the deferred CRON_SECRET rotation — updated, NOT closed, because three secret values have appeared in chat transcripts and the current canonical secret authenticates 6 production cron consumers); `supabase/functions/_shared/cron-auth.ts` (the verifier whose `provided !== expected` branch was returning 401 for every cron fire); `sql/14_longshort_signal_cron_schedule.sql` (header comment corrected — the prior "jobid:48 active, fires daily, no DNS-fail" canonical-live-verified claim was false; jobid:51 is now the canonical live-verified pattern as of 2026-06-08 11:52/11:54 UTC). |
| **Disposition** | **Resolved (FP-039 / operator-run, 2026-06-08).** The 6-consumer cron-auth path is restored end-to-end. Bucket C of FP-018 is closeable per ACT-130 on the 11:52/11:54 evidence. Forward-binding obligations: DEC-043 is now the binding attestation standard for any future cron/scheduled-job closure; DW-091 remains open (rotation deferred — accepted logged risk with a hard tripwire to close before Phase 8). |
| **Status** | Resolved (FP-039, 2026-06-08). |

## INC-70 — Polygon `/stocks/filings/vX/form-4` silently ignores `ticker` + `transaction_date` filters at our entitlement tier; FP-042 Signal #4 produced a phantom signal until ACT-155's market-wide rewrite forced the discovery

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-09 during the FP-042 second post-deploy investigation. The ACT-155 market-wide rewrite hit a pagination-cap throw (50 pages × 1000 rows = 50k rows on a 90-day window — far above the 5k–15k row estimate baked into the fetcher's header comment). A 9-variant probe of `/stocks/filings/vX/form-4` against the deployed `POLYGON_API_KEY` returned **byte-identical 1,852,139-byte / 1000-row responses** for: `ticker=AAPL` (baseline), `ticker.any_of=AAPL,MSFT,NVDA`, `ticker=AAPL,MSFT,NVDA` (comma), `ticker.in=AAPL,MSFT,NVDA`, `ticker=AAPL|MSFT|NVDA` (pipe), `ticker=AAPL&ticker=MSFT&ticker=NVDA` (repeated), market-wide 90-day, and **market-wide 14-day** (same byte-identical 90-day response — date window also ignored). `limit=100` was the ONLY parameter honored (returned 100 rows / 189,466 bytes). |
| **Smoking gun** | The 1000-row response to `ticker=AAPL` did NOT contain AAPL — top issuer buckets were DELL=312, FCNCA=41, SSMR=38, CXDO=16 — high-filing-volume names recently active. The endpoint is returning a **fixed firehose** (apparently ordered by descending filing recency) and treating `ticker=` / `ticker.any_of=` / `transaction_date.*` as no-ops. The probe edge function (`longshort-form4-probe`) was deployed, queried, and deleted in the same investigation; source file removed. |
| **Severity** | **High — data-correctness / phantom signal.** The original FP-042 per-ticker fetcher was silently broken since day one of deploy: it fetched the same firehose 839 times and locally filtered by `tickers[0] === ticker`, which would yield 0 matches for almost every mid/large-cap name (since the firehose is dominated by recent high-volume filers, not our S&P 900 universe). The compute layer dutifully marked each name `no_qualifying_transactions` — perfectly indistinguishable from the EXPECTED sparse profile for insider data. **The signal looked like it was working but was producing systematically wrong (mostly-empty) data.** No alarm fired because the spec explicitly says most names should be sparse. |
| **Lucky trigger** | The signal-correctness bug would have remained invisible indefinitely if not for the ACT-155 market-wide rewrite hitting the CPU page-cap and forcing the investigation. **A CPU/operational failure exposed a silent data-correctness failure.** Class lesson: operational failures should be treated as investigation opportunities, not just as problems to route around — the symptom that surfaces is often not the most important one underneath. |
| **Class** | Vendor-endpoint filter silently ignored; client-side filtering then producing wrong data while looking correct. Distinct from a 403/404/429/5xx failure (which would have surfaced immediately as a typed skip) — silently-honored-but-broken filters are the most dangerous failure mode because the response status code is 200 and the payload is well-formed. |
| **Fix (this PR — ACT-156)** | (1) Signal #4 disarmed in `signal_registry` (`status='live'` → `'planned'`); job_registry row stays `enabled=false`. The combiner imputes the non-critical signal's absence per §6.5; the rest of the signal stack runs unaffected. (2) Orchestrator catch-block double-tally bug (P2) fixed in the same PR: a market-wide throw now returns `outcome='failed'` with a single `failure_reason`, no per-ticker skips, no fall-through into the qualifying-filter loop (was emitting both `fetch_error` AND `no_qualifying_transactions` per ticker → 1678 skips for 839-ticker universe; run_id `1021808b-4b1c-4b04-bb45-d989d56b5193` is the diagnostic exhibit). (3) FP-042 compute / classifier / filter / z-score code is untouched — it is correct and will be reused with the EDGAR-direct fetcher. Only the data-acquisition layer needs replacing. |
| **Future fix (deferred)** | DW-094 — Rebuild Signal #4's Form 4 fetcher on SEC EDGAR direct (`company_tickers.json` for ticker→CIK, `data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK{10}.json` filtered to form type 4, parse Form 4 XML to per-transaction rows). EDGAR is the original source Polygon resells; it has authoritative per-CIK filtering, no entitlement, well-documented schemas. Requires a `User-Agent` header with a valid contact email (else 403) and respects a 10 req/sec limit. The FP-042 compute/classifier/filter/z-score layer is correct + reused as-is. |
| **Pattern lesson (codified — highest-value output)** | A `verifyFilterHonored()` discipline becomes a mandatory pre-flight for every vendor-endpoint fetcher: before trusting ANY documented filter (ticker, date, type), probe the endpoint with a filter value that SHOULD return zero rows (e.g. `ticker=ZZZZZZZZ`, or a far-future date) and confirm the response is empty — NOT a firehose. If a should-be-empty query returns data, the filter is silently ignored and client-side filtering will silently produce wrong data. This would have caught INC-70 on day one of FP-042. Documented at [`docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md`](../04-modules/longshort/signals/_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md) and bound as a §22.3-style pre-flight item for the remaining feed-signal FPs (#1 analyst, #2 PEAD, #3 options, #8 news — each has its own vendor endpoint whose filters must be probed before trust). |
| **Cross-references** | FP-042 (the implementing FP — compute layer correct, fetcher layer replaced); ACT-154 (original FP-042 ship); ACT-155 (the market-wide rewrite whose pagination-cap throw exposed this); ACT-156 (this PR — the P2 double-tally fix + Signal #4 disarm + governance); DW-093 (DEF-14A NEO enrichment — orthogonal upgrade, blocked by the same fetcher rebuild); DW-094 (the EDGAR rebuild); `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/insider-transactions.md` (ACT-156 addendum); `docs/04-modules/longshort/signals/_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md` (the pattern note); `signal_compute_log` run_id `1021808b-4b1c-4b04-bb45-d989d56b5193` (the diagnostic exhibit — 1678 skips for 839 tickers, the "impossible telemetry" tell). |
| **Disposition** | Open (data-acquisition replacement deferred to DW-094). Signal #4 is cleanly disarmed; the P2 double-tally telemetry bug is closed in this PR. The pattern lesson is codified for all remaining feed-signals. |
| **Status** | Open — Signal #4 disarmed pending DW-094 (EDGAR rebuild); orchestrator double-tally bug resolved at ACT-156. |

## INC-71 — Polygon Options Developer tier returns well-shaped but NBBO-absent payloads; real-time bid/ask/last/greeks are gated behind a higher paid entitlement

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-09 during FP-043's parallel vendor vetting of Polygon as a candidate options source for Signal #3 (alongside Tradier). The probe used `POLYGON_API_KEY` (the same Developer-tier key used by all other Polygon-backed signals) and queried `/v3/snapshot/options/{underlying}` for SPY across the nearest expiration. |
| **Smoking gun** | The endpoint returned 200 with a structurally well-formed payload — 100+ contract rows, correct `details` block (strike, expiration, contract_type), correct `day` aggregate (open, high, low, close, volume from EOD), correct `open_interest`. **But every `last_quote.bid`, `last_quote.ask`, `last_quote.last`, and `greeks.{delta,gamma,vega,theta}` field was `null`.** No 401 / 403 / 429. No documentation warning. The payload looked indistinguishable from a healthy quote — until you summed the populated bid+ask count: 0 / 118. |
| **Severity** | **High — phantom-signal class, second axis.** A naive consumer that only ran `verifyFilterHonored()` (INC-70's lesson) would have happily ingested 100% well-shaped, 100% structurally-empty payloads. The downstream FP-043 compute would have classified every contract `no_qualifying_flow` (`volume >= 100` against `volume=null`, `bid > 0` against `bid=null`) — exactly the same phantom-signal pattern as INC-70 (looks sparse, is actually wrong), surfaced via a different mechanism (filter-honoured but key-fields-gated). |
| **Class** | Entitlement-gated key fields silently returned as `null` on a 200 response — distinct from a 403 (which would surface as a typed `subscription_gated` skip). The fetcher cannot distinguish "no liquidity" from "entitlement gates real-time NBBO" without an external known-good probe. |
| **Fix (this PR — ACT-157)** | (1) Polygon Options Developer DISQUALIFIED for Signal #3 — recorded as a vendor-selection decision at DEC-045. (2) Tradier production locked in (4-axis vetting passed). (3) **The fetcher-honesty pattern is extended with a second axis — `verifyFieldsPresent()`** — codified in `_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md` "FP-043 extension" section. Every new vendor-endpoint fetcher MUST run both axes: filter-honored (INC-70) AND fields-present (INC-71). Either alone leaves a phantom-signal path open. (4) The Tradier fetcher (`tradier-options-chain-fetcher.ts`) embeds both axes at construction; the FP-043 SPY probe measured 92/92 numeric bid/ask/last/volume/open_interest plus 92/92 populated greeks. |
| **Pattern lesson (codified)** | A 200-with-well-formed-payload is **not** evidence that the fields the formula consumes are populated. `verifyFieldsPresent()` probes the endpoint with a high-liquidity known-good symbol (e.g. SPY for an options endpoint, AAPL for an equities endpoint) and asserts the required numeric / object fields are non-null on ≥ a threshold fraction of returned rows (FP-043: ≥ 90% numeric population). If the threshold isn't met, the fetcher throws at construction — entitlement-gated key fields are a constructor-time fatal contract violation, not a runtime degraded state. Bound forward on Signals #1 (analyst), #2 (PEAD), #8 (news). |
| **Cross-references** | FP-043 (the implementing FP — vendor lock + dual-axis discipline); ACT-157 (the vetting + codification PR); DEC-045 (Tradier vendor lock, Polygon Options Developer disqualification); DEC-046 (v1 chain-snapshot conscious approximation on top of the vendor lock); INC-70 (the first-axis precedent — filter-honored); `_pattern-vendor-fetcher-filter-honesty.md` "FP-043 extension" section (the dual-axis discipline); `_shared/longshort-signals/shared/tradier-options-chain-fetcher.ts` (first fetcher with both axes embedded). |
| **Disposition** | Closed — Polygon Options Developer disqualified, Tradier locked in, pattern extended. Re-procuring a higher Polygon options tier is out-of-scope absent an explicit DEC superseding DEC-045 plus a fresh 4-axis vetting. |
| **Status** | Resolved — vendor lock at Tradier (DEC-045); pattern extended with `verifyFieldsPresent()` second axis; binding forward on Signals #1 / #2 / #8. |

## INC-72 — FP-044 PEAD synchronous-orchestrator test-fire 504'd at the 150s HTTP wall; isolate terminated at ~190s with zero persisted rows and zero application logs (silent death — second instance of the rate-capped single-invocation fan-out anti-pattern after FP-043)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-09 20:21 UTC operator test-fire of `longshort-pead-compute` (cron handler shipped by FP-044 Phase 3). HTTP returned `504 Gateway Timeout` after `execution_time_ms=150242` (`x-deno-execution-id 11a267bc-ee77-4d54-a0ac-1571749f98cb`). Isolate `Boot` at `20:18:59`, `Shutdown` at `20:22:09` — wall ~189.7s, past the 150s HTTP gateway and short of the ~400s Pro background-worker budget. |
| **Smoking gun (telemetry)** | Three coexisting absences, all load-bearing: (a) `SELECT … FROM signal_compute_log WHERE signal_id='pead_sue_20d'` → **0 rows** (no `completed`, no `failed`). (b) `SELECT count(*) FROM signal_observations WHERE signal_id='pead_sue_20d'` → **0**. (c) `function_logs` for execution `11a267bc` carries **only** lifecycle events (`Boot`, `Shutdown`) — **zero application log lines** from the orchestrator. The persistence path sits after `Promise.all` over the full 839-name universe, so a mid-flight runtime termination bypasses every `try/catch` written in user code. Unlike INC-66/FP-043's catch-writes-failed-row pattern, **this orchestrator left no truthful artifact at all** — green-by-absence to any dashboard reading `signal_compute_log`. |
| **Severity** | **High — silent-death class, second instance.** First instance was FP-043's pre-token-bucket Tradier path (caught pre-production by the FP-043 review). FP-044 re-fired the same class in production telemetry: rate-bound work × single-invocation synchronous fan-out × persistence-after-full-fan-out = no durable progress + no failure row + no log line. The instrumentation gap (no per-ticker logging inside the `pLimitedMap` body) means even if the isolate had survived, names-processed-at-termination would be unrecoverable from telemetry — a second axis of the same defect class. |
| **Root cause** | Two concurrent defects, the first sufficient, the second compounding: (1) **Vendor-cap arithmetic defect at design time.** `pead-orchestrator.ts` ships with header rate-math claiming concurrency-5 dual-fetch is "≤10 req/sec... well under the cap." 10 req/sec × 60 = 600/min versus Finnhub's 300/min ceiling — 2× over, before the dual-fetch factor is counted. Concurrency-based throttling is latency-dependent and unbounded by design; it cannot enforce a vendor-rate ceiling. FP-043 had already solved this for Tradier via `token-bucket.ts` (productionClock-routed); FP-044 deliberately documented "NO token-bucket" in the fetcher headers, with the unsound math as justification. (2) **Architectural shape mismatch.** 1,678 Finnhub calls (839 × 2) at the correctly paced 5 rps cap = 335s floor (~395s at the operator-measured 4.25 rps effective). Both numbers exceed the 150s HTTP wall and approach/match the 400s background-worker wall. The synchronous-orchestrator shell — inherited from short-interest-change (a much smaller cached feed) — is architecturally infeasible for an 839-name universe under a 300 rpm vendor cap regardless of HTTP-vs-background dispatch. |
| **Blast radius (actual)** | Zero data-quality blast radius — the cron is DISARMED (MIG-081, `enabled=false`) and the test-fire was operator-initiated. The blast is governance-only: FP-044's Phase-3 attestation surface is structurally non-functional in its current shape and must be re-shelled before any subsequent operator test-fire. Signal #2 (`pead_sue_20d`) remains live in `signal_registry` (truth-in-telemetry honest) but produces no observations; the Phase-3 combiner will see `pead_sue_20d` exclusively via missingness imputation until INC-72 is structurally resolved. |
| **Detection path (load-bearing)** | Operator test-fire surfaced the 504. Passive observation of `signal_compute_log` would NOT have surfaced it — the row count remained at 0, which is structurally indistinguishable from "cron has never fired." This is the **third** instance of a class-of-failure where green-by-absence concealed a broken path (INC-69 cron-auth, INC-66 missing failed-row, INC-72 missing log+row). Class lesson: **truth-in-telemetry requires a written failure row from the runtime layer, not the user-code `catch` — single-invocation fan-out orchestrators cannot guarantee this and are therefore unsuitable for any path where attestation depends on a row landing.** |
| **Fix (FP-045 — staged, this INC's resolution vehicle)** | (1) Generalized cursor-drain queue-worker per DEC-047, sharded across many small slice invocations: each slice processes ≤sliceSize names under token-bucket pacing (REUSING FP-043's `token-bucket.ts`), writes durable progress to a staging table, deletes its cursor rows, and emits a per-slice audit event (the INC-72 instrumentation fix). On cursor-drain, a winner-elected finalizer aggregates staging into `signal_observations` and writes `signal_compute_log`. Any isolate death loses ≤ one slice; durable progress + a truthful failure row are structural guarantees, not best-effort. (2) Same engine instantiated for both known rate-bound consumers: PEAD (Finnhub 300/min) and options-flow (Tradier 120/min — un-parks Signal #3, closes DW-095). (3) FP-044 orchestrator rate-comment defect corrected in the same PR (file retained for the pure per-ticker arm). (4) Pre-flight arithmetic row (calls/slice × rate vs the 150s wall) added as a standing executor discipline — vendor-cap × call-count × wall-budget math becomes an explicit PR-evidence line for every feed-signal execution prompt going forward. |
| **Pattern lesson (codified)** | **Rate-bound work in a single edge-function invocation is an anti-pattern wherever (vendor-cap-paced runtime > smallest available wall budget).** The arithmetic is mechanical: if `(universe_size × calls_per_name) / (vendor_rate_per_sec × 0.85)` > 150 (HTTP wall) **or** > ~400 (Pro background wall), the synchronous shell is structurally wrong regardless of how clean the inner loop is. Counts as a hard pre-flight gate for every signal whose vendor entitlement has a published rate cap; absence of the row in the PR is itself the failure mode. Two instances now (FP-043 Tradier pre-production, FP-044 Finnhub production) — promoting to a structural class. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-044 Phase 3 (the originating cron handler — `longshort-pead-compute`); FP-045 (the corrective FP — DEC-047 generalized queue-worker, this INC's resolution vehicle); DEC-047 (the cursor-drain queue-worker decision — this INC is the re-entry trigger for its implementation); DEC-053 (split-vendor lock — unchanged; only the orchestration shell changes); INC-66 (FP-043 sibling — the first instance of the rate-bound fan-out class, caught pre-production); INC-69 (the green-by-absence class precedent — `cron.job_run_details.status='succeeded'` concealing 0% auth success); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/pead/pead-orchestrator.ts` (the file carrying the unsound rate-math comment header — corrected in FP-045); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/token-bucket.ts` (the sanctioned pacer, reused by the FP-045 queue-worker); `signal_compute_log` + `signal_observations` (the persistence surfaces whose 0-row state is this INC's smoking gun); `function_logs` execution `11a267bc-ee77-4d54-a0ac-1571749f98cb` (the empty log envelope). |
| **Disposition** | Open — resolution vehicle is FP-045 (staged, phased). INC-72 closes when (a) the FP-045 queue-worker is implemented and tested, (b) the FP-044 orchestrator rate-comment defect is corrected, (c) an operator test-fire of the PEAD queue path lands a real `signal_compute_log` row and per-slice audit events, and (d) the pre-flight arithmetic discipline is codified at supervisor-instructions §22.3 for every subsequent feed-signal FP. |
| **Status** | Open — staged for FP-045 Phase 0–3. |

## INC-73 — FP-048 Phase 3b first-fire (run `72d7f1e3`) crashed on Page 2 of the sequential feed; the engine masked the root cause across THREE telemetry contracts (generic 500 body, missing `failure_reason` stamp, missing `slice.failed` audit event), and the per-page heartbeat tracked the kernel-frozen `as_of` instead of wall time — so a long healthy drain was structurally indistinguishable from a frozen one to the sweeper

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-12 01:05–01:20 UTC during the operator-initiated FP-048 Phase 3b first natural fire of `news_sentiment_7d` on the FP-045 generalized queue engine. Slice-1 fetched Page-1 successfully (2257 `signal_queue_feed_items` rows, 630 distinct articles, ≈3.58 tickers/article), then crashed on Page-2 of the same slice. The `__feed__` cursor row stayed `claimed_at != NULL`, no `failure_reason` was stamped on `signal_queue_runs`, no `longshort.signal_queue.slice.failed` audit event was emitted, the per-minute slice cron returned no-ops for 15 minutes (claim never released), and `net._http_response` carried only `{"error":"slice_failed"}` — verbatim `Error.message` was discarded by the shim. The DEC-047 sweeper backstop fired at 01:20:01.273 (heartbeat 15min > 600s threshold) and terminal-failed the run with `failure_reason='stale_heartbeat (last beat 2026-06-12T01:05:01.353+00:00, observed 2026-06-12T01:20:01.273Z, status was running)'` — **first real-data validation of the DEC-047 stale-heartbeat backstop**; cite forward to DEC-047 §5. |
| **Root cause (composite — three independent defects, all in the feed-mode slice path)** | (1) **No try/catch around the feed-mode drain.** `queue-slice-worker.ts::runFeedSlice` let exceptions from `fetchPage` propagate to the cron-handler boundary without first stamping `signal_queue_runs.failure_reason`, releasing the cursor claim, or incrementing a consecutive-failure counter. The per-ticker slice path (lines 192–203) catches per-ticker `compute` throws and converts them to typed skips — the feed-mode path had no equivalent. (2) **Shim error masking.** `longshort-queue-slice/index.ts` line 56 returned `apiError(500, 'slice_failed', …)` — generic — discarding the verbatim `Error.message` even though the catch block had it in hand and wrote it into the audit metadata. The 500 body in `net._http_response` carried zero diagnostic surface. (3) **Per-page heartbeat tracked the kernel-frozen `as_of`** (`queue-slice-worker.ts` line 411: `const ts = as_of.toISOString()` for both `feed_cursor`/`feed_pages_fetched` advance AND `heartbeat_at`). Replay-determinism reasoning is correct for the compute-input timestamps but wrong for `heartbeat_at` — a long-running healthy drain (e.g., a 94.5s/slice signal mid-page) would look stale to the sweeper after the first 10–15 minutes regardless of actual progress. The composite is what made the first fire diagnostically opaque: even after operator pulled `net._http_response`, the engine offered no information about which page threw, what the upstream error said, or whether the run was recoverable. |
| **Detection path** | Operator-initiated post-fire investigation polled the run row every minute and observed `feed_pages_fetched` stuck at 1 with `heartbeat_at` frozen at 01:05:01.353. Cross-correlated with `net._http_response` for the 01:05:03.41 slice invocation (status 500, body `{"error":"slice_failed"}`). The DEC-047 backstop's first natural fire at 01:20:01.273 was the only telemetry surface that produced a usable failure reason — that's the load-bearing finding: the backstop fired correctly, but it was the **only** thing that did. |
| **Class** | Telemetry-contract drift on a newly-introduced engine mode. The per-ticker mode satisfies all five telemetry contracts (typed skip on throw, `failure_reason` stamped, `slice.failed` event emitted, claim released via cursor delete, verbatim error propagated). The feed mode satisfied ZERO of them. The class lesson (folded into `docs/ai-failure-modes.md` — **every new consumer mode re-validates ALL telemetry contracts, not just the happy path**) is the structural fix: a new mode passing happy-path tests is not evidence it satisfies the failure-path contracts the older mode does. |
| **Resolution (FP-048 INC-73 fix PR)** | (1) **Engine — `queue-slice-worker.ts::runFeedSlice`**: wrapped the entire page-drain in try/catch. On throw: read the current `slice_failure_count`, mask the error via `error-key-mask.ts`, stamp `signal_queue_runs.failure_reason = 'feed_slice_threw: <masked>'`, increment the counter, release the `__feed__` cursor claim, then re-throw with the masked message. On successful page-loop exit: reset `slice_failure_count` to 0 (consecutive-streak breaker). (2) **3-strikes terminal-fail**: when the incremented counter reaches `FEED_SLICE_FAILURE_THRESHOLD = 3`, the engine terminal-fails the run in-place with `failure_reason = 'feed_slice_threw_3x: <last masked error>'` instead of bumping-and-releasing — bounds operator response time on a structurally-broken upstream. (3) **Per-page heartbeat → `liveClock.getWallClockTs()`**: introduced an injectable `liveClock: ClockReader` slot on `QueueSliceWorkerContext` defaulting to `productionClock` (DEC-034 clause 4 sanctioned exception, already used identically by `longshort-clock.ts::productionClock`); `feed_cursor` + `feed_pages_fetched` still track the kernel-frozen `as_of` (replay determinism preserved), but `heartbeat_at` tracks `liveClock` per page so a long healthy drain is never confusable with a frozen one. **The kernel-frozen `as_of` is still the SOLE timestamp routed into compute inputs** — this is an additive surface, not a replay-semantics change. (4) **Shim — `longshort-queue-slice/index.ts`**: the catch block now masks the verbatim `Error.message` and propagates it as the 500 body's `error` field (with `code: 'slice_failed'` retained for response-shape compatibility); both the audit metadata and the response body carry the same masked message. (5) **Sweeper — `queue-sweeper.ts`**: when a stale-heartbeat CAS-to-failed wins, also releases any held cursor claim for that run_id (`claimed_at := NULL` where not null) — defence-in-depth; the staging-TTL prune still does ultimate cleanup, but releasing now is correct hygiene. (6) **Key-masking — `error-key-mask.ts`**: scrubs `apiKey=…` query params, `apikey: …` header echoes (both bare and JSON-quoted), and `Authorization: Bearer …` tokens from error messages before they cross the audit boundary or land in a 500 body. 5 unit tests. (7) **Regression suite — `queue-feed-slice-failure_test.ts`**: 4 tests covering throw-on-page-2 (failure_reason stamp + counter increment + claim release + re-throw), 3-strikes terminal-fail (status flips, last verbatim error captured), counter reset on successful slice, and monotonic per-page heartbeat under injected clock. Per-ticker suite UNMODIFIED (regression fence). (8) **MIG-089c**: `slice_failure_count int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` on `signal_queue_runs`. |
| **Forward bindings** | (a) Every new consumer mode on the queue engine re-validates ALL five telemetry contracts (typed-skip / `failure_reason` stamp / `slice.failed` event / claim release / verbatim error in 500 body) before first-fire authorization — class lesson codified in `docs/ai-failure-modes.md`. (b) The DEC-047 backstop is now confirmed-working on real data — cite forward in any future feed-mode investigation. (c) `signal_queue_feed_items` evidence (2257 rows × 630 articles × ≈3.58 tickers/article) shows the per-(article,ticker) fan-out plumbing is correct — the only positive read from the failed fire — but evidence-only, NOT cross-run reusable: every new run gets a fresh `run_id` and re-fetches Page-1 from scratch; the PK is `(run_id, article_id, ticker)`. |
| **Cross-references** | FP-048 Phase 3b first-fire post-mortem; DEC-047 (the stale-heartbeat backstop validated by this incident); DEC-034 (the injected-clock framework `liveClock` extends additively); MIG-089a (the feed-mode substrate this fix corrects); MIG-089b (the registry truth row that armed the fire); MIG-089c (the `slice_failure_count` column added by this PR); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/queue-worker/queue-slice-worker.ts` (engine try/catch + per-page liveClock heartbeat); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/queue-worker/queue-sweeper.ts` (defence-in-depth claim release); `supabase/functions/longshort-queue-slice/index.ts` (verbatim error propagation); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/queue-worker/error-key-mask.ts` (new); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/queue-worker/queue-feed-slice-failure_test.ts` (regression suite); `docs/ai-failure-modes.md` (class lesson: every new consumer mode re-validates ALL telemetry contracts). |
| **Disposition** | Resolved (FP-048 INC-73 fix PR — engine + shim + sweeper + MIG-089c + regression suite + class lesson). Re-fire choreography requires supervisor verification per the original brief. |
| **Status** | Resolved. |

## INC-74 — FP-048 Phase 3b second-fire (run `74e46ece`) on the INC-73-fixed engine failed at Page 2 because the feed-items batch sent the same `(article_id, ticker)` tuple twice in one INSERT — Postgres `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` rejected the row-affect-once rule three times in a row and 3-strikes terminal-failed the run

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-12 01:48:52–01:51:01 UTC during the operator-initiated FP-048 Phase 3b second sequential-feed fire of `news_sentiment_7d` (run `74e46ece-e15a-4f1d-93c8-ea8f289b3424`) on HEAD `d86b33e3` (the INC-73-fixed engine + verbatim-error shim). Slice timeline (verbatim from `signal_queue_runs` + `longshort_audit_logs`): t=0 (01:48:52) `run.started`; t+10s (01:49:02) Page-2 slice threw `feed_items upsert failed: ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE command cannot affect row a second time`; t+1m9s (01:50:01) same throw at next minute-tick (cursor re-claimed from same token — INC-73 retry contract held); t+2m9s (01:51:01) same throw → `slice_failure_count=3` → 3-strikes terminal-fail with `failure_reason='feed_slice_threw_3x: feed_items upsert failed: ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE command cannot affect row a second time'`. `feed_pages_fetched=1` (Page-1's rows persisted in `signal_queue_feed_items` but never finalized — `signal_compute_log` row count = 0). |
| **Headline finding (load-bearing)** | **All five INC-73 telemetry contracts validated on this live failure** — the engine surfaced root cause cleanly and was diagnosable from `signal_queue_runs` alone: (1) verbatim `failure_reason` stamped (not generic `slice_failed`); (2) `longshort.signal_queue.slice.failed` audit event fired 3×, masked-message intact; (3) cursor preserved (`feed_cursor IS NOT NULL`) after each crash and re-claimed on the next minute-tick — retry-from-same-cursor proven on real data; (4) 3-strikes backstop fired at `slice_failure_count=3` with `feed_slice_threw_3x:` prefix; (5) `heartbeat_at` advanced under `liveClock` per page. INC-73's instrumentation worked exactly as designed; the entire forensic chain was readable in <60 seconds without redeploying anything. |
| **Root cause** | The vendor feed (`PolygonNewsFeedFetcher` via `news-sentiment-queue-registration.ts::fetchPage`) flattens each article's `insights[]` into one `FeedItemRecord` per insight — but vendor data can legitimately surface the same `(article_id, ticker)` tuple more than once within a single page (multi-ticker article with a repeated ticker in `insights[]`, e.g. duplicate insight rows from the upstream feed; classification-survivor count drift). `queue-slice-worker.ts::runFeedSlice` (lines 410–420 pre-fix) sent the page's rows directly to `signal_queue_feed_items.upsert({ onConflict: 'run_id,article_id,ticker' })` without batch-level dedupe. Postgres rejects a single INSERT that targets the same conflict key twice — "command cannot affect row a second time" — even though `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` is set. The fetcher returned a structurally valid page; the engine's persistence-shape assumption (no within-batch dupes) was wrong. |
| **Candidate duplicate sources (both possible on real vendor data)** | (a) **Within one page's `insights[]`**: an article whose `insights[]` array carries two entries for the same ticker (vendor's per-article enrichment can legitimately emit two insights against the same ticker with same or differing sentiment); (b) **Cross-page within one slice**: an article with `published_utc` near a vendor page boundary appearing on both Page-N and Page-(N+1) when both pages drain inside the same slice (already DB-safe via per-page INSERT statement + PK conflict-update, but adds load and was the silent assumption the pre-fix path relied on for cross-page idempotency). The fix dedupes case (a) deterministically at the batch-assembly point; case (b) is unaffected — each page remains a separate INSERT, so the cross-page conflict-update path is untouched. |
| **Class** | Persistence-shape assumption drift on a new engine mode. The per-ticker mode has a 1:1 `(run_id, ticker)` cursor → staging mapping that structurally cannot produce within-batch dupes (claim-RPC is `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` against a UNIQUE-claimed row). The feed mode's batch is many-rows-per-vendor-page where the conflict-key uniqueness is a vendor-data property, not a structural one — the engine MUST defend the persistence-shape itself. Parallel class to INC-73 (every new mode re-validates its contracts) — extended here to **persistence-shape invariants must be defended by the engine, not assumed of the fetcher**. |
| **Detection path** | Operator-initiated per-minute polling of `signal_queue_runs.failure_reason` surfaced the verbatim error on the first throw at 01:49:02 — INC-73's instrumentation made root cause visible in one query. No log-archaeology, no shim-redeploy. The 3-strikes backstop bounded operator response time at 2m09s. |
| **Resolution (INC-74 fix PR — this PR)** | (1) **Engine — `queue-slice-worker.ts::runFeedSlice`**: new exported `dedupeFeedItems(run_id, items)` helper applied at the batch-assembly point (mode-scoped — engine-side, NOT inside the Phase-1 `PolygonNewsFeedFetcher`; the fetcher remains the dumb pipe per the persistence-shape-is-engine-concern principle). Deterministic first-occurrence-wins by `(article_id, ticker)`. Accumulates `duplicateTuplesDropped` and `duplicateConflicts` (conflict = same key, differing `sentiment_num` / `tier_weight` / `published_utc` against the keeper) across all pages drained THIS slice. (2) **Result type — `QueueSliceWorkerResult`**: new optional `duplicate_tuples_dropped` + `duplicate_conflicts` fields (feed-mode only; undefined in per-ticker mode for backward-compat). (3) **Handler — `longshort-queue-slice/index.ts`**: threads both counters into the `longshort.signal_queue.slice.completed` audit event metadata. **Observability first — if Phase 7 / live operation ever shows `duplicate_conflicts > 0` we revisit the first-wins rule by DEC.** (4) **Regression suite — `queue-feed-slice-dedupe_test.ts`**: 5 tests covering (a) same ticker twice in one article's insights → 1 drop / 0 conflicts / 1 row reaches DB; (b) same `(article, ticker)` spanning a page boundary within one slice → engine-side count = 0 (each page is a separate INSERT; cross-page is DB-side ON CONFLICT); (c) conflicting-sentiment duplicate → first-wins keeper preserved + both counters increment; plus two unit assertions on the pure helper. Per-ticker suite UNMODIFIED (regression fence). |
| **Forward bindings** | (a) **Persistence-shape invariants are engine-side, not fetcher-side** — codified as the FP-048-extension to the "every new mode re-validates ALL contracts" lesson (INC-73). Future engine modes whose persistence target carries a UNIQUE / PK over vendor-derived keys MUST dedupe at the batch-assembly point before the upsert. (b) **`duplicate_conflicts` is a Phase-7 review signal** — non-zero observation in live operation triggers a DEC to revisit first-wins (current default is "vendor's first insight per `(article, ticker)` is authoritative"). (c) The cross-page case (b) is intentionally not deduped engine-side — each vendor page is a separate INSERT and DB-side `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` handles it; the engine count for case (b) is therefore 0 by design (asserted in test). |
| **Cross-references** | INC-73 (the instrumentation that made INC-74 trivially diagnosable — direct dependency); DEC-047 (the sweeper backstop that already validated under INC-73 — not invoked here because the 3-strikes terminal-fail beat it); FP-048 Phase 3b second-fire post-mortem; `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/queue-worker/queue-slice-worker.ts::runFeedSlice` + `dedupeFeedItems` (engine-side dedupe at batch assembly); `supabase/functions/longshort-queue-slice/index.ts` (counters into `slice.completed` metadata); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/shared/queue-worker/queue-feed-slice-dedupe_test.ts` (regression suite); `docs/ai-failure-modes.md` (persistence-shape invariants extension). |
| **Disposition** | Resolved (INC-74 fix PR — engine dedupe + handler counters + regression suite + class-lesson extension). Re-fire of `news_sentiment_7d` authorized after deploy + supervisor verification. |
| **Status** | Resolved. |

## INC-75 — FP-049 Phase 3 first-fire `catalyst_meta.verb_gate_drops=0` / `numeric_gate_drops=0` was a structural-zero mirage: gate-drop counters lived in `classifyCatalystEvents` but the news path's `PolygonNewsKeywordFetcher` pre-filters via `matchKeywordEvent` and never feeds news rows into the classifier — so the meta surface measured a post-filter stage and reported a clean-FPR reading that was definitionally unreachable

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-12 (during the §22.5.1 validation read of run `ce74ea97-a0c1-4a5b-9a13-0e317b42016a`, the first `active_catalyst_flag` fire on the HEAD that landed Phase 3a/3b/3c). `catalyst_meta` carried `verb_gate_drops=0`, `numeric_gate_drops=0` across the entire 839-name universe and ~118 persisted rows — flagged as "the verb-gate's first production catch" but on supervisor source-trace returned zero, not because the gate is clean but because the counter is measured at the wrong stage. |
| **Headline finding (load-bearing)** | **Stage-mismatched instrumentation.** `classify-catalyst-event.ts:163-165` increments `verb_gate_drops` / `numeric_gate_drops` inside the keyword-derivation branch of `classifyCatalystEvents`. The orchestrator (`active-catalyst-orchestrator.ts:317-321`) calls the classifier with `news=[]` and routes the pre-classified Polygon news rows into `structured`. `polygon-news-keyword-fetcher.ts:104-152` runs `matchKeywordEvent` inline at the fetch stage and emits ONLY positive matches — gate-failing rows are silently `continue`d (no `drop_reason` reference anywhere in the file). The classifier therefore only ever sees pre-matched text → the meta counters **structurally cannot exceed 0** in normal operation. Distinct from a "low FPR" reading: the surface is not measuring what its name implies; it is measuring a stage downstream of the gate it claims to instrument. |
| **Class** | Telemetry-stage drift — the observability cousin of INC-73's "every telemetry contract validated on live data" lesson. INC-73 caught a *contract that didn't fire*; INC-75 catches a *contract that fires at the wrong stage and therefore measures something other than its name promises*. Class lesson: **telemetry contracts must be validated against the data path they nominally measure, not just executed**. A counter named `verb_gate_drops` must be wired to the actual verb-gate execution site; if the gate runs in two places (fetcher + classifier defence-in-depth), the meta must aggregate both stages, and the FPR-denominator (PRE-gate volume) must be carried on the same surface. |
| **Detection path** | Supervisor source-trace of the `verb_gate_drops=0` reading: grep for `verb_gate_drops` returned the increment site (classifier) and the read site (orchestrator → meta) but ZERO references in `polygon-news-keyword-fetcher.ts`. Cross-checked against the fetcher's match/emit site (lines 127-131, 165-178 pre-fix) — confirmed: positive matches emit, negative matches `continue` without telemetry. The "0 across 839 names" reading was structurally impossible to fail, which is itself the smoking gun. |
| **Resolution (INC-75 fix — this PR)** | (1) **Fetcher — `polygon-news-keyword-fetcher.ts`**: extended the in-loop match handling to count `verb_gate_drops` / `numeric_gate_drops` from `matchKeywordEvent.drop_reason` (the field was already returned by the classifier helper; the fetcher simply ignored it), and to count `articles_scanned` as the PRE-gate denominator (non-empty-text rows seen). All three counters returned on the `kind:'events'` result. (2) **Type surface — `catalyst-types.ts::CatalystFetchResult`**: three optional fields added to the `events` variant (`verb_gate_drops`, `numeric_gate_drops`, `articles_scanned`); only `PolygonNewsKeywordFetcher` populates them at v1 (other fetchers have no keyword gate). (3) **Orchestrator — `active-catalyst-orchestrator.ts`**: `catalyst_meta.verb_gate_drops` / `numeric_gate_drops` now AGGREGATE `polygonNewsRes.<field>` (fetcher-stage — real PRE-gate volume) **plus** `classified.<field>` (classifier-stage — defence-in-depth; structurally 0 because the news path is pre-filtered, retained as a contract surface so the classifier's own dedup path remains instrumented). `catalyst_meta.articles_scanned` added so `FPR = (verb_gate_drops + numeric_gate_drops) / articles_scanned` is computable from the meta envelope alone. (4) **Regression test — `polygon-news-keyword-fetcher_test.ts` (16)**: fixture batch of 5 articles → 2 matchable (executive_change + guidance true-positives) + 2 verb-gate failures (executive_change noun-only + partnership noun where "partners" substring trap is exercised) + 1 numeric-gate failure (guidance noun + verb, no numeric token) → expects `rows.length=2 / articles_scanned=5 / verb_gate_drops=2 / numeric_gate_drops=1`. Asserts the fetcher-stage telemetry directly. Existing 15 tests UNMODIFIED. |
| **Forward bindings** | (a) **Telemetry-stage discipline** codified as the FP-049-extension to INC-73's "validated on live data" lesson: any counter whose name describes a logical gate must be wired to every execution site of that gate, and the meta surface that aggregates them must also carry the PRE-gate denominator (scanned volume) so FPR is computable from the same surface. (b) The classifier-stage counters remain in `classifyCatalystEvents` as defence-in-depth — if a future code path EVER routes news through `classifyCatalystEvents.news`, the contract self-instruments. (c) The §22.5.1 validation playbook for `active_catalyst_flag` now expects `verb_gate_drops > 0` on a healthy fire across ~1,300 articles — the operator re-fire post-INC-75 is the first reading on the corrected surface. |
| **Cross-references** | INC-73 (the class precedent — contract-validated-on-live-data; INC-75 is the observability cousin); FP-049 Phase 3 first-fire validation read (the discovery surface); DEC-057 §(b) (the keyword + verb gate whose drops are now instrumented honestly); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/active-catalyst/polygon-news-keyword-fetcher.ts` (the fetch-stage gate execution site — now counter-instrumented); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/active-catalyst/active-catalyst-orchestrator.ts` (meta aggregation site); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/active-catalyst/catalyst-types.ts` (CatalystFetchResult contract extension); `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-signals/active-catalyst/polygon-news-keyword-fetcher_test.ts` (regression `(16)`); `signal_compute_log` run `ce74ea97-a0c1-4a5b-9a13-0e317b42016a` (the structural-zero reading that surfaced the defect). |
| **Disposition** | Resolved (INC-75 fix PR — fetcher-stage counters + CatalystFetchResult extension + orchestrator aggregation + articles_scanned denominator + regression test). Post-deploy re-fire by operator authorized; verb_gate_drops > 0 expected as healthy reading. |
| **Status** | Resolved. |

## INC-76 — GitHub Actions Node-20 runtime deprecation surfaced in strong-evidence run #376 annotations; pinned actions (`actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-node@v4`, `denoland/setup-deno@v1`, `actions/cache@v4`) are being force-run on Node 24 today and will break when GitHub removes the Node-20 runtime

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-24 (DW-128 Stage-2 toolchain-parity gate first green run — `strong-evidence` workflow run #376 on commit `a920a160`). 22 annotations on the run, all platform-level deprecation notices: "Node.js 20 is deprecated. The following actions target Node.js 20 but are being forced to run on Node.js 24." Appeared identically on BOTH jobs in the workflow (`toolchain-parity` and `strong-evidence`), confirming the notice is workflow-wide and not introduced by the DW-128 Stage-2 edit. |
| **Headline finding (load-bearing)** | **Platform-level action-runtime deprecation, NOT a CROSSWIND defect.** GitHub Actions is sunsetting the Node 20 runtime; actions that declare `runs.using: node20` in their `action.yml` are currently being force-shimmed onto Node 24. Affected actions in this repo: `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-node@v4`, `denoland/setup-deno@v1`, `actions/cache@v4`. Same set is used across the sibling workflows `.github/workflows/combiner-train.yml`, `.github/workflows/deploy-edge-functions.yml`, `.github/workflows/insider-discovery.yml` — the eventual sweep MUST cover all four workflow files, not just `strong-evidence.yml`. Non-blocking today (force-shim keeps green); will hard-break when GitHub removes the Node-20 runtime entirely. |
| **Class** | Platform-deprecation surface / deferred-maintenance. Distinct from any CROSSWIND defect class — the surface that flagged it (run-annotations) is also the surface that confirms it's external. The DW-128 Stage-2 parity gate did its job here: it surfaced a workflow-wide signal we'd otherwise have missed until breakage. |
| **Detection path** | Operator review of the run-#376 Annotations tab (22 warnings) during DW-128 Stage-2 closure verification. Cross-checked: identical notice appears on the pre-DW-128 baseline runs too (same action versions); not introduced by `a920a160`. |
| **Resolution (deferred)** | Future Tier-C maintenance pass: bump action major versions to their Node-24-native releases as they ship — `actions/checkout@v4` → v5, `actions/setup-node@v4` → next major, `actions/cache@v4` → next major, `denoland/setup-deno@v1` → next major (track upstream release cadence). Sweep ALL FOUR workflow files in one PR to avoid version drift across `.github/workflows/`. NOT actioned by this INC entry — this is the log-and-defer record so the notice isn't lost. |
| **Forward bindings** | (a) When any of the four actions ships a Node-24-native major version, this INC is the trigger to plan the sweep PR. (b) If a future strong-evidence run goes red with a "Node 20 runtime not available" error (rather than the current force-shim warning), THIS INC is the root-cause pointer — escalate the sweep from deferred-maintenance to urgent. (c) Add to the deferred-work register if the resolution slips past the next phase boundary. |
| **Cross-references** | DW-128 Stage-2 closure (the work that surfaced the notice via run #376 annotations); `.github/workflows/strong-evidence.yml` (toolchain-parity + strong-evidence jobs — both affected); `.github/workflows/combiner-train.yml`, `.github/workflows/deploy-edge-functions.yml`, `.github/workflows/insider-discovery.yml` (sibling workflows using the same action set — included in the eventual sweep scope); GitHub Actions changelog (upstream — runtime-deprecation timeline). |
| **Disposition** | Logged-and-deferred. No code/workflow change as part of this INC; resolution is a future maintenance-pass PR bumping action major versions across all four workflow files in one commit. |
| **Status** | Open (deferred-maintenance). Severity: low. |

## INC-77 — DEC-068 clause (f) line 133 prose-attests a paper-only runtime guard in `AlpacaPaperClient` that the landed code does NOT implement (constructor accepts any `baseUrlOverride` with zero validation); DEC-vs-code SAFETY-direction divergence — latent now, live-relevant at FP-056 E2 submitter build

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-24 (ACT-309 E2-design investigation; independently verified by supervisor fresh-clone). Recorded as a clean governance artifact in this charter PR (DEC-068 clause (k).8 cross-references this INC); the actual guard BUILD + the companion `scripts/check-paper-only-url.ts` `SCAN_ROOT` expansion are the E2-CODE PR work (separately scheduled, NOT in this charter PR). |
| **Headline finding (load-bearing)** | DEC-068 clause (f) **line 133 prose** (verbatim): *"the submitter's `AlpacaPaperClient` baseUrl is hard-asserted to start with `https://paper-api.alpaca.markets` at construction; any caller injecting an alternate base URL throws a typed error at first request."* The landed code at `src/features/longshort/services/broker/alpaca/alpaca-paper-client.ts` constructor sets `this.baseUrl = config.baseUrlOverride ?? ALPACA_PAPER_BASE_URL` with **ZERO validation** — any caller-supplied `baseUrlOverride` is accepted unchanged; no allow-list, no string-prefix assertion, no typed throw. The DEC attested a paper-only control that does not exist. Mirror of the ACT-308 clause-j reconciliation precedent (DEC-vs-code divergences earn their own clean governance record), reversed direction: there the CODE was right (DEC wording was stale); here the DEC FORWARD-DECLARED a control that was never built. |
| **Classification** | DEC-vs-code divergence, **SAFETY-DIRECTION** (the DEC attests a paper-only safety control that is absent on the only broker-boundary client). **Latent today** (nothing in the codebase submits orders via this client pre-FP-056 E2 — the only consumers are read-only fetchers per FP-006 sub-step 6.7). **Becomes live-relevant at FP-056 E2** when the sequential submitter is the first consumer that POSTs orders through `AlpacaPaperClient`; from that commit onward the guard's absence becomes a live paper-only-discipline gap. |
| **Related CI surface** | `scripts/check-paper-only-url.ts` (DEC-036 clause (2) static lint) is **complementary, not equivalent**: it greps `src/features/longshort/**` for the live URL string `://api.alpaca.markets`. Two coverage gaps: (i) the runtime path — a caller passing the live URL through `baseUrlOverride` from an unscanned source bypasses the lint entirely; (ii) the SCAN_ROOT is too narrow — `supabase/functions/` (where the E2 submitter and its `_shared/` deps live) is OUTSIDE the current SCAN_ROOT. Both the runtime-guard build and the SCAN_ROOT expansion belong in the E2-code PR. |
| **Detection path** | ACT-309 E2-design investigation read the `AlpacaPaperClient` constructor line-by-line to scope the E2 submitter's broker-boundary contract; the missing guard surfaced when the prompt-required "runtime guard claimed in DEC-068" was compared against the actual constructor body. Supervisor performed an independent fresh-clone verification before this charter PR was authored. |
| **Resolution (planned at FP-056 E2-CODE PR — NOT this charter PR)** | (1) Add a **construction-time allow-list** in `AlpacaPaperClient`: accept `baseUrlOverride` only if it starts with one of `https://paper-api.alpaca.markets`, `https://data.alpaca.markets`, or `http://localhost` / `https://localhost` (test seam for `fetchImpl` injection + replay fixtures). Throw a typed `PaperOnlyViolationError` at construction otherwise; the throw includes the offending URL prefix and the DEC-068 clause-(f) anchor for auditability. (2) **Expand `scripts/check-paper-only-url.ts` `SCAN_ROOT`** from `src/features/longshort/` to also cover `supabase/functions/` (where the E2 submitter and `_shared/longshort-execution/` modules live). Keep the existing self-exclude + `_test.ts` exclude semantics. (3) New unit tests covering: allow-listed override accepted; non-allow-listed override throws `PaperOnlyViolationError`; default (no override) resolves to `ALPACA_PAPER_BASE_URL`. (4) Static-lint coverage validated by adding a fixture file under the expanded SCAN_ROOT to confirm the violation is caught. The E2-code PR commit message + ACT entry cite INC-77 + DEC-068 clause (k).8 explicitly as the divergence-closure record. |
| **Why NOT retroactively edit DEC-068 line 133** | Append-only governance discipline (mirror of the ACT-308 reconciliation precedent — the DEC's audit trail is preserved by appending the resolution clause, not by rewriting the original prose). The forward-declaration is preserved as historical record; the resolution lives in clause (k).8 + this INC + the future E2-code PR commit + ACT entry. Future readers consulting line 133 see the forward-declaration AND the same-DEC clause-(k).8 cross-reference to this INC + the E2-code-PR closure. |
| **Forward bindings** | (a) The FP-056 E2-code PR MUST close this INC by landing the construction-time guard + the SCAN_ROOT expansion in the same commit. (b) The E2-code PR ACT entry MUST cite INC-77 + DEC-068 clause (k).8 + the four resolution sub-items above. (c) If the E2-code PR lands without closing INC-77, this is a STOP-condition violation; the divergence becomes live-relevant from that commit onward. |
| **Cross-references** | DEC-068 clause (f) line 133 (the forward-declared guard prose — NOT retroactively edited per append-only discipline); DEC-068 clause (k).8 (the canonical record of this divergence + the resolution path); `src/features/longshort/services/broker/alpaca/alpaca-paper-client.ts` (the constructor with the missing guard); `scripts/check-paper-only-url.ts` (the complementary static lint with the narrow SCAN_ROOT); ACT-308 (the precedent reconciliation — code right, DEC stale; INC-77 is the reverse — DEC forward-declared, code absent); ACT-309 (this charter PR — the divergence-record-only landing; resolution at the future E2-code PR); FP-056 E2 (the build PR that will close this INC). |
| **Disposition** | **CLOSED at FP-056 E2-code PR (ACT-310, 2026-06-24).** Runtime allow-list guard + typed `PaperOnlyViolationError` built into `AlpacaPaperClient` constructor; `scripts/check-paper-only-url.ts` `SCAN_ROOT` expanded to also cover `supabase/functions/`; six new guard unit tests (live-URL override throws; allow-listed accepted; localhost accepted; arbitrary host throws; default resolves to paper; dataUrlOverride independently gated); two new SCAN_ROOT-expansion lint tests via temp-tree fixture; 31 pre-existing `baseUrlOverride` test-seam callsites migrated from `'https://test'` / `'https://t'` / `'https://d'` to `http://localhost`. Both `scripts/check-paper-only-url.ts` (CLEAN) and `scripts/check-wall-clock.ts` (CLEAN) verified post-landing. INC-77 forward-binding (a) honored: closed in the SAME commit that introduces the first POST consumer (`order-submitter.ts`). |
| **Status** | **CLOSED 2026-06-24 at ACT-310 (FP-056 E2-code PR).** Guard built, SCAN_ROOT expanded, tests + fixtures landed. The divergence is closed; the safety control attested in DEC-068 clause (f) line 133 prose now exists in code. |

## INC-80 — Spot-check live fire (corr `bb3810bf`) FILLED a paper SPY order that was never recorded in `reconciliation_events` due to the four-violation writer defect; position since closed (flat); logged-incidental, NO backfill (the corrective row would inject a null-expected synthetic non-divergence + establish a hand-write precedent into the divergence detector)

> **Numbering note (2026-06-25):** originally landed as INC-78; renumbered to INC-80 because INC-78 was already claimed by the alpaca-fetchers `pending_new→pending` fixture-mismatch candidate (action-tracker ACT-310) and INC-79 by the `deno.lock` v5/v3 drift candidate (ACT-310). Content below is verbatim from the original landing; only the number changed.

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-25 (supervisor live fire of `longshort-rebalance-submit` `{"mode":"spot_check","symbol":"SPY","qty":1}`; corr `bb3810bf-d07f-43ee-946b-703d3632a639`; account `PA3CRAJBSVZO`). Confirmed via operator's Alpaca dashboard: 1 share SPY filled @ 736.99. The endpoint returned 500 INTERNAL_ERROR; the order was placed and filled BEFORE the throw (the throw was post-submit, during the local event emission). Position subsequently sold by the operator; account confirmed flat. |
| **Headline finding (load-bearing)** | The placement-trigger's `reconciliation_events` writer carried FOUR contract violations against the MIG-043 schema (see ACT-326 / DEC-068 clause (o) for the fix). The Alpaca order was placed and filled normally; the throw occurred AFTER `submitRebalance` returned, in `createSupabaseReconciliationEventWriter.emit` with `Could not find the 'payload' column of 'reconciliation_events' in the schema cache`. NO row was written to `reconciliation_events` for corr `bb3810bf`; the placement is therefore visible at Alpaca (broker prime) + in `longshort_audit_logs` (the local failure record) but absent from the reconciliation surface. |
| **Disposition** | **Logged-incidental. NO BACKFILL row in `reconciliation_events`.** Rationale: (a) the reconciliation surface is a live divergence detector keyed on expected-vs-observed at decision time, not a trade journal — a backfilled row has no real `expected_value` (the throw was post-submit; the decision-time expectation was never materialized through the writer's mapping) and would inject a synthetic non-divergence into the tier/outcome distributions the escalation logic reads; (b) the audit trail is preserved at the broker prime (Alpaca `/v2/orders` retains the filled order indefinitely on paper) + at `longshort_audit_logs` (the local failure record carries the correlation + the verbatim throw); (c) a hand-write would establish a precedent ("we hand-write `reconciliation_events` when the writer breaks") that is exactly the phantom-divergence risk the §22.5.1 gate guards against; (d) the corrective forward-action (centralize the writer + add the `writer_smoke` §22.5.1 harness + ratify the tier mapping) is recorded under ACT-326 / DEC-068 clause (o). The §22.5.1 gate added in ACT-326 (the `writer_smoke` mode in `longshort-rebalance-submit`) prevents the recurrence; the orphaned fill's accounting truth is the broker prime + this INC entry. |
| **Forward bindings** | (a) If a future operator-action requires a system-side per-event record of corr `bb3810bf` (e.g. an end-of-period reconciliation report), the canonical citation is this INC + the Alpaca `order_id` (operator-recoverable from the Alpaca dashboard or `/v2/orders` listing for the relevant fill window) — NOT a backfilled row. (b) If a second instance of "filled-but-unreconciled" surfaces from any cause, escalate from logged-incidental to an emergency-DEC review of the placement→reconciliation seam (the writer defect was a single-point failure mode that the centralized writer + writer_smoke gate is intended to close; a recurrence implies the gate is insufficient). |
| **Cross-references** | `longshort_audit_logs` row for corr `bb3810bf-d07f-43ee-946b-703d3632a639` (the local failure record carrying the verbatim throw); MIG-043 (`reconciliation_events` schema — the contract violated); ACT-326 (the REVISION-FIX that landed the centralized writer + tier mapping + writer_smoke §22.5.1 harness); DEC-068 clause (o) (the addendum that ratifies the tier mapping + the 'weak' prohibition + the §22.5.1 gate); `_shared/longshort-execution/reconciliation-event-writer.ts` (the ONE production writer); `_shared/longshort-execution/classify-submission-event.ts` (the SubmissionResult decomposition); `longshort-rebalance-submit/index.ts` `writer_smoke` mode (the non-money §22.5.1 verification harness). |
| **Status** | Closed-incidental. Severity: medium (the surfacing event was a live-money throw with a 736.99-USD filled order that has since been closed; the structural fix is ACT-326). |

## INC-82 — Dual-tree broker-module silent drift across the FP-056-E6 → FP-062-6I.2a window (src/ tree dead-to-Vite via tsconfig exclude masked the drift; edge was the complete superset, ZERO logic lost)

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Surfaced** | 2026-06-29 (FP-062 sub-step 6I.2a investigation; resolved same-day at ACT-381). |
| **Headline finding** | After ACT-316 transcribed the 12 alpaca-* broker modules from `src/features/longshort/services/broker/alpaca/` to `supabase/functions/_shared/longshort-broker/` (FP-056 E6-build-revision restoring the membrane), the src/ tree was NOT deleted and entered a "dual-tree" window with both copies live. Over the subsequent FP-056 / FP-057 / FP-061 build window the EDGE copies received functional improvements that the src/ copies did NOT receive (`order-submitter` gained `market` order type + optional `limit_price`; `open-orders-fetcher` got the cid-regex fix; `position-fetcher` added `listOpenPositions()` + `mapPosition` helper + `qty`; `quote-fetcher`/`halt-status-fetcher`/`order-acceptance-fetcher` each gained a field). The src/ tree silently rotted. **No logic was lost** (edge was the strict superset on every paired file — investigation grep-VERIFIED). |
| **Why the drift was invisible (LOAD-BEARING)** | `tsconfig.app.json` line 37 (pre-delete) excluded `src/features/longshort/services/broker/alpaca` from the Vite build, so the src/ tree was DEAD-TO-VITE — the production app never saw it, the type-checker never validated it against the rest of the codebase, and the natural "compile-error" forcing function that normally surfaces drift was disabled. The only safety net was `scripts/check-broker-parity.{ts,_test.ts}` which asserted src↔edge behavioral parity on 3 (later 8) scenarios — but they did NOT enforce TYPE-LEVEL surface equality, only behavioral parity on the SHARED surface, so EDGE-only additions (the supersets above) didn't trip the gate. |
| **Classification** | Dead-code-rot / dual-tree-window hygiene. Low severity (zero logic loss; production runtime never touched the src/ tree). Surfaces a structural lesson: **MOVE operations must DELETE the source in the same window as the transcription, not "transcribe + leave src/ until later"**. The check-src-imports.ts + check-edge-imports.ts gates codify the membrane in import direction but DO NOT detect dead duplicates on either side. |
| **Resolution** | ACT-381 (this turn) — src/ tree DELETED entire, edge-resident is now the sole owner. The vestigial `check-broker-parity.{ts,_test.ts}` gates were deleted in the same atomic commit (nothing left to compare). The ADR-002 multi-pending harness was MOVED to edge-resident rather than deleted, preserving the §8.6.1.1/DEC-036 empirical-validation capability. tsconfig.app.json exclude entry dropped (path no longer exists). |
| **Forward-binding** | Future MOVE operations (FP-062 6I.2c onward will MOVE additional modules per BINDING DECISION 1) MUST delete the source same-commit. The "transcribe-then-delete-later" pattern is hereby anti-patterned by repo precedent — every dual-tree window risks silent drift, and tsconfig-exclude (or any other "hide from the type-checker") removes the only surviving cross-tree safety net. |
| **Status** | Closed at ACT-381 (resolved same-day as surfaced). |

## INC-90 — `overshoot-entry-run` selection loader hardcodes `t_close_ref`/`pre_event_ref` as `NULL::numeric`; every I5 recheck receives `t=NaN pre=NaN` and refuses `reference_prices_malformed` 100% of the time

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced by** | ACT-484 (I5 full-refusal diagnosis, 2026-07-07) after §9.2 first-light bracket refused 20/20 selections. |
| **HEAD at surfacing** | `7d910637`. |
| **Location** | `supabase/functions/overshoot-entry-run/index.ts:593-605` (selection SELECT) + `index.ts:727-728` (`?? NaN` coercion) + `_shared/overshoot-execution/i5-recheck.ts:146-153` (malformed refusal). |
| **Symptom** | Handler-side selection SELECT: `SELECT e.ticker, e.side, e.rank_score, e.tier, NULL::numeric AS t_close_ref, NULL::numeric AS pre_event_ref FROM overshoot_events e WHERE e.run_id = ${linkage.runId}::uuid AND e.selected_for_entry = true`. Coercion at line 727-728: `const tCloseRef = sel.t_close_ref ?? NaN; const preEventRef = sel.pre_event_ref ?? NaN`. I5 kernel refuses on `!Number.isFinite(tCloseRef) OR tCloseRef <= 0 OR !Number.isFinite(preEventRef) OR preEventRef <= 0` with reason string `tCloseRef and preEventRef must be finite and > 0 (got t=NaN pre=NaN)`. |
| **Empirical evidence** | §9.2 first-light bracket 2026-07-07 09:31 ET, both DRY (`run_id=1ffad48b`) and LIVE (`run_id=e44ff3d1`) runs: 20 targets loaded, 0 orders submitted, 20 i5_refusals per run — split 17 `polygon_snapshot_stale` + 23 `reference_prices_malformed` across the two runs. The stale bucket is an ordering-first artifact (i5-recheck.ts:137 fires before 146); if the stale check were bypassed, all 20 would refuse malformed on the very next line. |
| **Schema evidence** | `overshoot_target_positions` columns: `(run_id, ticker, side, target_shares, target_notional, rank_score, computed_at)` — no `t_close_ref` / `pre_event_ref`. `overshoot_events` columns: `(event_id, run_id, as_of_date, ticker, side, excess_w1..w5, argmax_window_days, momentum_quintile, drawdown_bucket, days_to_nearest_earnings, earnings_alias_used, filter_passes, filter_refusal_reason, selected_for_entry, rank_score, study_cell_ref, created_at, tier)` — no `t_close_ref` / `pre_event_ref` either. The reference-price fields the entry engine needs live nowhere in the detection-side persistence contract; the intended source is presumably a downstream JOIN to `overshoot_daily_bars` for the T-close session, but that JOIN was never wired. |
| **Classification** | **Wired-in TODO / silent-sentinel-adjacent defect at the detection → entry handoff.** The `NULL::numeric AS <col>` pattern is a load-bearing placeholder that compiles clean, types clean, and passes the separation guard — but the runtime consequence is 100% pre-open refusal for every session until a real source is wired. The I5 DEFAULT-DENY discipline held (zero silent drops, every refusal persisted with typed reason string), so the money-path safety envelope is intact — but the input was structurally invalid, not a real market signal. |
| **Money-path impact** | Zero (safety envelope worked as designed). No orders submitted. No positions opened. Account state unchanged. Impact is **opportunity cost**: 100% of first-light candidates refuse until the wiring is corrected. |
| **Corrective options** | Enumerated in ACT-484 (five options A/B/C/D + advisory). Load-bearing corrective is **Option A**: rewrite the SELECT to JOIN `overshoot_daily_bars` on `(ticker, session_date = detection.as_of_date)` for `t_close_ref` and to the pre-event bar per detector-window contract for `pre_event_ref`; add unit test asserting non-NaN propagation. Not fixed this turn per ACT-484 investigation-mode discipline. |
| **Anti-phantom-defaults classification** | This is exactly the failure mode the anti-phantom-defaults directive was written to forbid at money-path boundaries (`value ?? NaN` is a typed-Optional-adjacent pattern, but the underlying source is a hardcoded NULL literal — a silent sentinel in disguise). The `?? NaN` coercion is the CORRECT defense; the DEFECT is upstream at the SELECT — no real source is being consulted. |
| **Standing invariant reinforced** | Any new `NULL::<type>` placeholder in a money-path SELECT is now anti-patterned by INC-90 precedent. Placeholders MUST be surfaced as `-- TODO(INC-NNN): join <table> for <field>` in the query text and simultaneously registered as an INC on the same PR — the "compile-clean placeholder" pattern is forbidden as a silent sentinel class. |
| **Status** | **Resolved (structural + behavioral).** Structural fix landed at ACT-485 (2026-07-07). Behavioral proof landed same-day at DRY run **`9fe034be-92d6-461f-ace9-e1b38ba2704c`** (correlation `d07951de-8dee-40c6-88cf-ed31e3b560dc`): `reference_prices_malformed = 0`, `reference_bar_missing = 0`, 20 targets loaded with real T-close + pre-event refs. Refusals were market-truthful (9× `i5_reversion_exceeded` with reversionPct 0.50–1.24 vs 0.5 tolerance; 2× I5 open-feed lag; 3× residual entry-price wall-clock skew — the latter surfaced as **INC-91** and closed at ACT-486). The `NULL::numeric` sentinel class is extinct from the four overshoot money-path handlers; CI gate prevents reintroduction. |
| **Fix references** | ACT-485 (structural); ACT-486 (INC-91 sibling-site close-out, INC-90 behavioral flip); DRY run `9fe034be` (behavioral proof); `_shared/overshoot-execution/i5-recheck.ts:22-32` (I5 contract docstring — ref definitions); `overshoot-entry-run/index.ts:593-621` + `index.ts:729-746`; `.github/workflows/overshoot-guards.yml` (INC-90 CI gate step). |
| **Added by** | Lovable at operator direction, 2026-07-07, HEAD `7d910637` → INC-90 surfacing via ACT-484; Resolved-structurally at ACT-485; Resolved (structural + behavioral) at ACT-486. |
