## INC-91 — Option-B snapshot-age widening (`[-1000, 15000]ms`) not applied to the sibling entry-/exit-price constructors; ACT-485 shipped Option B I5-only and skipped the class audit

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced by** | DRY run `9fe034be` (2026-07-07, 09:35 ET) — 3/20 targets refused at the entry-price fetch site (`overshoot.entry.price_refusal.polygon_snapshot_stale`) with snapshot ages **−109ms, −811ms, −887ms** — all small negative wall-clock skew, i.e. the exact ACT-484 pathology that Option B was ratified to absorb. The i5-recheck bucket (which received the widening) correctly surfaced only genuine feed lag (28.9s, 60.6s). |
| **Root cause** | ACT-485 shipped Option B by adding `OVERSHOOT_I5_SNAPSHOT_MIN_AGE_MS = -1_000` to `i5-recheck.ts` only, with an explicit scope-discipline note deferring the widening at the sibling sites "until a separate amendment ratifies the same widening there." The class audit that would have enumerated those sibling sites was NOT run. Two sibling sites carried the same `< 0` hardcoded lower bound: `entry-price-construction.ts:116` and `exit-price-construction.ts:128`. |
| **Class audit (this turn)** | `rg -n "snapshotAgeMs\s*[<>]" _shared/overshoot-execution supabase/functions/overshoot-*` enumerated three snapshot-age comparison sites: (1) `i5-recheck.ts:154-161` (already widened at ACT-485), (2) `entry-price-construction.ts:115-122` (defect), (3) `exit-price-construction.ts:127-134` (defect). No other overshoot handler compares a snapshot timestamp to a clock. |
| **Classification** | **Sibling-site drift / class-audit-miss.** Same pathology, same fix, three call sites — but the audit that would have found all three was skipped. Money-path safety envelope intact (typed refusal, no silent zeros), so behavior was "correctly refusing" — but the refusal cause was the fixable clock-skew class rather than a real market signal, exactly the framing INC-90 taught us to reject as "as designed." |
| **Money-path impact** | Zero (safety envelope worked). No orders. No positions. Opportunity cost: ~15% of first-light entries refused for clock skew until the widening extends. The exit-price site (`exit-price-construction.ts`) had not yet triggered in production because T+5 exits fire mid-session where the skew is negligible, but the class defect was identical. |
| **Fix (this-turn, ACT-486)** | Single-home the bounds in a new `_shared/overshoot-execution/snapshot-age-bounds.ts` exporting `OVERSHOOT_SNAPSHOT_MIN_AGE_MS = -1_000` and `OVERSHOOT_SNAPSHOT_MAX_AGE_MS = 15_000`. All three sibling sites import from the shared home and reference the shared constants in their predicates and reason strings. Historical constant names preserved via re-export for backwards-compat. Source-sentinel test (`snapshot-age-bounds_test.ts`) + CI grep gate (`overshoot-guards.yml`, `INC-91 gate`) forbid any numeric-literal snapshot-age bound at any sibling site. |
| **Standing invariant (new)** | Snapshot-age comparisons in `_shared/overshoot-execution/` MUST import bounds from `snapshot-age-bounds.ts`. Numeric-literal bounds at any sibling site are a class-drift risk and are forbidden by the INC-91 CI gate. |
| **Meta-lesson for future single-site fixes** | When Option B (or any bounded-widening) is ratified against a diagnosed pathology, the class audit MUST run in the SAME turn (grep for the predicate shape across the module tree; enumerate sibling sites; either widen all or explicitly justify per-site divergence). ACT-485's "scope-discipline" deferral of the sibling widening was the wrong call — the correct call is single-home + widen everywhere the pathology applies. |
| **Status** | **Resolved-structurally, pending behavioral proof.** Structural fix landed at ACT-486 (2026-07-07). Behavioral proof carried to tomorrow's 09:35 ET §9.2 DRY bracket — success criterion: `entry_price.polygon_snapshot_stale = 0` (or non-zero ONLY at ages > 15000ms or < -1000ms, both real staleness). INC-91 flips to **Resolved (structural + behavioral)** once that bracket lands. |
| **Fix references** | ACT-486 (this-turn corrective); ACT-485 (parent, closes into ACT-486); DRY run `9fe034be` (surfacing evidence); `_shared/overshoot-execution/snapshot-age-bounds.ts` (new single home); `_shared/overshoot-execution/entry-price-construction.ts:41-51/115-122`; `_shared/overshoot-execution/exit-price-construction.ts:37-52/127-134`; `_shared/overshoot-execution/i5-recheck.ts:54-73` (refactored to import from shared home); `_shared/overshoot-execution/snapshot-age-bounds_test.ts` (source-sentinel + import check); `.github/workflows/overshoot-guards.yml` (INC-91 CI gate step). |
| **Added by** | Lovable at operator direction, 2026-07-07 → INC-91 filed and Resolved-structurally at ACT-486. |

## INC-92 — Ratified capacity 36/4 wired into entry sizing (T3b) but NOT into detection-time selection; detector selection cap remains 20/side (v2 spec `capacity_per_side_default: 20`)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced by** | Post-bracket census after the 2026-07-07 §9.1b detection bracket (LIVE run `2b7fe0a4-a6b1-4d6e-8bd5-83d72d39dfac`, as_of 2026-07-07): selected_count=20 (all LONG/T2), refusal `capacity=165` — the capacity refusals are being computed against the detector's per-side selection cap of 20, not the ratified deployment capacity (LONG 36 / SHORT 4). Entry sizing at T3b consumes the ratified 36/4 correctly; the drift is upstream at the selection stage. |
| **Root cause** | Detector v2 predicate spec carries `capacity_per_side_default: 20` as a versioned constant; the ratified deployment capacity (LONG 36 / SHORT 4) was wired only into the entry-sizing path (T3b) and never propagated to the detection-time selection cap. Handler-param census across `overshoot-detection-run` + `_shared/overshoot/detector/` needed to confirm the exact wiring surface (predicate-spec constant vs handler param vs deployment config lookup). Same defect family as INC-87 (partial constant wiring — one arm of a shared bound updated, sibling arm missed). |
| **Impact (a) — LONG ROI clamp** | LONG book deploys ~55% of ratified allocation (20 of 36 slots filled; 16 slots idle daily). Money-path is correctly sized on the 20 selected — no over-risk — but ~44% of ratified LONG allocation goes undeployed each detection fire. ROI clamp, not a safety issue. |
| **Impact (b) — SHORT hazard (severity driver)** | Detection may select up to 20 SHORTs against an entry capacity of 4 at 2.5%/slot → potential **50% short notional vs the ratified 10% short sleeve**, i.e. a 5× over-risk if the selection cap fires against a live short book. **Currently masked** by SI (short-interest) staleness — 0 shorts selected in tonight's LIVE bracket, and the SHORT arm has been structurally 0 across recent brackets — so the hazard cannot trigger in the current data regime. Severity driver: the hazard is latent until SI freshness restores, at which point an un-fixed selection cap would immediately over-risk the SHORT sleeve. |
| **Classification** | Partial-constant-wiring drift (INC-87 class). Sizing arm updated, selection arm skipped. Latent SHORT hazard masked by unrelated SI staleness — the mask is not a safeguard. |
| **Money-path impact (tonight)** | Zero. LIVE bracket selected 20 LONG / 0 SHORT; the 20-name LONG book is correctly sized (2.5%/slot × 20 = 50% of the ratified 90% LONG sleeve — half-deployed, zero over-risk). SHORT hazard cannot trigger with 0 shorts selected. Tomorrow's 09:35 ET §9.2 bracket proceeds on the same 20-name book with the same envelope. |
| **Spec-semantics question (STEP A charter, deferred)** | Selection capacity arguably belongs in **deployment config OUTSIDE the versioned predicate spec** (a dial, not an edge definition — changing deployed capacity should not require a detector version event). Resolving this determines whether the fix is (i) a handler-param change reading from deployment config (no detector version bump), or (ii) a detector version event that lifts `capacity_per_side_default` and rebinds the predicate spec. STEP A charter to enumerate the wiring sites (handler params, `_shared/overshoot/detector/`, predicate-spec constants), classify each as spec-semantic vs deployment-dial, and pick the fix shape. |
| **Fix** | **NO fix tonight.** Tomorrow's 09:35 ET §9.2 bracket proceeds on the 20-name book (correctly sized, half-deployed, zero over-risk; SHORT hazard cannot trigger with 0 shorts selected). STEP A charter follows after tomorrow's bracket closes. |

## INC-94 — RLS deny-pattern class defect: RESTRICTIVE `FOR ALL USING(false)` on tables with permissive SELECT blanks all authenticated reads

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| **Surfaced by** | Operator console review during ACT-494 (2026-07-09). Universe page showed "no rows" against 839 live rows; Execution page showed "No entry-run rows yet" against 5 live rows in `overshoot_entry_runs`. The Universe symptom was ratified at ACT-494 as an instance; ACT-494a promoted it to a class sweep. |
| **Root cause (class)** | Postgres RLS `RESTRICTIVE` policies AND-combine across ALL commands, including `SELECT`. A `RESTRICTIVE FOR ALL USING(false)` policy — intended as a "deny writes" guard — actually denies SELECT too, unconditionally. Since restrictive policies AND with permissive ones, no permissive SELECT rule can override it. Result: any table carrying this pattern is read-blank to authenticated regardless of the `<strategy>.view` permission. |
| **Sweep (this-turn)** | `SELECT … FROM pg_policies WHERE permissive='RESTRICTIVE' AND cmd='ALL' AND qual='false'` returned **7 hits, all `public.overshoot_*`**: `overshoot_backfill_runs`, `overshoot_daily_bars`, `overshoot_earnings_calendar`, `overshoot_entry_runs`, `overshoot_study_candidate_events`, `overshoot_study_cell_results`, `overshoot_study_runs`. Longshort and platform tables clean. |
| **Fix (ACT-494a migration)** | Each hit: dropped `FOR ALL USING(false)`, replaced with three per-command `RESTRICTIVE` denials (`FOR INSERT WITH CHECK(false)`, `FOR UPDATE USING(false) WITH CHECK(false)`, `FOR DELETE USING(false)`). Write-denial preserved; SELECT unblocked (governed by existing permissive `<perm>.view` policies where present). |
| **Read-restoration status** | **Reads restored** on: `overshoot_backfill_runs`, `overshoot_daily_bars`, `overshoot_earnings_calendar`, `overshoot_entry_runs` (each carries a permissive `SELECT USING(has_permission(auth.uid(),'overshoot.view'))`). Post-fix pg_policies confirms per-command restrictive denials + permissive SELECT coexist correctly. Row-count probe as service_role: `overshoot_entry_runs = 5` (matches the "No entry-run rows yet" console symptom — behavioral confirmation on operator's next Execution-page load). |
| **Pending ratification (reads NOT opened)** | `overshoot_study_candidate_events`, `overshoot_study_cell_results`, `overshoot_study_runs` — no permissive SELECT policy exists for authenticated. The restrictive-all-false was removed to satisfy the class invariant, but reads remain closed by absence of any permissive grant. These are research artifacts; operator ratification required to decide whether `overshoot.view` (or a research-scoped perm) should read them. |
| **Equity-snapshot check (operator concern)** | `overshoot_equity_snapshots` was NOT on the hit list. Its `overshoot_equity_snapshots_no_direct_write` deny is `PERMISSIVE` (not restrictive) and therefore OR-combines: reads work via `overshoot_equity_snapshots_read_view_perm`. Snapshot-curve rendering is unblocked ahead of the first operator bracket. |
| **Standing invariant (new)** | No `public.*` table may carry a `RESTRICTIVE` policy with `cmd='ALL' AND qual='false'`. Write denials MUST be per-command (`INSERT`/`UPDATE`/`DELETE`) so `SELECT` remains solely governed by permissive policies. |
| **CI-shaped check (recorded, deferred as cheap follow-up)** | Add a `db-guards` step: `psql -c "SELECT count(*) FROM pg_policies WHERE schemaname='public' AND permissive='RESTRICTIVE' AND cmd='ALL' AND qual='false'"` and fail if `> 0`. Trivially cheap; queued as INC-94 CI gate. |
| **Meta-lesson** | The "deny writes" idiom `FOR ALL USING(false)` is a foot-gun in Postgres RLS. Correct shape is either three per-command restrictive denials, OR keeping the deny `PERMISSIVE` (permissive policies OR-combine, so a permissive `ALL USING(false)` does not block a sibling permissive `SELECT`). Prefer per-command restrictive for defense-in-depth. |
| **Status** | **Resolved (structural, class-wide).** Behavioral proof carries to operator's next authenticated console view of the Portfolio, Universe (already confirmed at ACT-494), and Execution pages — `overshoot_entry_runs = 5` rows expected to render post-deploy. |
| **Fix references** | ACT-494 (Universe instance); ACT-494a (this-turn class sweep + fix); migration `20260709_071737` (7-table policy rewrite); pg_policies before/after captured in ACT-494a evidence bundle. |
| **INC-number assignment rule (LOAD-BEARING, recorded here so the class of slip dies)** | Before assigning a new INC number, `grep -oE '^#{2,3} INC-[0-9]+' docs/06-tracking/incidental-findings.md \| sort -u` and pick the next free integer above the max. The initial draft of this entry was filed as INC-93 by counting `^## INC-` headers only and missing the `### INC-93` fill-sweep entry at file head (filed 2026-07-08, ACT-489). Corrected to INC-94 in the same turn. Both `##` and `###` INC headers count — grep MUST match `^#{2,3} INC-`. |
| **Added by** | Lovable at operator direction, 2026-07-09 → INC-94 filed and Resolved-structurally at ACT-494a. |

**Behavioral deltas (operator-confirmed on paste-back):**

- ACT-494b (2026-07-09): Execution page renders 5 `overshoot_entry_runs` rows and Universe page renders 839 rows on operator's authenticated console — reads restored end-to-end for both permissive-SELECT `overshoot_*` tables in the sweep. Portfolio broker-mirror, reconciliation primary/history, and empty-state patterns all confirmed compliant.
- ACT-494c (2026-07-09): Overview KPI-strip display defects (D1 tier snapshot arbitrary-run selection; D2 UTC-date shift; D3 kill-switch honest-empty) all fixed structurally.
- ACT-494d (2026-07-09): kill_switches read surfaced `permission denied for table kill_switches` on Overview. Diagnosis: RLS policies present (`kill_switches_read_scoped` for authenticated with longshort.view/overshoot.view/superadmin; `kill_switches_no_direct_write_policy` USING(false) restrictive) but table-level GRANTs entirely absent. §22.5.1 evidence — pg_class.relacl on `public.kill_switches`: **before** = `{postgres=arwdDxtm/postgres}` (authenticated + service_role absent); **after** = `{postgres=arwdDxtm/postgres, anon=Dxtm/postgres, authenticated=rDxtm/postgres, service_role=arwdDxtm/postgres}` — 'r' (SELECT) granted to authenticated; ALL granted to service_role; writes remain denied by absence of `awd` grants AND-combined with the restrictive USING(false) policy. Class-echo of INC-94: RLS ≠ GRANTs; both must be present for PostgREST reads.
- ACT-494e / INC-82 mini-bracket (2026-07-09): first `overshoot_equity_snapshots` row landed. Bracket: (1) fence `cron.job` overshoot rows = 0; (2) arm `job_registry.enabled=true` on id `overshoot_equity_snapshot` (note: operator ticket said `overshoot.equity_snapshot` with a dot — actual id is underscore, delta surfaced); (3) operator fired `overshoot-equity-snapshot` from DevTools, status=200, correlation_id `cf7f1b75-4199-4cfa-9c11-5b5a6f52735e`; (4) verify — row for `snapshot_date=2026-07-09`, `broker_equity=100129.27`, `cash=56024.743`, `long_market_value=44104.527`, `short_market_value=0`, `positions_priced=18/18`, matches response envelope byte-for-byte; (5) disarm `enabled=false`; (6) fence re-proof `cron.job` overshoot count = 0. Equity Curve tab now renders its first point.

## INC-95 — `overshoot-alerts-dispatcher` watchdog cron-overdue check floods pages overnight: (1) schedule-blind wall-clock threshold (`staleMs > 18h`), (2) idempotency key bucketed on wall clock (`nowMs / 30min`) instead of missed-slot identity

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| **Filed** | 2026-07-10 (Wave-1 unattended cycle-1 morning triage) |
| **Scope** | `supabase/functions/overshoot-alerts-dispatcher/index.ts` — `scanCronOverdue()` only. Read-only watchdog; NO money-path impact. |
| **Symptom** | 145 `overshoot_alert_dispatch` rows in 8h (03:40Z → 11:35Z Fri 2026-07-10) — 143 `cron_overdue` (26 dispatched + 119 skipped_idempotent) + 1 `daily_digest` dispatched + 1 `daily_digest` failed. Zero real engine-failure/reconciliation/kill-switch alerts. Every page against a job whose schedule did NOT expect a fire during the window. |
| **Defect class 1 — schedule-blind overdue** | `scanCronOverdue` compared wall-clock `nowMs - lastFireMs > 18h` regardless of the cron expression. This false-pages every schedule that has periods > 18h of expected silence: `* 13-21 * * 1-5` (fill_sweep — expects ZERO fires 21:00Z → 13:00Z next weekday, 16h; and full silence Fri-21:00Z → Mon-13:00Z, 64h), `0 22 * * 1-5` (detection — 24h between fires Mon-Thu, 72h across weekend), `10 21 * * 1-5` (equity_snapshot — same 24h/72h), `0 21 1,15 * *` (SI TRAP — up to 14 days between fires and must NEVER page meanwhile). Overnight window Thu 21:00Z → Fri 07:45Z is fully within the fill_sweep's expected-silence gap, but the watchdog paged the whole time. |
| **Defect class 2 — wall-clock idempotency key** | `source_row_id = \`${id}:${bucket}\`` with `bucket = Math.floor(nowMs / 30min)` cycles the dedup key every 30 min regardless of whether a new expected fire slot has been missed. Combined with defect 1, a persistent "job never fired" state re-pages every 30 min for the life of the outage. 143 pages in 8h ≈ 4 armed jobs (dispatcher itself + detection + fill_sweep + short_interest — equity_snapshot key `overshoot.equity_snapshot` was ALSO missing from the trigger→artifact map, so it silently escaped both false-pages AND real-overdue detection) × 16 half-hour buckets × dedupe ratio ≈ observed. |
| **Fix (this-turn structural)** | (a) New shared parser `supabase/functions/_shared/cron-schedule.ts` (Deno-only, dependency-free) — 5-field UTC cron expressions with `*`/literal/range/step/enum, Vixie DOM-DOW OR-vs-AND rule. Public API: `parseCron`, `lastExpectedFireAt(expr, at)`, `evaluateOverdue(expr, now, lastActualMs, toleranceMs)`. (b) `scanCronOverdue` rewired to `evaluateOverdue` — overdue iff `now > lastExpectedSlot + tol AND lastExpectedSlot > lastActual + tol`. (c) Dedup key changed to `\`${id}@${lastExpected.toISOString()}\`` — one alert per genuinely missed slot; a subsequent watchdog tick against the SAME slot insert-conflicts on the unique index and returns `skipped_idempotent`; a NEW slot missed → new key → new alert. (d) `equity_snapshot` map keys added under both dotted (`overshoot.equity_snapshot`) and underscore (`overshoot_equity_snapshot`) ids to reconcile the INC-82 registry-id drift noted at ACT-494e. (e) Body preview now surfaces `last_expected_slot`, `last_actual_fire`, `stale_hours`, and `dispatcher_version` for post-page triage. (f) Version echo `OVERSHOOT_ALERTS_DISPATCHER_VERSION = 'inc95-cron-aware-overdue-v1-20260710'` returned by GET and watchdog POST response envelopes per INC-84 §5 attestation standing rule. |
| **Tests (this-turn)** | `supabase/functions/_shared/cron-schedule_test.ts` — 12 Deno.test cases covering all five armed schedules: */5 rounding, fill_sweep window OUT vs IN, weekday-21:10Z pre-fire, detection over-weekend gap, SI TRAP (last-slot may be weeks back; must NOT page when it fired on time), and the two CLASS-1 regression fences (`last_actual=never` DOES page for a genuinely missed overnight slot with stable slot key across ticks; `last_actual > last_expected` does NOT page). |
| **What the false pages MASKED (load-bearing follow-up finding)** | This morning's checklist (A)(B)(C) exposed that the "false alarms" partially reflected a REAL fact the naive watchdog surfaced by accident: NONE of jobids 121-129 have fired since arming. `cron.job_run_details` shows only jobid 120 (`overshoot-alerts-dispatcher`, first fire 03:25Z Fri) has EVER produced a run row for `overshoot-*`; jobids 121-129 were installed AFTER their most recent expected slot Thu evening and have no fire scheduled again until later today (fill_sweep 13:00Z, exit 19:50Z, equity 21:10Z, detection 22:00Z; SI next 15 Jul). Unattended cycle-1 evidence therefore lands TONIGHT (Fri fires), not this morning. Not a code defect in engines — an arming-timing artifact. Entry-arm decision at ~09:20 ET is **NOT GREEN**: zero cycle-1 fires observed yet. Recommendation carried to ACT-497 Wave-1 closure: entry stays disarmed until Fri-night cycle produces GREEN detection + equity fires and A5 reconcile PASS on Sat morning. |
| **Behavioral proof (post-deploy, this-turn)** | Cited alongside the deploy: `curl … /overshoot-alerts-dispatcher` GET returns `version = 'inc95-cron-aware-overdue-v1-20260710'`; watchdog POST at Fri 11:45Z returns `summary.cron_overdue = 0` (predicate correctly rejects overnight silence for `* 13-21 * * 1-5`, `10 21 * * 1-5`, `0 22 * * 1-5`, `0 21 1,15 * *`; only pages if last_expected slot is > tolerance old AND last_actual < last_expected — which requires a genuine missed fire). Idempotency proof carried forward: next false-alarm-class regression fence is that a genuine missed slot pages EXACTLY ONCE per slot across successive ticks (asserted structurally in test file; behavioral fence lands the next time a real slot is genuinely missed). |
| **Cross-references** | ACT-497 Wave-1 arm (parent bracket); INC-82 (equity_snapshot registry-id underscore/dot drift referenced in fix (d)); INC-84 §5 (version-echo standing rule referenced in fix (f)); INC-94 (same-week alerts/RLS class close-out, adjacent scope); `_shared/cron-schedule.ts` + `_shared/cron-schedule_test.ts` (this-turn additions); `src/features/longshort/utils/cron-staleness.ts` (front-end sibling — pre-existing; edge helper mirrors semantics but is dependency-free). |
| **Status** | **Resolved (structural + behavioral).** Follow-up work carried: (i) Fri-night cycle-1 evidence collection (unattended); (ii) ACT-497 Wave-1 closure disposition of the entry-arm decision when cycle-1 lands; (iii) OPTIONAL harden: shared cron parser could be pulled by `job-alert-evaluation` if it grows a similar overdue check — deferred until requested. |
| **Added by** | Lovable at operator direction, 2026-07-10 → INC-95 filed and Resolved same-turn. |

### INC-95 refinement (2026-07-10, backlog — not this-turn) — suppress overdue alerts for slots that predate the cron row's installation time

**Finding.** The INC-95 CLASS-1 fix correctly pages when `last_expected_slot > last_actual + tol AND now > last_expected_slot + tol`, but treats `last_actual = 0` (job never fired) as if the entire history of expected slots were missed. The Thu 2026-07-09T21:59Z `overshoot-fill-sweep` "missed slot" flagged by the fence test is arithmetically true (last expected slot in the `* 13-21 * * 1-5` window, no run row) yet **operationally void**: the `cron.job` row for jobid 125 was created AFTER 21:59Z Thu (ACT-497 Wave-1 installed the overshoot cron rows Thu evening). No fire was ever scheduleable for that slot — pgcron cannot fire slots that predate its own row. Paging on it is a category error and would recur every time a new cron is armed mid-schedule.

**Fix shape (backlog, not applied).** In `scanCronOverdue` (and `evaluateOverdue` if promoted upstream), read `cron.job.jobid → created_at` (or the earliest observable proxy: `min(cron.job_run_details.start_time)` for the jobid, floored to the cron row's install time when a fresh `cron.schema_migrations`-style timestamp is exposed; simplest path is a `SELECT jobid, jobname FROM cron.job` join enriched with an install-time column we add via a lightweight audit table on `cron.schedule`/`cron.unschedule` calls). Then: `effectiveLastActualMs = max(lastActualMs, cronRowInstalledAtMs)`. Overdue predicate re-uses the existing arithmetic with the effective floor — slots strictly before install time can never be "missed."

**Why not this-turn.** (a) pg_cron does not expose `cron.job.created_at` natively — need either a wrapper migration (write-side capture on schedule/unschedule) or a heuristic (first `cron.job_run_details.start_time` as a proxy, which fails exactly in the "never fired" case that motivates the fix). Both are non-trivial and warrant a dedicated bracket. (b) Zero operational impact today: the only cron rows this touches (jobids 121-129, armed Thu evening) will each accumulate a real `last_actual` on their next fire, and from then on the existing CLASS-1 fix is sufficient. Refinement only matters at the next cron-arming event.

**Standing invariant (recorded, not enforced).** New pg_cron rows SHOULD carry an install-time metadata row (planned wrapper: `job_registry.cron_installed_at` populated by the same migration that runs `cron.schedule`). Once populated, the watchdog can floor `lastActual` at `cron_installed_at` and this class of "true-by-numbers, void-operationally" page dies.

**Cross-references.** INC-95 parent above; `supabase/functions/_shared/cron-schedule_test.ts` — the CLASS-1 regression fence test that legitimately asserts the 21:59Z Thu slot as "overdue" against `last_actual=0`; this refinement would ADD a second dimension (`cronInstalledAtMs`) to that fence rather than remove the existing assertion. Carried on the ACT-497 Wave-2 closure follow-up list — no code changes this turn.

**Added by** Lovable at operator direction, 2026-07-10 → filed as backlog refinement; no fix applied.

## INC-96 — CRITICAL Tier-A: NO AGGREGATE ALLOCATION CAP in overshoot entry engine (per-slot sizing constants not aggregate-enforced; Alpaca BP gate cannot substitute) — WAVE-2 BLOCKER

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| **Filed** | 2026-07-10 (ACT-497 Wave-1 cycle-1 LIVE manual bracket, response-envelope Tier-A review) |
| **Surfaced by** | LIVE manual entry bracket run_id `1c705ad2-61c0-4207-a658-b37ad5e61026`, correlation_id `896701de-dd7b-48ba-920d-cbe7dd43ddc0`, session_date `2026-07-10`, detection_run_id `6caaee6c-…` (36-target ratified book). Operator-flagged Tier-A on the paste-back. |
| **Class** | Same defect family as INC-87 / INC-92 — ratified constants wired at the **per-slot** decision point but **NOT** re-enforced against an aggregate projection over `(current_book_MV + this_run_accepted_notional + this_order_notional)`. |
| **Evidence (numeric, from the LIVE response envelope)** | `sizingBase` = **$99,472.48** (≈ $99.5K equity). Ratified LONG sleeve cap = `OVERSHOOT_ALLOCATION_LONG × margin_multiplier × equity` = `0.90 × 1.00 × 99.5K` ≈ **$89.5K**. Pre-fire held LONG lots ≈ **$77.4K** (32 names, `position_already_open=9` from the 36-book overlap). Headroom to ratified cap ≈ **$12.1K** — ≈ **4–5 slots** at the book's median notional. **Observed:** `orders_submitted = 18`, submitted-notional ≈ **$44.7K**, `buying_power_refusals = 0`, `sizing_refusals = 0`, `regime_throttled_t2 = 0`. **Projected post-fill LONG MV ≈ $122K on $99.5K equity → ~1.22× gross, ~$22.7K of simulated RegT margin** vs the ratified 1.00× cash-line target. Overshoot ≈ **$32.5K over cap**, ≈ **36% over the intended sleeve**. |
| **Why the BP gate did NOT catch it** | Alpaca `buying_power` on the paper account reflects **RegT 2× intraday margin**, not the cash line and not `margin_multiplier=1.00`. The entry-loop BP check compares projected notional against `buying_power` and will happily accept LONG orders up to ≈ 2× equity before refusing. `margin_multiplier=1.00` is a **ratified soft cap with no software enforcement point** — the loop reads it for per-slot sizing math (share-count denominator) but never re-computes an aggregate. `strategy_allocation_pct=1.0` for this account means the sleeve **is** the equity line — there is no headroom above the sleeve for the shortfall to hide in. |
| **Fix shape (WAVE-2 BLOCKER — Monday autonomous entry MUST NOT arm without this)** | In `overshoot-entry-run/index.ts`, insert a **pre-submit aggregate projection** per side, inside the ranked-order iteration and BEFORE broker submission. Compute `projected_MV_side = current_open_lots_MV_side + already_accepted_this_run_notional_side + this_order_notional`; refuse the order with a new **typed reason `allocation_cap_reached`** iff `projected_MV_side > OVERSHOOT_ALLOCATION_<SIDE> × margin_multiplier × equity`. Mirror for the SHORT sleeve with `OVERSHOOT_ALLOCATION_SHORT`. **Rank order preserved** — iteration is already in detector-rank order, so the best names claim the remaining headroom and the tail truncates cleanly. Add `allocation_cap_reached` counter to the response envelope's `refusals` block alongside the existing typed reasons. |
| **Tests (mandatory pre-deploy; all five must be green)** | (T1) **today-shape scenario**: 32 held LONG names + 36-target book + `equity=$99.5K` + `alloc_long=0.90` + `margin_mult=1.00`, headroom ≈ $12K → assert only ~4–5 orders submit, remainder carry `allocation_cap_reached`, `buying_power_refusals=0`. (T2) **zero-headroom**: held MV ≥ cap → 0 submissions, all 36 refused with `allocation_cap_reached`. (T3) **empty book**: no prior lots, full cap available → loop truncates at cap (not at 36) — bounds the upper edge. (T4) **SHORT mirror**: same three shapes for the SHORT sleeve with `OVERSHOOT_ALLOCATION_SHORT × margin_multiplier × equity`. (T5) **rank-preservation**: first-N-by-detector-rank submit, tail refused — no reordering induced by the cap. |
| **Standing invariant (new — added to trading-panel design contract)** | Every ratified allocation/sizing constant in the overshoot engine MUST have BOTH a per-slot decision point AND an aggregate enforcement point over `(current_book_MV + this_run_accepted_notional + this_order_notional)`. Per-slot alone is a **known-false gate** whenever the book is deep. Pinned to the class INC-87 / INC-92 / INC-96 as the recurring family. |
| **Operator ruling requested (STATE — do not decide)** | **(a)** Does Alpaca **paper** charge simulated margin interest on the ~$22.7K over-cap balance until reconciled — i.e., is today's overshoot P&L-visible or purely a leverage-window record? **(b)** Projected gross leverage once today's 18 fill: LONG MV ≈ **$122K** / equity **$99.5K** ≈ **1.22×**; margin borrowed ≈ **$22.7K**. **(c)** Trim-vs-carry options: **Option A** — trim ~$22.7K of tail names now (rank-preserving reverse trim) to restore 1.00×; **Option B** — carry the overshoot into tomorrow and let the exit engine's natural exits normalize the book; **Option C** — hybrid: trim only the single highest-notional overshoot name. **W5 measurement note (LOAD-BEARING regardless of option chosen):** the leverage window (T_fill → T_normalize) MUST be annotated in the ACT-497 W5 evidence bundle. Any Sharpe / drawdown / hit-rate math derived from a period containing this window is measured on a **1.22× book**, not the ratified 1.00× book, and MUST carry the leverage-annotation footnote. |
| **Cycle-1 evidence still pending (unaffected — the sweep leg is independent of this INC)** | `overshoot.fill_sweep` cron (`* 13-21 * * 1-5`) is armed and owns adoption of today's 18 broker orders into `overshoot_lots`. Expected outcome: existing lot count grows by up to 18 as fills land → **A5 = 50 = 50** if pre-fire book was 32 held LONG lots + 18 new fills. Watchdog will page if `unadopted_fills > 0` past the next tick. Post-adoption count to be reported. |
| **Wave-2 gate (revised — supersedes prior "Monday ~09:20 GREEN → arm" plan)** | Monday ~09:20 ET autonomous entry arm is **BLOCKED** until: (a) `allocation_cap_reached` refusal shipped + all 5 tests green + redeploy + version echo bump (`OVERSHOOT_ENTRY_RUN_VERSION = 'inc96-aggregate-cap-v1-<date>'` per INC-84 §5 standing rule); (b) Saturday checklist adds **item (D) "allocation-cap fix verified"** — dry-run probe against a synthetic-book scenario asserting non-zero `allocation_cap_reached` count. GREEN across A/B/C/D → arm Monday. Any red → entry stays disarmed and the fallback manual bracket is repeated with the aggregate cap now armed in-code. |
| **Cross-references** | INC-87 (per-slot sizing not aggregated — parent class); INC-92 (ratified constant wired per-decision but not per-run); DEC-072 (ratified allocation constants provenance); ACT-490 (per-side capacity structural cap — orthogonal, does NOT substitute for allocation cap); ACT-497 Wave-1 (this INC surfaces here); handler source `supabase/functions/overshoot-entry-run/index.ts` (aggregate check to be inserted between per-slot sizing and broker submission). |
| **Status** | **OPEN — Tier-A blocker.** Fix + tests + redeploy + version-echo pending; operator ruling pending on trim-vs-carry disposition for today's live overshoot; Saturday checklist item (D) added. Entry registry stays disarmed; sweep / equity_snapshot / detection continue autonomous. |
| **Added by** | Lovable at operator direction, 2026-07-10 → INC-96 filed post-LIVE-bracket; next free number grep-verified across `docs/`, `scripts/`, `supabase/`, `src/` (max prior = INC-95). |

## INC-97 — unattended cycle-1 failed: fill-sweep cron rejected every tick, checklist reused stale submit-time broker state, and watchdog had no independent broker-ledger truth

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| **Filed** | 2026-07-10, operator verdict override; next-free grep verified across both `##` and `###` INC headers (max prior INC-96). |
| **Ground truth / stale-data recurrence** | Operator screenshots showed Alpaca broker=50, internal=32, broker-orphan=18 and cash approximately -$19,336 while the checklist asserted the 18 orders were still working. That assertion recycled the submit-time order snapshot instead of re-reading broker truth at checklist time: a recurrence of INC-93's meta-pattern, "asserted contracts not pinned against live artifacts." Future cycle verdicts MUST read the broker at verdict time; prior response envelopes are historical evidence, never current state. |
| **Cron mechanism — proven from `net._http_response`** | 105 consecutive in-window responses from 13:30:01Z through 15:13:00Z were HTTP 401 with `Invalid or expired token`. `sql/34` correctly sent the anon Authorization header plus `X-Cron-Secret` and a body containing only wall-clock `time`; body/as-of fallback was not reached. Source branch at pre-fix `overshoot-fill-sweep/index.ts:130-131` unconditionally called `authenticateRequest()` + RBAC, so the anon cron JWT failed before request parsing. `cron.job_run_details='succeeded'` proved only the SQL/net enqueue, not handler success. |
| **Parity restoration evidence** | The cron-auth repair deployed while the registry remained armed. The first post-deploy autonomous tick at 15:14:00Z adopted all 18 fills using broker average prices, `fetch_errors=0`, and returned A5 `broker_count=50, ledger_count=50, symmetric_diff=[], ok=true`; correlation `4e33a56a-b4cb-4392-b125-04da29ae5fff`. DB verification: 50 open lots and 18 `overshoot.lot.opened` rows stamped 15:14:01-02Z. A DRY preview was attempted first but could not authenticate before the fix; the scheduled LIVE tick pre-empted a second dry invocation. This sequencing deviation is recorded rather than rewritten. |
| **Alerting blind spot — proven** | The dispatcher mapped `overshoot.fill_sweep` overdue evidence to `overshoot_entry_runs.created_at`, so a fresh entry row falsely made the dead sweep look alive. Its shortfall scanner selected nonexistent `submitted_count`/`adopted_count` columns from `overshoot_entry_runs` and ignored the query error. Its A5 scanner only read `overshoot_reconciliation_state`; broker-vs-ledger A5 was computed by the sweep itself. Therefore a sweep stopped at auth was a single point of failure: no sweep audit, no A5 artifact, and no page. The observed 234+ dispatches were INFO/cron-overdue noise, not broker-ledger protection. |
| **Fixes** | (1) Sweep authenticates `X-Cron-Secret` before the manual JWT/RBAC branch and attributes cron audit rows to the canonical system operator. (2) Every live sweep writes `overshoot.fill_sweep.tick`; cron overdue maps to that action, never entry runs. (3) Dispatcher independently fetches Alpaca positions and open ledger lots every watchdog tick and pages CRITICAL on symmetric diff, so a dead sweep cannot silence its own detector. (4) Fill-sweep audit scan reads real `discovery_shortfall` / `a5_divergence` audit rows and no longer references nonexistent run counters. |
| **Tests / deploy attestation** | 21 focused Deno tests green (18 sweep + 4 dispatcher after final scanner fence; suites overlap count reported by runner). Deployed echoes: sweep `inc97-cycle1-v2-20260710`; dispatcher GET `inc97-independent-a5-v1-20260710`. |
| **Revised autonomous-entry gate** | Monday entry remains MANUAL. Monday must produce one full-day proof where the cron sweep autonomously adopts that day's fills and a controlled synthetic or real broker-ledger divergence produces a CRITICAL dispatch. Only that evidence permits autonomous entry to arm Tuesday. Today's repaired 50=50 parity does not satisfy the new full-day gate. |
| **Status** | Structural fixes deployed; parity restored. Behavioral gate remains OPEN through Monday's autonomous sweep adoption + page proof. |

### INC-97 Evidence Pack (Tuesday-gate consumable, filed 2026-07-10)

| Item | Content |
| --- | --- |
| **(1) Root cause — verbatim** | Hypothesis confirmed: **"cron auth branch rejects the anon bearer before X-Cron-Secret is inspected."** Evidence: `net._http_response` rows for `overshoot-fill-sweep` from 13:30:01Z through 15:13:00Z = **105 consecutive HTTP 401** with body `{"error":"Invalid or expired token"}`. Source citation: pre-fix `supabase/functions/overshoot-fill-sweep/index.ts:130-131` called `authenticateRequest(req)` unconditionally, which passed the anon cron JWT to `supabase.auth.getUser(token)` — that call returns `AuthError('Invalid or expired token')` for the anon key, because it is not a user session token. The `X-Cron-Secret` header was present on every request but never read: the handler returned 401 before body parse, before discovery-window construction, before any Supabase write. Alternative hypotheses ruled out: `sql/34` cron command was byte-correct (anon Authorization + `X-Cron-Secret` + JSON body with `time`); pg_net enqueue succeeded on every tick (`cron.job_run_details.status='succeeded'`); the discovery window (`sessionDate − 14d` … `sessionDate + 2d`) covers today's fills — none of these paths were reached. |
| **(1a) Timeline-anomaly resolution** | Fills existed at broker by ~14:44Z; checklist read 0 adopted at 14:40Z (consistent — sweep had been 401ing for 74 minutes by then); adoption at 15:14:00Z was **because the cron-auth fix deployed between 14:40Z and 15:14Z and the registry stayed armed across the deploy**. Deploy-time attestation: last pre-fix 401 in `net._http_response` = 15:13:00Z; first post-deploy 200 = the 15:14:00Z tick with correlation `4e33a56a-b4cb-4392-b125-04da29ae5fff` and version echo `inc97-cycle1-v2-20260710`. No other change is in the interval (registry `enabled` unchanged; `sql/34` unchanged; no broker-side event). The 15:14Z success is single-cause attributable to the deploy. |
| **(2) "Build unsuccessful" note** | One transient build-unsuccessful event appeared during the INC-97 patch stream while sweep tests iterated on the discovery-shortfall pause-suppression assertion. The failing artifact was the **sweep function's Deno test bundle**, not the runtime handler. It was retried after fixing the assertion, went green, and the redeploy that followed is the artifact the 21/21 tests ran against. Version echoes on the deployed artifact — sweep `OVERSHOOT_FILL_SWEEP_VERSION = 'inc97-cycle1-v2-20260710'` (from `supabase/functions/overshoot-fill-sweep/pure.ts`); dispatcher `OVERSHOOT_ALERTS_DISPATCHER_VERSION = 'inc97-independent-a5-v1-20260710'` (from `supabase/functions/overshoot-alerts-dispatcher/index.ts`). Both echoes are stamped into responses and visible in the 15:14Z tick body and the dispatcher GET. No stale intermediate artifact reached production. |
| **(3) INC-97 findings + fixes (registered)** | **F1 — cron silent failure mechanism.** Sweep handler authenticated cron bearer as a user before checking `X-Cron-Secret`; 105 ticks 401ed while `cron.job_run_details` said `succeeded`. *Fix:* `overshoot-fill-sweep/index.ts` now branches on `X-Cron-Secret` presence and calls `verifyCronSecret` before the user-JWT path; attributes cron audit rows to the canonical CRON_OPERATOR_ID. **F2 — checklist stale-data recurrence (INC-93 meta-pattern).** Cycle-1 checklist reused the submit-time order snapshot ("18 still working") instead of re-reading broker truth at verdict time. *Fix:* documented invariant in ACT-497 addendum — verdicts read broker at verdict time; prior envelopes are historical, never current. **F3 — watchdog blind spot.** Dispatcher mapped fill_sweep overdue evidence to `overshoot_entry_runs.created_at` (a fresh entry row masked a dead sweep) and its shortfall scanner selected nonexistent columns from `overshoot_entry_runs`. *Fix:* new `overshoot.fill_sweep.tick` audit action written every live sweep; dispatcher overdue mapping repointed to that action; dispatcher owns an independent Alpaca-vs-ledger A5 scan (`scanBrokerLedgerDivergence`) that pages CRITICAL on symmetric diff; audit-scan reads real `discovery_shortfall`/`a5_divergence` rows instead of nonexistent run counters. |
| **(4) Watchdog CRITICAL-page proof plan (Tuesday gate)** | **Method:** **synthetic divergence via a temporary ledger-side ghost row in a sandbox scope — NEVER a real lot mutation and NEVER a broker-side action.** Exact test: (i) with entry disarmed and sweep armed, insert a single synthetic open `overshoot_lots` row for a ticker not held at broker (`source_order_id='INC97-WATCHDOG-PROOF-<ts>'`, `qty=1`, `side='long'`, marked with a `metadata.synthetic=true` flag); (ii) wait one dispatcher tick (`*/5 * * * *`); (iii) confirm one `overshoot_alert_dispatch` row with `trigger_kind='a5_broker_ledger_divergence'`, severity CRITICAL, and a Resend delivery ID in the operator inbox; (iv) delete the synthetic row; (v) confirm the next dispatcher tick returns to `symmetric_diff=[]`. **Safety rationale:** synthetic row is ledger-only, uniquely tagged, not visible to the entry or exit engines (entry disarmed; exit reads `entry_ts`-anchored lots — the ghost `entry_ts` is set to `now()` so it is outside any exit horizon); no broker order is ever placed; teardown is a single DELETE by `source_order_id`. **When it runs:** Monday post-close (after autonomous sweep evidence has been collected for the day), operator-driven, single invocation, evidence bundle attached to the Tuesday-arm decision. Fallback: if a real broker-vs-ledger divergence occurs organically Monday and pages, that satisfies the proof and the synthetic test is skipped. |
| **(5) Monday plan (restated)** | Manual: operator drives the entry bracket at ~09:20 ET using the two-token DRY→LIVE flow (aggregate-cap gate INC-96 now active). Autonomous: `overshoot-equity-snapshot` (21:10Z), `overshoot-detection-run` (22:00Z), `overshoot-fill-sweep` (60s ticks in-window), `overshoot-alerts-dispatcher` (5-min ticks). **Monday must produce for Tuesday's arm:** (a) same-day autonomous adoption — one or more `overshoot.fill_sweep.tick` audit rows adopting Monday's fills into `overshoot_lots` without operator intervention, with A5 parity `broker=ledger` at close; (b) the CRITICAL divergence page from the (4) proof, with Resend delivery ID; (c) a clean Saturday-style checklist including item (D) allocation-cap probe echoing `inc96-aggregate-cap-v1-20260710` with `allocation_cap_reached > 0` on the synthetic 36-book. All three → autonomous entry arms Tuesday ~09:20 ET. Any one red → entry stays disarmed and Tuesday repeats the manual bracket. |
| **(6) Arming-protocol amendment — VERIFIED-TEST-FIRE (binding, forward + retroactive)** | Filed to `docs/04-modules/overshoot/overshoot.md` §13. Rule: ARMING A CRON LEG REQUIRES ONE VERIFIED TEST-FIRE THROUGH THE ACTUAL HTTP PATH — after cron.job row install + registry `enabled=true`, and before the leg is declared armed, either verify the first natural tick's `net._http_response` (200 + expected envelope) or trigger one authenticated synthetic tick. Registry + cron.job-row evidence alone is necessary-not-sufficient. Wave-1 declared five legs armed on registry evidence alone; INC-97's 105-tick 401 storm masked by `cron.job_run_details.status='succeeded'` proves the pattern insufficient. **Retroactive Monday checklist item (E):** paste one `net._http_response` 200 + valid envelope per armed leg — (E1) sweep = 15:14:00Z tick (correlation `4e33a56a-b4cb-4392-b125-04da29ae5fff`, echo `inc97-cycle1-v2-20260710`); (E2) dispatcher = any in-window `overshoot_alert_dispatch` row with `inc97-independent-a5-v1-20260710` echo; (E3) equity-snapshot = Monday 21:10Z first natural tick; (E4) detection = Monday 22:00Z first natural tick; (E5) SI compute = 2026-07-15 21:00Z (weekly); leg is DISARMED-EQUIVALENT until proven. Any leg missing (E) evidence at the Tuesday-arm decision does NOT count toward the autonomous-entry gate. |
